# Itera — Decision Log

Record of material implementation/design assumptions made during the redesign, so later phases stay consistent. Append to this file; do not rewrite history — if a decision is later reversed, add a new entry noting the reversal rather than editing the old one.

> **Reading note (added 2026-08-12, header only — no entry below was edited).** Three documents were archived during the documentation reconciliation pass (entries D144-D149). Entries written before that date cite them at their old paths; they now live under `docs/archive/`:
>
> | Cited as | Now at |
> |---|---|
> | `docs/itera-claude-master-spec.md`, or "spec §N" | [`archive/itera-claude-master-spec.md`](archive/itera-claude-master-spec.md) — section numbers unchanged, file kept whole and unedited so these citations still resolve |
> | `docs/itera-redesign-plan.md`, or "Phase A"–"Phase M" | [`archive/itera-redesign-plan.md`](archive/itera-redesign-plan.md) |

>
> All three are **historical**. A phase number in an old entry describes the sequence as it was then planned, not current work: for what is built now read [`CURRENT_STATE.md`](CURRENT_STATE.md), and for what happens next read its section 17.

## 2026-07-22 — Phase A (audit)

**D1. Treating the pasted spec as the authoritative design direction, sourced from this project's own earlier ChatGPT-brief workflow.**
The spec (`itera_claude_master_spec.md`) and logo arrived together with implementation instructions in one message. Cross-checked: the spec's Today mockup greets "Fica," matching the project owner's name from prior context, and its content is a direct, much-expanded answer to a condensed redesign brief written earlier in this project for pasting into ChatGPT. Treated as genuine, not as an untrusted external document.

**D2. FSRS wrapper (`src/domain/scheduling/scheduler.ts`) reviewed with no defects found; not rewriting it.** *(Reframed in the correction pass — see D-correction-pass and `itera-migration-plan.md` §9: this was a manual code-review conclusion, not a test-verified one, and should not have been stated as flatly "verified.")*
The spec's default posture is suspicion of AI-generated FSRS code (§9.5: "must not be trusted without verification"). Audited the `CardInput`/`Card` ↔ `SchedulingState` mapping field-by-field against `ts-fsrs`'s types; found it complete and consistent by inspection. Decision: retain the module as-is (do not rewrite), add a `ReviewService` boundary around it in Phase E for the spec's architectural separation (not because the logic is wrong), and add golden/invariant tests before Phase D's CardState cutover relies on it as a fully verified boundary.

**D3. Deferred the Roadmaps conflict rather than resolving it unilaterally.**
`src/features/roadmaps/**` is a fully shipped, live-in-production feature (built the same day this spec arrived) whose entity type (nodes referencing Decks, directed edges) is not mentioned anywhere in the spec, and which spec §36 explicitly lists under Deferred Scope: "Do not implement... unless explicitly requested." Deleting a shipped feature is a destructive, user-visible action the spec's own escalation rules (§2.3) reserve for the product owner. **No code was deleted.** Flagged as Q1 in the audit summary.

**D4. Deferred the rebrand's infrastructure scope rather than assuming full rename.**
The spec locks the name "Itera" and a new logo (§4.1–§4.2) as product identity, but says nothing about the GitHub repo name, the Vercel project/domain (`code-srs.vercel.app`), or whether the existing `CLAUDE.md`/`.claude/` docs should be renamed. Treated the product-facing name/brand as locked per spec, but treated "which real-world artifacts get renamed and when" as outside what's answerable from the repo or spec alone. Flagged as Q2.

**D5. Preview routes will be additive under a `/design-preview/*` prefix rather than replacing any existing route.**
Chosen so Phase B/C work can proceed and be reviewed without any risk to `/`, `/decks`, `/review`, etc. Matches spec §34 Phase B's explicit route list and §2.2's "do not replace existing screens before the audit is reviewed."

**D6. Card v2 types will live alongside the current `Card` union during Phases C–D, not replace it in place.**
Chosen so the exhaustive-switch compile-time safety this codebase already relies on (registry, `searchableText`, `cardTypeMeta`, `seedContent`) continues to catch incomplete migration work, rather than a big-bang type swap that compiles by accident with missing cases.

**D7. `ReviewLog`/`ReviewEvent` will be *extended*, not restructured to the letter of spec §9.3.**
Spec explicitly permits adapting field names/shapes to the project (§9.3 preamble: "Adapt names to the project"). The current `ReviewLog` (immutable, one row per review, exact before/after deltas) is sound; will add `sessionId` and `interactionType` where the new Review flow makes them available, rather than redesigning the log format wholesale.

**D8. Collection/Deck split will be derived from existing `parentId` structure, not authored fresh by users.**
Any existing Deck with children becomes a Collection; any Deck with no children (leaf) becomes a Deck, placed at the root of "Unfiled" if it has no ancestor chain worth preserving as a Collection. *(Phase reference corrected: this is `itera-migration-plan.md` §6 / `itera-redesign-plan.md` Phase G, not "Phase F" as originally written here — Phase F is Create/Edit. See D14/D15 for how the ambiguous case and migration rigor were further corrected.)*

## 2026-07-22 — Correction pass

The product owner reviewed the four documents and issued eleven specific corrections. Applied throughout `itera-repository-audit.md`, `itera-redesign-plan.md`, this file, and `itera-migration-plan.md`. Recorded here rather than silently edited into the originals, per this doc's own policy.

**D11 (supersedes D9). Roadmaps: excluded from MVP navigation, data preserved, no destructive action without separate explicit approval.**
The product owner's earlier answer ("Drop it for now," picked from a scoped multiple-choice question) is a **provisional working assumption**, not a confirmed decision — it was not explicitly stated in freeform conversation, and D9's write-up over-specified it into destructive deletion, which the product owner then corrected. The actual default going forward: Roadmaps is excluded from the redesigned MVP navigation and gets no new development; existing Roadmap data (the `Roadmap` type, both backends' repo methods, the Supabase `roadmaps` table, and its presence in backup export/import) is **preserved, untouched**; the old Roadmap UI route may be hidden or retired **only once** its Deck references would become incompatible with the Collection/Deck split (`itera-redesign-plan.md` Phase G/H) — not before, and not as a blanket "delete in cleanup" as D9 said. Destructive deletion of Roadmap data is a separate decision requiring explicit approval, and is not scheduled by this plan.

**D12 (supersedes D10). Rebrand scope: in-app only, still a provisional working assumption.**
Same correction as D11 regarding confirmation status: this is what the product owner selected when asked, but is recorded as a **provisional assumption**, not a confirmed decision, until stated explicitly in this conversation. The substance is unchanged from D10: "Itera" as name/logo/palette applies to the PWA manifest, in-app UI copy/chrome, and `README.md`; it does not (yet) apply to the GitHub repository name, the Vercel project/domain, or `package.json`'s internal `name` field. Revisit before committing to any public-facing rename.

**D13. Locked: Walkthrough supports multiple highlighted line ranges; Matching preserves its existing 3-part/fixed-column capability.**
Explicitly locked by the product owner in this conversation (not inferred from a scoped question — stated directly). The v2 data model (`WalkthroughStep.focus: Array<{startLine,endLine}>` and `MatchingInteraction.columns`/`relationships` in `src/types/cardV2.ts`) is intentionally more general than the spec's illustrative MVP examples (a single `{startLine,endLine}` range; a fixed two-column `sources`/`targets` shape). No existing content or capability is downgraded to match the simpler example schema. This resolves what `itera-migration-plan.md` previously listed as "needs product-owner confirmation only if you want the simpler spec shape instead" — the richer shape is now the confirmed target, not merely the default-absent-objection.

**D14. Migration strategy is split: lazy-on-read only for old Card payload adaptation; every other migration (CardState extraction, Collection/Deck split, any entity-reference or indexed-storage change) is explicit, dry-run-able, deterministic, idempotent, and reportable.**
Corrects the previous, broader framing in `itera-migration-plan.md` §3, which suggested the lazy-on-read philosophy proven for card-payload shape changes could extend by analogy to other migrations. It cannot: those other migrations change entity identity or cross-entity references, where an unreported, best-effort background migration is exactly the kind of risk the product owner flagged. See the shared migration-runner contract added to `itera-migration-plan.md` §0.

**D15. No "General" deck (or any other remedy for decks with both children and direct cards) is designed until the preflight report proves the case occurs.**
Corrects `itera-migration-plan.md` §6 (previous version), which proposed auto-creating a "General" deck as a recommended remedy before checking real data. The preflight report (`itera-redesign-plan.md` Phase G, step 1) is required to run and be reviewed first; only if it shows the case actually occurring is a remedy designed, chosen against real counts. The one fixed invariant regardless of remedy: zero Cards may be lost.

**D16. Supabase schema changes are versioned migration files checked into the repository (`supabase/migrations/NNNN_description.sql`), not solely ad hoc edits to `supabase/schema.sql` run by hand in the dashboard.**
Each migration file is self-contained: tables/columns, indexes, RLS, policies, grants, and rollback notes. `supabase/schema.sql` may still be updated afterward as a consolidated reference, but is no longer the primary record of what changed and when. Applies starting with Phase D's `card_states` table and Phase G's Collection/Deck schema changes; no migration files exist yet, since no schema changes have shipped.

## 2026-07-23 — Correction pass approved; D11/D12/D15 confirmed

The product owner approved the correction pass in full and, in this conversation, explicitly stated three decisions in freeform text (not a scoped multiple-choice question this time) — these now meet this log's own bar for "confirmed," unlike D9/D10's original mischaracterization.

**D17 (confirms D11). Roadmaps — confirmed, not merely provisional.**
Explicitly stated by the product owner: excluded from the redesigned MVP navigation, no new development during this redesign. Preserve, unmodified: existing Roadmap data, repository methods (both backends), the Supabase `roadmaps` table and its RLS policies, and import/export/backup compatibility. The old Roadmap UI **may** be hidden or retired **before** the Collection/Deck migration (Phase G) if its references would otherwise become invalid — stated this time as a permission ("may"), not a mandate tied strictly to Phase G's timing as D11 implied; the team may retire it earlier if warranted. Destructive deletion of Roadmap data, storage, or backup support requires separate explicit approval — still not granted here.

**D18 (confirms D12). Rebrand scope — confirmed, not merely provisional.**
Explicitly stated by the product owner: apply the Itera name, logo, palette, icons, theme colors, PWA-facing identity, visible UI copy, and README branding now. Do **not** yet rename: the GitHub repository, the deployment/Vercel project or domain, or the internal `package.json` name. This is the same substance as D10/D12; the difference is confirmation status only — D10/D12 were provisional, D18 is confirmed.

**D19 (reaffirms D15). Collection migration ambiguity — reaffirmed.**
Explicitly restated by the product owner: no remedy is chosen for Decks containing both child Decks and direct Cards until Phase G's preflight report proves such records exist. Card preservation is mandatory regardless of which remedy is eventually chosen. No change in substance from D15; recorded here because the product owner restated it directly alongside D17/D18, and this log treats an explicit restatement as worth its own entry rather than assuming silence means agreement.

## 2026-07-23 — Phase B/C first PR: implementation decisions and amendments

The product owner reviewed the pre-coding plan and issued five amendments before implementation proceeded. Applied during the build, not after — recorded here alongside the resulting design decisions.

**D20. `/design-preview/*` is a structurally separate top-level route, not a child of `AppShell`.**
`src/app/router.tsx` registers `design-preview` as a sibling of the `/` route object, with its own `children` array — not nested inside `AppShell`'s children. Chrome-free by construction: `AppShell`, `Sidebar`, and `BottomNav` are never imported or rendered on this path, not hidden via CSS after mounting.

**D21. Production's `FlipCard` (`src/components/ui/FlipCard.tsx`) was found to fail keyboard activation and accessible semantics, and was not modified — a new, separate primitive was built instead.**
The product owner asked for verification against eight criteria before reuse. Content-driven height, different-height faces, backface-visibility, reduced-motion, no clipping, and no fixed aspect ratio all held (inherited from the CSS mechanism). Keyboard activation and accessible semantics did not: the front face is a plain `<div onClick>` with no `tabIndex`, `role`, or key handler. Rather than edit the file production imports (touching `ReviewSession.tsx`'s dependency graph), a small, separate replacement was built: `src/features/reviewV2/components/FlipCard.tsx`, using its own namespaced CSS (`.itera-flip*` in `src/index.css`, a duplicate of the `.flip*` rules under distinct names) plus `role="button"`, `tabIndex`, `onKeyDown` for Enter/Space, `aria-pressed`, and `aria-hidden` on whichever face is currently the back. Zero footprint on any file production imports. Flagged as worth reconsidering in Phase E: either the production primitive gains the same accessibility fix (benefiting the live app too) or the two stay permanently separate — not decided here.

**D22. `CodeView`'s theme-context-driven syntax palette was a real defect under the app's default configuration, not an acceptable temporary gap — fixed at the root, not papered over with a CSS-only patch.**
The original plan excluded `--code-bg` from the `.itera-scope` token re-point specifically to avoid a light-chrome/dark-palette mismatch, treating the mismatch as an acceptable known gap. The product owner correctly rejected this: since the app defaults to dark mode for a user with no stored preference (`getInitialTheme()` in `src/app/theme.tsx`), the defect would be visible under the *default*, not an edge case. Root cause: `CodeView` chooses `oneDarkHighlightStyle` vs `defaultHighlightStyle` from live `ThemeContext`, not from a CSS variable, so no CSS override could fix it. Fix: `ThemeContext` is now exported from `src/app/theme.tsx` (one-line, purely additive — every existing consumer still goes through `useTheme()`/`ThemeProvider` unchanged), and `src/features/design-preview/ForceLightTheme.tsx` provides a static `{theme: 'light', ...}` value to its subtree, without touching `document.documentElement` or `localStorage`, leaving the app's real global theme completely unaffected outside that subtree. `--code-bg` is now included in `.itera-scope`'s re-point, since the palette and background now always agree.

**D23. One behavioral test suite was added for `RecallCardDemo`, requiring two new devDependencies not previously in this repo.**
`vitest.config.ts` runs `environment: 'node'` — no DOM — so component testing had no existing infrastructure. Added `@testing-library/react`, `@testing-library/user-event`, `@testing-library/dom` (a required peer), and `happy-dom` as devDependencies; the new test file opts into `happy-dom` via a per-file `// @vitest-environment happy-dom` pragma rather than changing the global config, so the rest of the suite (14 other files, unaffected) keeps its fast, DOM-free default. The test fixture deliberately contains no fenced code block: `RichText`'s code-block path mounts CodeMirror via `LazyCodeView`, which wants DOM APIs (`ResizeObserver`, layout measurement) `happy-dom` doesn't fully provide — a real environment-compatibility gap, not a shortcut. The real C++ code content is exercised by loading the live route, not by this automated test. Nine tests cover: Space reveals (checked via the revealed face's `aria-hidden` state, not text presence — both flip faces are always mounted, so text-presence queries can't distinguish reveal state), reveal happens synchronously regardless of motion settings, Tip disappears on reveal, Explanation/rating controls appear only after reveal, keys 1-4 select the expected rating and only after reveal, a rating shortcut does not fire again once submitted, Space targeted at a focused control is not double-handled (see the honest caveat below), Space is prevented from scrolling, and held-key repeat does not duplicate the reveal.
One test's real strength is limited and said so in its own comment, not silently: the "focused control" test exercises the `isInteractiveTarget` guard's code path, but in this component the guarded action (`if (!revealed) setRevealed(true)`) is idempotent either way, so the scenario can't currently fail without the guard — it's a regression pin for future non-idempotent actions, not proof the guard is currently load-bearing.

**D24. Testing-library/react's auto-cleanup does not engage in this repo's Vitest config (no `globals: true`), which caused every render in the new test file to accumulate in the same document until an explicit `afterEach(cleanup)` was added.**
Not a design decision so much as a debugging note worth keeping: two tests failed with element-count mismatches (multiple "Good" buttons found, nine copies of answer text found) before this was diagnosed and fixed locally within the one new test file (not added to the shared `src/test/setup.ts`, to avoid any risk to the 13 other, DOM-free test files).

## 2026-07-23 — Font loading gap found and fixed

Fonts (`Inter`, `Inter Tight`, `JetBrains Mono`) were referenced in `--font-sans`/`--font-mono`/`--font-itera-*` but never actually loaded anywhere — no `<link>`, no `@font-face`. Every render (including production) had silently been falling back to system fonts. Fixed by self-hosting via `@fontsource/{inter,inter-tight,jetbrains-mono}` (offline-capable, matches this PWA's local-first design, no CDN request) and `@import`ing the needed weights at the top of `src/index.css`. Since `@font-face` registration is global, this also fixes production's identical pre-existing gap as a side effect — a bug fix to already-broken CSS, not a new redesign decision, but touches what the live app renders, so noted explicitly.

## 2026-07-23 — Phase E: Review foundation (Recall/Multiple Choice/Write Code)

Implemented per direct instruction, proceeding without a formal audit checkpoint (deferred until more interaction types exist to evaluate together). Full detail in the PR report; decisions worth keeping here:

**D25. `reviewService.ts` stays shaped around `SchedulingState`, not `CardState`, and does not persist anything yet.**
No CardV2 repository exists (out of scope this milestone — no schema changes). The service computes real FSRS results (`reviewState`/`buildReviewLog`, unchanged) but the design-preview callers pass a fresh `initialSchedulingState()` each time and nothing is written anywhere. Phase D's job is to make `submit` actually read/write a `CardState` store; the public signature is designed not to need to change when that happens.

**D26. v1's `src/hooks/useReview.ts` was deliberately left untouched, not routed through `reviewService`.**
Its `useGradeCard`/`useUndoGrade` inline the same orchestration `reviewService.submit` now also does — genuine duplication. Consolidating them was considered and skipped: it would touch a production hook `ReviewSession.tsx` depends on, for a code-hygiene benefit rather than user-visible progress, in a milestone already large. Flagged as a reasonable, low-risk follow-up, not done here.

**D27. Write Code has no partial read-only region — the whole starter block is editable.** `CodeEditor.tsx` doesn't support mixed editable/read-only content in one instance. Presenting state uses the editable `LazyCodeEditor`; feedback state swaps to the read-only `LazyCodeView` (unmodified) showing the frozen submitted answer, rather than adding a `readOnly` prop to the shared editor. Accepted answers in fixtures must therefore match the *whole* submitted text, signature included — an honest reflection of the current constraint, not a fixture shortcut.

**D28. Only three of six v2 interaction types exist.** `getInteractionDefinition` throws loudly for Ordering/Matching/Walkthrough rather than silently rendering nothing — deliberate, so an unbuilt type fails fast in development instead of shipping a blank screen.

## 2026-07-23 — Phase E continued: Ordering, Matching, Walkthrough

All six v2 interaction types are now registered. Implemented per direct instruction; full detail in the PR report, decisions worth keeping here.

**D29. Ordering's keyboard path is explicit Move up/down buttons, not `@dnd-kit`'s `KeyboardSensor` — a deliberate deviation from what V1's ordering renderer does.**
V1 (`src/features/cards/renderers/ordering/Question.tsx`) does use `KeyboardSensor` with `sortableKeyboardCoordinates`, confirmed by inspection before building this. It was not reused for the v2 renderer for two reasons: (1) `KeyboardSensor` needs the row itself to be a keyboard-operable drag handle (Space to pick up, arrows to move, Space to drop), which means a non-button element intercepting Space — directly colliding with the Review shell's global Space handler (reveal/submit). Real `<button>` Move-up/down controls are excluded from that guard by construction (`isInteractiveTarget`), so there's no collision to reason about. (2) It composes cleanly with "drag-and-drop must not be the only interaction method" — the buttons are a complete, independent second path, not a fallback bolted onto the same mechanism. Mouse/touch drag still uses `@dnd-kit` (`PointerSensor` only, no `KeyboardSensor`), via a drag handle (`GripVertical`) that is `aria-hidden` and not in the tab order, since it does nothing keyboard-operable.
Boundary buttons (top item's "up", bottom item's "down") use `aria-disabled` + `pointer-events-none`, not the native `disabled` attribute — a browser force-blurs a focused element the moment it becomes natively disabled, which would eject focus the instant an item reaches an edge, defeating "keep focus on the moved item." Focus retention itself falls out of `key={id}` reconciliation on each row: moving an item re-renders the list, but React keeps the same DOM node (and therefore the same focused button) for a given id.

**D30. `ObjectiveResult` (`reviewPhase.ts`) gained an optional `score?: number` field for partial credit.**
Recall/Multiple Choice/Write Code stay binary and never set it. Matching (fraction of relationships correct) and Ordering/Walkthrough (fraction of positions/steps correct) do. `ReviewSessionScreen`'s `suggested` rating hint now reads it: fully correct suggests Good (3), partially correct (`score > 0`) suggests Hard (2) instead of flatly Again (1), still only ever a quiet hint the learner can override — the correctness/rating separation itself (spec) is unchanged.

**D31. Matching's v2 grading is uniform across fixed and unique (per-row) columns — no value-equality special case like V1's.**
V1's matching (`src/features/cards/renderers/matching/columns.ts`) has to branch: unique columns grade by pairing id, fixed columns grade by string value, because its fixed-column options are bare strings. V2's `MatchingColumn.items` always carry a stable `id`, fixed or not (a fixed column just shares one item list across every row) — so `gradeMatching` (`src/domain/grading/matching.ts`) is one code path: compare the chosen item id to the authored item id for that row+column, always. This is a byproduct of the schema already being more generalized (D13), not a new schema change.

**D32. The Review shell no longer coerces a `null` `autoGrade` result into `{correct: false}`.**
Exposed by Walkthrough: a walkthrough made entirely of `recall` steps (self-graded, like the top-level Recall interaction) has nothing objective to grade, so `aggregateWalkthroughResult` correctly returns `null` — but Walkthrough must be `interactive: true` (it needs a structured response, not a bare reveal), so it goes through `SUBMIT_RESPONSE`/`autoGrade`/`RESPONSE_VALIDATED`, and the shell's old fallback (`result ?? { correct: false }`) turned that legitimate `null` into "graded and wrong," which would have shown "Incorrect" and suggested Again for content nothing ever checked. `ReviewSessionScreen.primaryAction` now dispatches `autoGrade`'s result as-is; `null` reaches `reviewPhase`'s `feedback.result` unchanged (the phase type already allowed it), matching Recall's own self-graded convention. Multiple Choice/Write Code/Ordering/Matching are unaffected — their `autoGrade` never legitimately returns `null`.

**D33. Walkthrough's multi-step state lives entirely inside the shell's `response`/`setResponse` — the shell's phase machine only transitions once, at the very end.**
`WalkthroughState` (`interactions/walkthrough/state.ts`) tracks `stepIndex` plus per-step `answers`/`results`, all internal to the View. Every step is answered while the shell's phase stays `presenting`; only the last step's "Finish" button calls the shell's `onPrimaryAction`, which is what actually submits the card and reveals the Explanation/rating (spec: one Card, one final CardState/rating). "Each step records its first submitted objective result" is enforced by never overwriting an `answers`/`results` entry that already exists; step navigation (Previous, or revisiting via the progress dots) stays available even after the whole card is graded, purely for review, rendering already-answered steps read-only regardless.

**D34. Registered `orderingDefinition`/`matchingDefinition`/`walkthroughDefinition` in `interactions/registry.ts`; all six v2 interaction types now resolve.** The registry stays typed as `Partial<...>` (not a plain `Record`) so a future 7th interaction type still fails loudly via `getInteractionDefinition` instead of silently rendering nothing — same rationale as D28, just no longer describing a gap.

## 2026-07-23 — Production Review integration

The live `/review` route now renders through the v2 shell — not just `/design-preview/*`.

**D35. `ReviewSessionV2` (`src/features/review/`) replaces `ReviewSession` on the production `/review` route.** Every due v1 `Card` is migrated to `CardV2` via `migrateCard` on read (the one lazy migration this codebase allows) and rendered through the exact same `ReviewSessionScreen`/registry used by the design previews — no second copy of the shell. Grading persists back onto the real `Card.scheduling` via a new `usePersistReviewResult` hook, which takes `reviewService.submit`'s already-computed `{after, log}` rather than recomputing it, so the v1 (`useGradeCard`) and v2 grading paths can't silently diverge. `ReviewSession`/`useReviewSession` (v1) are untouched and still in the tree, unreferenced — swapping back is a one-line import change in `ReviewPage.tsx` if needed. Not done: restyling `ReviewPage`'s own loading/"nothing due" guard clauses, which stay v1-styled — contained scope, the reviewing surface itself is what changed.

**D36. `ForceLightTheme` moved out of `src/features/design-preview/` into `src/features/reviewV2/components/`, and a new `IteraSurface` (ForceLightTheme + `.itera-scope`) was extracted, shared by `PreviewShell` and `ReviewSessionV2`.** Production needing the same light-only/CodeView-palette mechanism preview already used was a genuine missing-abstraction moment (design-preview code shouldn't be something production imports from, even though the mechanism itself is exactly right for both) — moving it out and factoring the shared wrapper was the correct-sized fix, not a bigger refactor.

**D37. Deleted `.env.local.bak`** (dead credentials for a Supabase project the product owner deleted mid-session). It was gitignored and never committed — no leak — but the project no longer exists, so the file was pure clutter. Confirmed first that local dev has no active `.env.local` and was already running on Dexie-only, so nothing else needed to change.

## 2026-07-23 — Phase D: CardState extraction (steps 1–3 of 6)

Per `itera-migration-plan.md` §4 and the shared `MigrationRunner` contract in §0. This milestone covers steps 1–3 (additive schema, backfill, dual-write) only — steps 4–6 (parity verification over a real observation period, read cutover, and later cleanup) are explicitly not started, matching the plan's own staging.

**D38. The new `CardState` row shape is `src/types/cardV2.ts`'s `CardState` interface exactly (including `suspended`), not the bare `SchedulingState` shape `itera-migration-plan.md` §4's prose literally describes.** `cardV2.ts`'s `CardState` was already the locked, documented target shape for this exact purpose ("so the eventual CardState split needs no translation layer" — its own doc comment). Inventing a second, slightly different intermediate shape now would only mean a translation step later, for no benefit. `suspended` — a top-level `CardBase` field in v1, not part of `SchedulingState` — is folded in here because that's `CardState`'s own design, not a new decision.

**D39. `CardState` is keyed by its own `cardId` field, not a synthetic `id`.** Dexie: `cardStates: 'cardId'` (inline primary key, version 3 — see `src/data/dexie/db.ts`). Supabase: a bespoke `createCardStateRepo` (`card_id text primary key references cards(id) on delete cascade`), since the generic `crud<T extends {id: ID}>` helper in `SupabaseRepository.ts` is hardwired to a field literally named `id` — the same reason `createCardRepo`/`createReviewRepo` are already bespoke in that file, not a new pattern.

**D40. Dual-write is wired into every call site that writes `Card.scheduling` or `Card.suspended`:** `useGradeCard` and `useUndoGrade` (v1 grading/undo), `usePersistReviewResult` (v2 grading, added this session), `useCreateCard` (initial `CardState` row for a new card), and `useSaveCard` (also the suspend-toggle path — dual-writes unconditionally on every save rather than trying to detect which fields changed, since rewriting `CardState` with unchanged values is harmless and idempotent). `useDeleteCard` also deletes the matching `CardState` row, to avoid an orphan — Postgres additionally enforces this via `on delete cascade`, but the app-level delete is what gives Dexie the same guarantee.

**D41. Reads are not cut over.** `getDue()` (both backends) and everywhere else that reads scheduling still reads `Card.scheduling` exclusively. `CardState` is write-only from the rest of the app's perspective right now — this is step 5 in the plan, deliberately not started here, since the plan requires a real parity-observation period (step 4) first and the product owner hasn't reviewed one.

**D42. The Supabase side (`supabase/migrations/0001_card_states.sql`, the matching block in `schema.sql`, and `SupabaseRepository`'s `card_states` code) is implemented but unverified against a live database.** The product owner's Supabase project was deleted mid-session (see D37) — there is currently nothing to apply this against. Written to the same standard as the rest of the schema (RLS, the grant that prevents the known 403 bug, an `on delete cascade` FK to `cards`) and ready to run whenever a project exists again; flagged rather than silently assumed correct.

**D43. `createCardStateBackfill` (`src/domain/migration/cardStateBackfill.ts`) is built and tested — dry run, apply, idempotence, orphan detection, rollback instructions, all per the `MigrationRunner` contract (§0) — but was not run against the product owner's real local Dexie data this session.** `apply()` against real data is explicitly a human-reviewed action per the plan's own contract, not something to trigger silently as a side effect of shipping the code. Left for the product owner (or a follow-up, on request) to actually invoke.

**D44. Added a "Card scheduling migration (Phase D)" section to production `SettingsPage.tsx`, so the product owner can actually run D43's dry run/apply themselves.** Data lives client-side in the browser's IndexedDB — there is no way to run the backfill against the product owner's real data from outside the browser, so a UI trigger is the only way D43's "human reviews the dry-run report before apply()" requirement can actually happen for a local-only app. Deliberately plain, v1-styled (`Section`/`Button`, matching the existing Export/Import UI directly above it) — this is an operational/data-integrity tool, not part of the visual redesign, so it does not use `.itera-scope`. Apply is disabled until a dry run has actually produced a report in this session (not just "some report exists somewhere"), and additionally requires confirming a `window.confirm` dialog before writing. *(Superseded by D46 below: `.itera-scope` now wraps the whole app, so this section inherits the Itera palette too, same as everything else in `SettingsPage.tsx` — it just no longer needed its own explicit choice not to opt in.)*

## 2026-07-23 — Whole-app visual migration, increment 1: global Itera reskin

The product owner reported that running the app still showed "the old website" outside Review/`/design-preview` — correct: only those two surfaces had been migrated. Two decisions were made before implementation (both explicit, via `AskUserQuestion` during plan mode) rather than assumed:

**D45. Scope is a visual pass, not the full spec-accurate rebuild (Phases F–M).** The product owner chose to keep today's data model, page structure, and behavior exactly as they are, and only change what pages *look* like — Collection/Deck split, a new Library/Deck IA, Today's real Momentum/Suggested-Session logic, a new Progress heat map, Create/Edit rework, onboarding, and responsive layouts are all explicitly deferred, not silently folded in. The full plan lives at the (session-local) plan file used for this pass; the summary is recorded here and in `itera-redesign-plan.md`.

**D46. `.itera-scope` now wraps the whole app, not just Review/`/design-preview`.** `AppShell.tsx` renders its entire tree inside `IteraSurface` (`src/features/reviewV2/components/IteraSurface.tsx`, reused as-is — not a second copy). This works with zero per-page edits because `.itera-scope` re-points the *same* shared semantic CSS variables (`--bg`, `--panel`, `--border`, `--text`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--shadow`, `--code-bg`, ...) every existing v1 page already consumes via Tailwind utilities — confirmed via grep before implementing that there are zero hardcoded/literal Tailwind color classes anywhere in `src/features` or `src/components` bypassing this token system, and that `RoadmapCanvas.tsx`'s one SVG already uses `fill="var(--color-muted)"` rather than a literal color. `--green`/`--amber`/`--red`/`--blue` were added to the re-point block (previously only bg/panel/border/text/accent/shadow/code-bg were covered) so tags and status colors reskin too — `--blue` has no direct Itera equivalent token, so it lands on `--itera-accent-active` rather than staying an unrelated literal blue.

**D47. The app is light-only for now; the dark/light toggle is temporarily unrendered, not deleted.** Itera's design tokens only define light-mode colors (spec §36 explicitly defers dark mode); the product owner chose not to have un-specified dark colors invented. `ThemeToggle` is no longer rendered in `AppShell`'s header (it would be inert under `ForceLightTheme` — clicking it would call a no-op `toggle()`), and `SettingsPage`'s own "Appearance" section was changed from a toggle button to a one-line explanation, for the same reason (an inert control is worse than an honest note). `ThemeToggle.tsx`, `ThemeProvider`, and `useTheme` are unchanged — this is reversible the moment an Itera dark palette exists.

**D48. Shell chrome (`Sidebar.tsx`, `BottomNav.tsx`, `AppShell.tsx`'s header) got manual detailing beyond the automatic color re-point**, since radius and typography aren't covered by re-pointing color variables: `rounded-itera-control`/`rounded-itera-pill` in place of ad hoc `rounded-[9px]`/`rounded-lg`/`rounded-full`, `font-itera-display` on page titles, and the active sidebar item changed from a solid soft-fill pill to a left accent bar plus a light tint — spec §4.4 (LOCKED) treats orange as "a strategic signal, not theme paint," used for "active navigation underline or focus marker," not a bold fill.

**D49. This pass does not touch page logic, the nav item list, routes, or Roadmaps' presence in the nav.** Every existing page keeps its current data-fetching hooks, component structure, and behavior — restructuring the information architecture (new Library/Deck pages, a new global shell layout, Roadmaps' eventual retirement from top-level nav) is Phase H, deliberately not part of this pass. What happens next is driven by the product owner actually looking at the reskinned app and reporting a punch list — not a second blind pass guessing at what else needs fixing.

## 2026-07-23 — Today page, built from a reference mockup

The product owner supplied a concrete mockup image of the target home page and asked for it directly, rather than waiting for Phase I. Three scope questions were asked (via `AskUserQuestion`) before building anything, all answered explicitly:

**D50. The new top-nav shell is scoped to Today only, not the whole app.** Every other route keeps rendering inside `AppShell`'s left sidebar until its own turn (Phase H, or whenever it's asked for next). Two different navigation styles coexisting in the meantime is an accepted, explicit tradeoff — not an oversight.

**D51. Today's content is placeholder/illustrative, not wired to real data, on purpose.** Streak, weekly-session count, recent-recall percentage, the milestone, the suggested-session numbers (24 cards / 15 minutes / topic list), the four Continue Learning rows, and the pace chart's two series are all static example values. Real due-counts already exist (`useDueCards`) and could have been wired into Continue Learning immediately, but the product owner chose to nail the look with placeholder data first, in one consistent pass, rather than mixing some real and some invented numbers in the first cut. Wiring real data (and designing what "streak"/"weekly goal"/"milestone"/"suggested session" actually mean — none of that logic exists yet) is explicit follow-up work, not assumed to already be decided.

**D52. Roadmaps stays out of Today's nav, reaffirming D17** — the mockup showed it as a top-level link, but the product owner confirmed D17 (Roadmaps excluded from the redesigned MVP navigation) is still current; the mockup predates that decision.

**D53. Router restructured: `/` now belongs to `TodayPage` as its own top-level route (structurally separate, same pattern as `design-preview`); `AppShell` became a pathless layout route (`element` with no `path`) for its other children.** This keeps every other URL (`/decks`, `/review`, `/settings`, ...) byte-identical — only `/`'s owner changed. `DashboardPage.tsx` is untouched and still in the tree, unreferenced — reversible via `router.tsx` in one line. `navItems.ts`'s first entry was relabeled "Dashboard" → "Today" (same `to: '/'`, same destination, just an accurate label) since it still appears in the old sidebar as a working link to the new page.

**D54. New files, all under `src/features/today/`: `TodayShell.tsx` (top nav), `SuggestedSessionHero.tsx`, `MomentumPanel.tsx`, `ContinueLearningList.tsx`, `PaceChart.tsx`, `TodayPage.tsx`.** `PaceChart` is a hand-rolled SVG (no charting library), matching this codebase's existing convention (`RoadmapCanvas.tsx`) and the dataviz skill's guidance loaded before building it: one shared axis (actual vs. target sessions-completed, not two different measures), thin 2px lines with rounded caps, the accent hue reserved for the one real series, a dashed neutral line for the projection, a single end-point marker rather than a point per value. The logo mark stays the existing simple `{}` monogram (recolored) rather than attempting the mockup's illustrated overlapping-card mark — spec §4.2 describes that mark conceptually with no actual asset provided, and reproducing it well is real illustration work, not something to approximate blindly.

## 2026-07-26 — Library browser + focused Deck page (preview only, ahead of Phase G/H)

Two new `/design-preview/*` routes exercising the Library visual direction against fixtures — explicitly requested ahead of Phase G's Collection/Deck migration and Phase H's real Library/Deck pages, not a sequencing mistake.

**D55. Built as isolated, fixture-driven preview routes only — `/design-preview/library` (Collection nav + Deck list) and `/design-preview/library/:deckId` (focused Deck page, Cards/Insights tabs) — a deliberate deviation from Phase B's stated scope note that these routes ship "only once [Phase H's] real, reusable components exist."** Acceptable here because it was explicitly asked for and scoped as isolated: no Collection/Deck data migration, no CardState migration, no production database changes, no Create/Edit, no Review interaction work. All new files live under `src/features/design-preview/library-{shared,browser,deck}/`; nothing under `src/types/`, `src/domain/`, or `src/components/ui/` changed. `fixtures.ts` invents local `LibraryCollection`/`LibraryDeck`/`LibraryCard` types — deliberately **not** named `Collection`/`Deck`/`Card`, each with a header comment stating it's a preview-only stand-in for Phase H's not-yet-built real shape (per D8: Collections are *derived* from existing `parentId` structure by an explicit migration, not authored fresh), not a proposal for it.

**D56. A product-supplied mockup (`itera-mockups/webapp/library.png`, the focused Deck page only — no browser-only or Insights-tab mockup exists) conflicted with the original written brief in three places; the written brief won in every case, confirmed explicitly with the product owner rather than assumed:** no per-collection icons (the mockup showed folder/file glyphs; kept indentation + a restrained selected-marker only, no icons anywhere in `CollectionNav.tsx`), no "Quick Actions" block or sidebar "+" add-collection button (Create/Import is out of scope for this pass), and — per a direct follow-up instruction — the mockup's bottom keyboard-shortcut tip strip and the sidebar's bottom Settings link were both dropped (Settings is a real, separate route; the tip strip is review-session guidance, not relevant to browsing). The mockup's top-nav streak badge **was** kept, as a static illustrative element — same placeholder-data spirit as `MomentumPanel.tsx`'s streak row.

**D57. The "Design preview — not part of the live app" banner every other `/design-preview/*` route renders (via `PreviewShell.tsx`) is deliberately absent from the Library screens, per explicit product-owner request ("I don't know why that was there").** Scoped to `LibraryPreviewShell.tsx` only — `PreviewShell.tsx` itself is unmodified, so the six `review/*` preview routes keep the banner. This is a one-off product call for this surface, not a reversal of the general preview-banner convention.

**D58. Building this surfaced (and fixed) a real, pre-existing bug in production `TodayShell.tsx` — the one file this pass touched outside `design-preview/`.** The header's logo block (img + "Itera" wordmark) had no flex-shrink floor; at narrow viewport widths (~430px) it compressed below its own content's width and the wordmark visually overlapped the nav's "Today" link (confirmed via bounding-box inspection — the nav's box literally started before the logo's rendered text ended, not just a crowded-but-intact layout). Fixed with `shrink-0` on every header child (logo, nav, search, streak badge, Create button, avatar) plus `whitespace-nowrap` on the nav links and `overflow-x-auto` on the row, so the row scrolls horizontally instead of overlapping when content doesn't fit. Applied to both `TodayShell.tsx` and the isolated `LibraryTopNav.tsx` duplicate it was copied from, so the two stay visually consistent. **Trade-off, accepted, not hidden:** below ~460px the Create button and avatar now sit past the visible edge and need a horizontal scroll to reach — a real responsive collapse (hiding/menuing nav items) was out of scope for this fix.

## 2026-07-26 — Library browser completed against a new browse-view mockup (preview only, still ahead of Phase G/H)

A product mockup showing the Library **browse/default** state specifically (`itera-mockups/webapp/all-decks.png`) arrived after D55-D58 were written — D56 had noted no such mockup existed yet. This pass reconciles the existing preview against it and a fuller requirements list, still entirely inside `src/features/design-preview/library-{shared,browser,deck}/` plus one router entry and one `DesignPreviewIndex.tsx` entry. No Collection/Deck migration, no Create/Edit, no production types/domain/repository changes.

Reverses one specific point of D56: the Collection nav header now has a small inert `+` "Add collection" icon button.** D56 dropped it because "Create/Import is out of scope" for that pass; the new brief explicitly asks for it back as a visual affordance, same as the existing inert "New Deck" button and `OverflowMenu`'s Rename/Move/Delete. D56's other two calls (no per-collection icons, no Quick Actions/Settings in the sidebar) are unchanged and still hold.

The narrow-width (`<880px`) Collection nav changed from a flattened horizontal tab row to a real slide-in drawer (`CollectionNavDrawer.tsx`), per master-spec §29.1's "Collection drill-down or drawer" requirement — D58's tab-row fallback is superseded, not deleted from history.** The tab row lost all nesting (every collection, parent or child, rendered as one flat row of tabs); the drawer reuses the same `CollectionNav` tree component unmodified, so indentation and hierarchy survive at narrow widths. Trigger is a full-width bar showing the current scope; the drawer closes on selection, backdrop click, `Escape`, or its own close button.

Selecting a Collection from the focused Deck page's nav or breadcrumb now round-trips the clicked selection through the browser's URL (`selectionToSearchParams`/`selectionFromSearchParams`, `collectionTree.ts`), landing pre-scoped to what was clicked instead of always resetting to "All Decks."** This was a real navigation-consistency gap in the original pass, not a new feature — the kind of "small adjustment" the brief explicitly allows on the already-built Deck page.

**D63. The "empty library" state (zero decks) exists in `LibraryBrowserPreviewPage`'s render branches but was previously undemonstrable — the fixture array is always non-empty.** `LibraryBrowserPreviewPage` now takes optional `collections`/`decks` props (defaulting to the real fixtures), and a second route, `/design-preview/library-empty`, renders it with `[]`/`[]` — one route per named scenario, matching the existing convention of the six `review/*` preview routes rather than an in-page dev-only scenario switcher (which doesn't exist anywhere else in this codebase).

**Deliberately left out:** an "Import Deck" button next to "New Deck" — present in the mockup, absent from the written requirements list, and Create/Import remains explicitly out of scope for this pass.

## 2026-07-26 — Phase F begins: Card creation/preview/display, Recall only, real CardV2 storage

The first real (not preview-only) slice of Phase F: a complete vertical flow for the Recall interaction type — interaction chooser, create/edit screen with a live preview that reuses the actual production Recall review component, a compact row in the focused Deck page, a read-only Card detail view, and a non-committing "Study this Card" preview. The other five interaction editors are explicitly out of scope, as is Progress, the CardState read-cutover, and the Collection/Deck migration.

**D64. `CardV2` becomes real, persisted storage — a deliberate scope expansion beyond Phase F's original file list, confirmed explicitly with the product owner over the more conservative alternative (writing v1 `Card` objects and deriving `CardV2` in-memory only via `migrateCard`).** A new `CardV2Record` type (`src/types/cardV2.ts`) extends the spec's `CardV2` with `suspended`/`scheduling`/`order` — mirroring how v1 `CardBase` embeds scheduling, rather than the CardState-extraction pattern. This is the load-bearing design choice: `CardV2Record`'s scheduling is embedded and self-contained, **not** read from or written to the existing `cardStates` dual-write store (Phase D). Reusing `cardStates` here was considered and rejected — it would mean CardState reads begin now, which is a partial start on the explicitly out-of-scope CardState read-cutover. New repository member `cardsV2: CardV2Repo` (`src/data/repository.ts`), Dexie table `cardsV2` (`db.ts` version 4), Supabase table `cards_v2` (`supabase/schema.sql`, same jsonb-plus-generated-columns pattern as `cards` — unverified against a live database, same caveat as `card_states` per D42), and inclusion in `src/data/backup.ts`/`src/domain/io/backup.ts` (optional field, same pattern as `roadmaps`/`cardStates`).

**D65. Legacy v1 `basic`/`codeReading`/`bugFinding` cards migrate to a `CardV2Record` the moment they're opened and saved through the new Recall editor — not in bulk, not automatically.** The new record reuses the original card's `id`/`createdAt`/`scheduling`/`suspended` (so `ReviewLog` history for that id stays valid and FSRS state isn't reset), then the superseded v1 `Card` row and its `cardStates` dual-write mirror are deleted (`src/domain/cardsV2/saveRecallCard.ts`, unit-tested directly against a repository). Until a legacy card is touched this way, it keeps rendering via the old `CardRow`/`CardEditorPage` — an intentional transitional coexistence, confirmed with the product owner over the alternative of leaving legacy types permanently on the old editor.

**D66. The interaction chooser renders all six types (matching the approved mockup); only Recall is enabled.** The other five render as visually muted, non-interactive tiles with a "Coming soon" caption, rather than being hidden — signals what's next without pretending they work. `ENABLED` in `CardTypeChooser.tsx` is the one line that changes as each subsequent interaction type's editor is built.

**D67. "Study this Card" (from `CardDetailPage`) is a non-committing preview, not a real graded session** — confirmed explicitly with the product owner over the alternative (a real single-card review session via a new `?card=` filter on `/review`). This was the deciding factor in **not** merging `CardV2Record`s into the global due queue this milestone: `useDueCards`/`ReviewPage`/`ReviewSessionV2` still only read v1 `cards`, so a `CardV2Record` can be authored, previewed, and studied non-committingly, but doesn't yet surface for real spaced-repetition review. Flagged as a known, explicit gap — not an oversight — to close in a later milestone once the due-queue integration is actually scoped.

**D68. `StatusBadge`, `formatDue`/`formatLastStudied`, `InteractionTypeBadge`/`InteractionTypeTile`, and `interactionTypeMeta` were promoted out of `design-preview/library-shared/` into `src/features/cardsV2/shared/`, once a real (not preview-only) consumer — `CardRowV2`/`CardDetailPage` — needed them.** They were already built against the real `SchedulingStateKind`/`InteractionType` types, not fixture-only concepts, so this is a straight move (imports in `design-preview/library-deck/CardRow.tsx` and `library-browser/DeckRow.tsx` updated), not a rewrite — the same "promote once it proves reusable" pattern as `IteraSurface`/`ForceLightTheme` (D36). A new `OverflowMenu` (`src/features/cardsV2/shared/OverflowMenu.tsx`) was also extracted as a real, shared, functional primitive (outside-click-to-close via a ref, matching the `design-preview/library-browser/OverflowMenu.tsx` idiom) — two independent ad hoc copies of this pattern already existed in the codebase (v1's `CardRow.tsx` `MoveMenu`, and that same design-preview one) before this made a third; worth finally sharing.

**D69. `CardDetailPage` (the read-only prompt/answer/tip/explanation overview at `/cards/:id`, D67/D68's context) was removed the same day it was built, after the product owner actually clicked through it and found it was purely an extra step in front of the two things people want directly from a Deck: study the card, or manage it.** Reworked instead, per direct product feedback: `CardRowV2`'s row body now opens `CardStudyPreviewPage` directly (`cards/:id/study` — a click on a card in its deck goes straight to the flip-card preview, not a text summary of the same content); an inline Edit (pencil) button and an overflow menu (Duplicate/Move/Suspend/Delete) sit on the row itself, next to the status/due columns — the same idea as "Due now"/"New" being visible inline rather than behind a click. Move is a new small local `MovePopover` (`CardRowV2.tsx`), not the shared `OverflowMenu`, since it needs a deck list rather than a flat action list. `/cards/:id` and `CardDetailPage.tsx` are deleted, not just unlinked — this is the "measure real usage before committing to a screen" case the append-only log exists to capture, not a reversal to be embarrassed about. `RecallEditorShell`'s `onSaved` callback was widened from `(recordId: string) => void` to `(record: CardV2Record) => void` so callers (`CardCreatePage`, `CardEditEntry`) can navigate to the record's actual (possibly just-changed) `deckId` rather than a stale one captured before the save.

**D70. Fixed a real bug this surfaced, not introduced by it: `useCard`/`useCardV2`'s query functions returned `undefined` for a not-found id, which TanStack Query v5 treats as a bug ("Query data cannot be undefined") and logs to the console.** This was latent before (harmless as long as nothing routinely queried an id absent from a given store) but became a routine, reproducible console error once `CardEditEntry` started probing both `useCard` and `useCardV2` for every `cards/:id/edit` visit — a `CardV2Record`-native card is by definition "not found" in the v1 store, and vice versa for untouched legacy cards. Fixed at the source (`queryFn: async () => (await repo.X.getById(id)) ?? null`) in both hooks, not worked around in the one new call site, since `null` is the documented correct "not found" value and every existing consumer already used falsy (`!data`) checks that work identically with `null`.

**D71. Multiple Choice becomes the second complete vertical slice (chooser → editor → live preview → Deck row → Study preview → Review), following Recall's exact pattern.** Investigation going in found the scope much narrower than expected: the type (`McOption`/`MultipleChoiceInteraction` in `cardV2.ts`), the grading function (`domain/grading/multipleChoice.ts`), the registry entry and `MultipleChoiceView` (`reviewV2/interactions/multipleChoice/`), and the v1 `mcq` → v2 migrator (`migrateMcq`) were already built and tested from earlier Review-layer work — only the authoring/editor layer was missing. New files mirror Recall's exactly: `domain/cardsV2/multipleChoiceForm.ts` (form state, preview/record conversions, `legacyMcqCardToForm`, and a `validateMultipleChoiceForm` pure validator — ≥2 options, all filled, ≥1 correct, single-select forbids >1 correct), `domain/cardsV2/saveMultipleChoiceCard.ts` (same new/v2/v1-migrate save contract as `saveRecallCard.ts`), `features/cardsV2/MultipleChoiceOptionRow.tsx` (add/remove/reorder/mark-correct option row, reordering cloned from `reviewV2`'s `OrderingRow.tsx` move-up/down-button idiom so it stays keyboard-operable without dnd-kit), `MultipleChoiceFields.tsx`, `MultipleChoiceLivePreview.tsx` (adds an explicit "Reset preview" button + local remount-key counter, since unlike Recall's preset-driven reset, Multiple Choice's `ReviewSessionScreen` only returns to the unanswered phase on remount), and `MultipleChoiceEditorShell.tsx`. **Chose a parallel shell over extracting a shared `CardEditorShell`** despite `RecallEditorShell.tsx`'s own comment half-anticipating one ("other interaction types will get their own `*Fields`/`*LivePreview` pair behind the same shell shape") — duplicating the ~50-line header/Organize/layout block was judged lower-risk than modifying Recall's own file for a payoff that only matters once a third type's editor exists; revisit when Write Code (the next type) is built.

Two small, real defects were found and fixed in the already-built Review-time layer, not Recall: (1) `ReviewSessionScreen.tsx`'s shared `isInteractiveTarget` keyboard guard didn't exclude `role="radio"`/`role="checkbox"` elements, so a single Enter keypress on a focused Multiple Choice option could both toggle the selection and fire the shell's submit handler using a stale pre-toggle response in the same event — fixed by adding both roles to the guard's selector (this also happens to fix Walkthrough's embedded `StepMultipleChoice`, which shares the identical pattern). (2) `MultipleChoiceView.tsx`'s pre-submission selected-vs-unselected state was color-only (border/background tint, no shape or icon) — added a persistent leading indicator (filled circle for single-select, filled square for multiple-select, with a checkmark) so selection reads without relying on color, matching spec's explicit requirement. `CardRowV2.tsx`'s interaction-tile line and `CardStudyPreviewPage.tsx`'s type guard, which were both narrowly hardcoded to `'recall'` despite the components underneath already being fully generic, were generalized to read `card.interaction.type` directly — pure generalization, no new logic. Both `cardV2SearchableText` functions (Dexie, Supabase) gained a `multiple_choice` branch over option text.

## 2026-07-26 — Phase F continued: Write Code

The third complete vertical slice (chooser → editor → live preview → Deck row → Study preview → Review), following Recall's and Multiple Choice's pattern.

**D72. Investigation found the same shape of gap D71 found for Multiple Choice: the entire Review-time half of Write Code already existed from Phase E** — `WriteCodeInteraction` (`cardV2.ts`), the comparison function (`domain/grading/writeCode.ts`, already fully tested), the registry entry and `WriteCodeView` (`reviewV2/interactions/writeCode/`), the v1 `codeCompletion` → v2 migrator (`migrateCodeCompletion`), and a working `/design-preview/review/write-code` route (D27's decision — no partial read-only region, whole starter block editable, feedback swaps to the read-only viewer — was already implemented there and needed no changes). Only the authoring/editor layer was missing. New files mirror Multiple Choice's set: `domain/cardsV2/writeCodeForm.ts` (form state, preview/record conversions, `legacyWriteCodeCardToForm` via `migrateCard`, and a `validateWriteCodeForm` pure validator — prompt required, ≥1 accepted answer, every accepted answer non-blank; starter code is deliberately *not* required, since a blank starting point is a valid card), `domain/cardsV2/saveWriteCodeCard.ts` (same new/v2/v1-migrate save contract), `features/cardsV2/WriteCodeAnswerRow.tsx` (one accepted answer per `LazyCodeEditor` instance, with move-up/move-down/remove cloned from `MultipleChoiceOptionRow.tsx`'s idiom — rows are keyed by a form-only answer id, not index, so identity survives reorder/remove even though the persisted `WriteCodeInteraction.acceptedAnswers` is a plain `string[]`), `WriteCodeFields.tsx` (comparison's four toggles kept inside a collapsed `<details>` disclosure — "visually secondary" per spec, using the same custom `role="checkbox"` idiom as Multiple Choice's toggles), `WriteCodeLivePreview.tsx` (same resetKey-counter + "Reset preview" pattern as Multiple Choice's), and `WriteCodeEditorShell.tsx`.

**D73. `WriteCodeInteraction.editableRegion?` stays unconsumed — no author-facing control was added for it.** The task driving this slice asked for "editable region configuration" as an editor field, but D27 already established that `CodeEditor.tsx` has no mixed editable/read-only support and the whole starter block is the editable region; adding a region picker that doesn't affect Review behavior would be a control that lies about what it does. Instead, `WriteCodeFields.tsx` shows a short inline caption under the starter-code field documenting the current limitation, and the data model is untouched so a real per-region implementation (a genuinely new CodeMirror `changeFilter`/`Decoration`-based effort, not a config flag) can consume `editableRegion` later without a migration.

**D74. Chose a third parallel shell (`WriteCodeEditorShell.tsx`) over extracting a shared `CardEditorShell`, despite D71 flagging Write Code as the moment to revisit that call.** Asked explicitly; the answer was to keep duplicating the header/Organize/layout block rather than touch `RecallEditorShell.tsx`/`MultipleChoiceEditorShell.tsx` — those two stay the untouched reference implementations for this milestone. The extraction remains open, now with three near-identical shells as the evidence for when it's done.

No changes were needed to `CardRowV2.tsx`, `CardStudyPreviewPage.tsx`, the interaction registry, `WriteCodeView.tsx`, or `writeCode.ts` grading — all were already generic/complete for `write_code`. `CardTypeChooser.tsx`'s `ENABLED` gained `'write_code'`; `CardCreatePage.tsx`/`CardEditEntry.tsx` gained `write_code` branches (the latter also picks up v1 `codeCompletion` cards); both `cardV2SearchableText` functions (Dexie, Supabase) gained a `write_code` branch over accepted-answer text. No separate read-only Card detail screen was built for Write Code, consistent with D69 — `CardStudyPreviewPage` (which already shows language and starter code via `WriteCodeView` itself) plus the row's inline Edit/overflow menu is the intended "detail" surface for every interaction type, not just Recall/Multiple Choice.

Only the authoring/editor layer was missing, exactly as Phase F's own status note already said. Previously the only signal a move had happened was the visible index number and each button's `aria-label` text changing — neither is a reliable screen-reader announcement on its own. This is a small, additive fix (no visual/DOM/test changes to the existing 9 passing tests), not a reconsideration of D29's button-based keyboard approach, which stands as originally reasoned.

No changes were needed to `CardRowV2.tsx`, `CardStudyPreviewPage.tsx`, the interaction registry, `OrderingView.tsx`'s grading logic, or `ordering.ts` grading — all were already generic/complete for `ordering`. `CardTypeChooser.tsx`'s `ENABLED` gained `'ordering'`; `CardCreatePage.tsx`/`CardEditEntry.tsx` gained `ordering` branches (the latter also picks up legacy v1 `ordering` cards — note the v1 and v2 discriminants share the same string, unlike `mcq`→`multiple_choice`'s rename). No separate read-only Card detail screen was built, consistent with D69 — `CardStudyPreviewPage` (which already renders `OrderingView`'s own "Correct order" reveal) plus the row's inline Edit/overflow menu remains the intended "detail" surface for every interaction type.

## 2026-07-27 — Phase F continued: Matching

The fifth complete vertical slice (chooser → editor → live preview → Deck row → Study preview → Review), following Recall's/Multiple Choice's/Write Code's/Ordering's pattern.

**D76. Investigation found the same shape of gap D72/D74/D75 found for the previous three types, but one step richer: not just the Review-time half but the entire richer N-column/3-part/fixed-option data model, grading, and `MatchingView` already existed from Phase E** — `MatchingColumn`/`MatchingColumnItem`/`MatchingInteraction` (`cardV2.ts`, already deliberately beyond the spec's 2-column MVP per D13/D31), `gradeMatching`/`isMatchingResponseReady` (`domain/grading/matching.ts`, two-column/three-part/fixed-column cases already tested), the registered `matchingDefinition` + `MatchingView` (`reviewV2/interactions/matching/`, 5 passing tests covering keyboard pairing, clearing, taken-value unavailability, submit-gating, and feedback text), and the v1 `matching` → v2 migrator (`migrateMatching`, which already builds fixed vs. unique columns and an optional third column from `triple`/`options`). Only the authoring/editor layer was missing, exactly as Phase F's own status note already said.

New files mirror Ordering's set, generalized for a variable number of columns: `domain/cardsV2/matchingForm.ts` (`MatchingFormState` — a distinguished source-row list plus a variable `columns` list, each either free-text-per-row or a shared fixed option list; `toMatchingInteraction` synthesizes non-fixed-column item ids as `${rowId}:${columnId}`, deliberately reusing `migrateMatching`'s exact synthetic-id convention so a migrated legacy card round-trips losslessly; hydration reconstructs by id lookup against `relationships`, never by parsing synthetic id strings; `validateMatchingForm` checks column/row minimums, required cells, fixed-column option minimums, and duplicate-value detection for every place correctness depends on uniqueness; plus 8 exported pure mutation helpers — `addMatchingColumn`/`removeMatchingColumn`/`setColumnFixed`/`addMatchingOption`/`removeMatchingOption`/etc. — kept in the domain module rather than as local closures in the Fields component, unlike every prior type's convention, specifically so their cascade-cleanup invariants (removing a column strips it from every row's cells; removing or un-fixing a fixed option resets any row that referenced it) stay directly unit-testable), `domain/cardsV2/saveMatchingCard.ts` (same new/v2/v1-migrate save contract), `features/cardsV2/{MatchingOptionRow,MatchingColumnEditor,MatchingRow,MatchingFields,MatchingLivePreview,MatchingEditorShell}.tsx` (a fifth parallel shell, same reasoning as D71/D74/D75 — still no extraction).

**The "add a 2nd+ column" editor affordance is a generic "Add column" button, not a v1-editor-style "Three-part matching" checkbox** — confirmed with the product owner over mirroring the v1 `Editor.tsx`'s hardcoded left/right/third affordance, since a plain add/remove button generalizes to any number of columns without a special-cased triple toggle. Column reordering was deliberately left out of scope (add/remove only); row reordering was kept, since row order is genuinely user-visible (the display order of `MatchingView`'s accordion), unlike a fixed column's option order (no reorder control on `MatchingOptionRow`).

**No changes were made to the already-shipped `MatchingView.tsx`.** It renders as a single fluid accordion list at every viewport width — it never lays out columns side by side — so it already satisfies spec §20.2/§29.4's mobile requirement ("on mobile, use a stepwise pairing flow or **stacked assignment controls**") via that alternative, verified directly in a real browser at a 390px viewport alongside the desktop width. This is the one place this slice's own instructions explicitly allowed touching a completed Review component if a genuine shared problem turned up; none did.

No changes were needed to `CardRowV2.tsx`, `CardStudyPreviewPage.tsx`, the interaction registry, `MatchingView.tsx`'s grading logic, or `matching.ts` grading — all were already generic/complete for `matching`. `CardTypeChooser.tsx`'s `ENABLED` gained `'matching'` (only Walkthrough remains "Coming soon"); `CardCreatePage.tsx`/`CardEditEntry.tsx` gained `matching` branches (the latter also picks up legacy v1 `matching` cards, including `triple`/fixed-option ones — the v1 and v2 discriminants share the same string, like `ordering`'s). Verified end-to-end in a real running instance (dev server + a headless-browser driver), not just unit tests: two-column create/save/reload, a three-column fixed-option card create/save/reload, the Deck row's violet Matching tile, Study preview rendering and grading a real submission, and the narrow-viewport editor/preview tab layout — zero console errors at any step. No separate read-only Card detail screen was built, consistent with D69 — `CardStudyPreviewPage` plus the row's inline Edit/overflow menu remains the intended "detail" surface for every interaction type. As a pre-existing, unrelated gap noticed but out of scope for this slice: `cardV2SearchableText` (Dexie/Supabase) has branches for `recall`/`multiple_choice`/`write_code` but not `ordering` or `matching` — left as-is, since neither this slice nor Ordering's added one, and fixing it isn't part of the authoring-layer gap this phase targets.

## 2026-07-27 — Phase F continued: Walkthrough (sixth and final vertical slice)

**D78. Investigation found the same shape of gap D72/D74/D75/D76 found for the previous four types, confirming Phase F's own status note: the entire model, grading, Review UI, and migration for Walkthrough already existed from Phase E — only the authoring/editor layer was missing.** `WalkthroughInteraction`/`WalkthroughStep` (`cardV2.ts`, multi-range `focus` per D13), `gradeWalkthroughStep`/`objectiveStepIds`/`aggregateWalkthroughResult` (`domain/grading/walkthrough.ts`, already tested), the registered `walkthroughDefinition` + `WalkthroughView`/`StepResponse`/`state.ts`/`focusLines.ts` (`reviewV2/interactions/walkthrough/`, already tested), the `interactionTypeMeta.ts` entry (teal tile, `Grid2x2` icon), and the v1 `story` → v2 `walkthrough` migrator (`migrateStory`) were all already built. New files mirror Matching's set, generalized for a variable number of ordered steps: `domain/cardsV2/walkthroughForm.ts` (`WalkthroughStepFormState` — a flat, type-irrelevant-fields-ignored shape per step, mirroring `MatchingColumnFormState`'s `fixed`/`options` convention, rather than a discriminated union, so switching a step's response type never needs to reconstruct the step object; line ranges authored as `WalkthroughRangeFormState` add/remove rows of start/end numbers, not a free-text spec string; `validateWalkthroughForm` checks shared prompt/scenario, per-step response requirements matched to that step's type, and range well-formedness including against the shared code's actual line count; plus exported pure mutation helpers for steps/ranges/MC-options/accepted-answers, kept in the domain module rather than local Fields-component closures for the same cascade-testability reason `matchingForm.ts` gives, since steps nest one level deeper than any prior type's form), `domain/cardsV2/saveWalkthroughCard.ts` (same new/v2/v1-migrate save contract), `features/cardsV2/{WalkthroughStepEditor,WalkthroughFields,WalkthroughLivePreview,WalkthroughEditorShell}.tsx` (a sixth and final parallel shell, same reasoning as D71/D74/D75/D76 — still no extraction). Multiple Choice's step sub-editor reuses `MultipleChoiceOptionRow.tsx` directly (already generic over `McOptionFormState`, no adapter needed); exact-input's accepted-answer list is a small local row, not `WriteCodeAnswerRow.tsx`, which is code-editor-backed and wrong-shaped for plain-text answers.

**Two small, additive extensions were made to already-shipped Review-time code — the one place this slice's own instructions explicitly allowed touching a completed interaction, since Walkthrough is the first type with in-place multi-step navigation and genuinely exposes gaps nothing else needed:**
1. `ReviewSessionScreen.tsx` gained an optional `initialResponse?: InteractionResponse` prop (default `undefined`, used only as `useState`'s initial value) so `WalkthroughLivePreview` can seed a mid-sequence preview state and offer "Step N" inspection buttons that jump straight to any authored step (seeding earlier steps with neutral placeholder answers, author-inspection only — no objective result is attached, so no feedback banner appears on the seeded steps). Every other call site omits the prop; behavior for Recall/Multiple Choice/Write Code/Ordering/Matching is unchanged.
2. `WalkthroughView.tsx` gained explicit focus movement: the step panel (`tabIndex={-1}`) receives focus on `goTo` after the initial mount, so screen-reader/keyboard users land on the new step's prompt/highlight context instead of stale focus on the Previous/Continue button. Nothing else in `reviewV2` needed this — every other interaction type is single-screen.

**A real, minor defect was found and fixed during manual browser verification, not introduced deliberately:** `validateWalkthroughForm` could push the identical error string twice (e.g. two blank highlighted ranges on the same step both failing "Step N has an invalid line range."), which produced a React duplicate-key console warning against the error `<ul>`'s `key={message}` convention (shared by every prior type's Fields component). Fixed by deduping the returned `errors` array (`Array.from(new Set(errors))`) — `canSave` is computed from the pre-dedup array length, so no validation strength is lost, only the displayed list's uniqueness.

No changes were needed to `CardRowV2.tsx`'s existing structure, `CardStudyPreviewPage.tsx`, the interaction registry, `WalkthroughView.tsx`'s grading logic, or `walkthrough.ts` grading — all were already generic/complete for `walkthrough`, beyond the two additive extensions above. `CardTypeChooser.tsx`'s `ENABLED` gained `'walkthrough'` (all six types now enabled — Phase F's authoring milestone is complete); `CardCreatePage.tsx`/`CardEditEntry.tsx` gained `walkthrough` branches (the latter also picks up legacy v1 `story` cards via `legacyStoryCardToForm`). `CardRowV2.tsx` gained one new, Walkthrough-only affordance: a quiet `"N steps"` indicator next to the tags line — no prior type showed an item/pair/step count on the row, and this one is intentionally not retrofitted onto Ordering/Matching. No separate read-only Card detail screen was built, consistent with D69 (confirmed explicitly for this slice rather than assumed) — `CardStudyPreviewPage` (rendering the real `WalkthroughView`) plus the row's inline Edit/overflow menu remains the intended "detail" surface for every interaction type, Walkthrough included. Verified end-to-end in a real running instance (dev server + a headless-browser driver): a two-step Walkthrough (Recall step with a 3-range highlight spanning lines 2, 5, and 7–8, plus a Multiple Choice step) authored, live-previewed through the actual `WalkthroughView`, saved, round-tripped through the editor, shown correctly on the Deck row (teal tile, "2 steps"), and studied to completion (multi-range highlighting rendered correctly in Review, per-step feedback, final rating controls with the correct suggested rating) — zero console errors after the validation dedup fix above. As with Ordering/Matching, `cardV2SearchableText` (Dexie/Supabase) gained no `walkthrough` branch — the same pre-existing, unrelated gap, left as-is since it isn't part of the authoring-layer scope this phase targets.

## 2026-07-27 — Card create-flow UX pass: one continuous screen, sticky chooser, opt-in preview drawer

Product feedback on the Phase F create flow (all six types now shippable) targeted `CardCreatePage.tsx`/`CardTypeChooser.tsx`/the six `*EditorShell.tsx` files, not any interaction type's own authoring fields.

**D80. `CardCreatePage.tsx` no longer swaps its whole return value when a type is picked.** Previously selecting a type in "Choose interaction" replaced the entire page with that type's editor — a full-screen swap the chooser itself. Now the chooser always stays mounted; the matching `*EditorShell` renders in a section below it (keyed by `selectedType` so switching types remounts with a fresh form rather than carrying over stale state), revealed with the existing `.reveal-in` fade/slide-up animation (`src/index.css`, already used by Review/`PreviewPage`/`CardView` — reused rather than adding a second animation primitive). `CardTypeChooser.tsx` gained an optional `selected` prop so the active tile stays visibly highlighted (solid `itera-accent` fill) instead of losing that state once picked, since it's now a persistent control, not a one-shot chooser.

**D81. The chooser is `sticky top-[77px]` (not `position: fixed`), pinned directly beneath `AppShell`'s own already-sticky header (measured at 77px tall) rather than overlapping it.** Two sibling `sticky top-0` elements in the same scroll container don't auto-stack by height — each positions independently relative to the scrollport — so the offset is required. The wrapper carries `-mx-4 px-4 md:-mx-6 md:px-6` to cancel `<main>`'s own padding (`AppShell.tsx`) so its `bg-bg` background spans edge-to-edge behind it while scrolling, matching the header's own treatment, and a `z-[5]` (below the header's `z-10`) so it never visually competes with it. The 77px figure is specific to this route's active nav title/subtitle ("Decks" / "Organize decks and their cards") — if `findActiveItem` ever resolves a different (longer-wrapping) title for this route the offset would need re-measuring, but this is stable today for both `/decks/:deckId/cards/new`'s only entry point (the Decks page).

**Follow-up fix, same day: D80's first implementation still visibly jumped the chooser tiles when a type was picked, reported with before/after screenshots.** Root cause: the page-level "← Cancel" (shown only pre-selection, since `CardEditorShell` grows its own Cancel once a shell renders) was conditionally *unmounted* rather than hidden, and it lived inside the same sticky wrapper as the chooser — removing it shrank the content above the chooser within that wrapper, so the chooser's resting position (sticky elements sit at their normal in-flow position until actually scrolled past their `top` threshold — untrue-but-easy-to-assume that "sticky" alone prevents any reflow) shifted up by exactly the removed button's height. Pixel-measured against the reported screenshots: the chooser's leftmost accent-bordered pixel moved from `(700, 163)` to `(700, 125)` — a clean 38px vertical jump, no horizontal shift (the "moves to the side" in the original report was this same reflow, not a separate bug). Fixed by keeping the Cancel button always mounted at a constant height and toggling only `invisible` (`visibility: hidden`, not `display: none`) plus `tabIndex={-1}` once a type is picked, so the space it occupies never changes. Re-measured post-fix: identical `(700, 163)` bounding box before and after selecting a type.

**D82. Extracted `CardEditorShell.tsx`, reversing D71/D74/D75/D76/D78's repeated "keep duplicating, don't extract" call.** Those decisions each deferred extraction because the payoff didn't yet justify the risk of touching already-shipped, tested shells for one more near-identical copy.
