That calculus changes once a change must land *identically* in all six shells at once, which this request (opt-in preview drawer below) is — asked explicitly, and confirmed extraction now rather than a seventh hand-duplicated copy. `CardEditorShell` owns the header (Cancel / title / "Preview card" toggle / Save) and the wide-vs-narrow layout; each `*EditorShell.tsx` now only builds its `editorPane` (Fields + Organize block, unchanged) and passes it plus its `*LivePreview` element in. All six shell files shrank from ~135 lines to ~90, with no behavior change to their save/validate logic.

**D83. The live preview is now opt-in, not a permanent half-width column, on wide screens.** Previously `isWide` always rendered editor and preview as two `grid-cols-2` columns inside a `max-w-3xl` page (each column ~360px, too narrow for e.g. Matching's multi-column editor or Write Code's code editor). Now: the editor pane gets the page's full width (up to `AppShell`'s `<main>` cap of 1080px) and is itself centered at `max-w-2xl` for readable line lengths; a "Preview card" button in the header (only rendered when `isWide` — the narrow breakpoint keeps its pre-existing Editor/Preview tab toggle, untouched) reveals the preview in a drawer to the right. The narrow (<980px, `useIsWideEditor`) tab behavior is completely unchanged.

**D84. The drawer's open/close animation is a plain `width`/`opacity` CSS transition on an `overflow-hidden` wrapper around a fixed-width (`420px`) inner element, not a transform/translate.** This was deliberately chosen over `translateX` because animating the *box width* while clipping via `overflow-hidden` naturally produces "reveal grows left-to-right, hide retracts right-to-left" (more of the fixed-width content becomes visible from its left edge as the box widens, and vice versa) — exactly the direction asked for — with no JS measurement or transform math needed. The row's `gap` (0 ↔ 1.5rem) transitions in step via a sibling `.preview-shell-row` class so the editor pane doesn't jump when the drawer reaches zero width. Both rules live in `src/index.css` alongside a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` override (matching `.reveal-in`'s existing convention) that disables both transitions outright. Verified via a real running instance that no horizontal page overflow occurs mid-transition (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth` stayed pinned to the viewport width throughout), and that the narrow-viewport tab layout and all `cardsV2` component tests (36 passing, unaffected by the refactor since they only assert on the always-rendered editor tab/Save button) still pass.

## 2026-07-27 — Card create-flow UX pass continued: header realignment + a simplified preview

Product feedback (with before/after screenshots) on D80-D84's header: "Cancel / New card / Preview+Save" were three unrelated alignment anchors above a body whose width changed when the preview opened, and the live preview itself was cluttered with Review-session chrome irrelevant to authoring.

**D85. `CardEditorShell` now shares one literal outer width between its header and its editor/preview row, instead of each being independently laid out.** Both live inside one root div whose own `max-width` is `42rem` (`EDITOR_COL`) with the preview closed, or `calc(42rem + 1.5rem + 420px)` with it open (a `.card-editor-shell` CSS class transitions this, mirroring D84's drawer/gap transitions) — the editor pane no longer centers itself independently inside a wider shell (that self-centering was the actual bug: header spanned the full available width while the editor visually sat indented within it). The header is a `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto]` (stacking to one column below `sm:`) with a left-aligned title + contextual subtitle and a right-aligned action group — **"New card" → "Create card"**, subtitle is now `"{Interaction} · {Deck path}"` (a new `editorSubtitle()` helper in `shared/`, computed identically by all six shells from data they already have — `flatDecks`/`form.deckId`), **Cancel renamed "← Back to deck" and moved into the action group** (previously left-floating, now grouped with Preview/Save), and **Save is the only solid-orange action** (Preview/Back to deck are both neutral/ghost — already true for Preview, now true for Back to deck too).

**D86. Added `PageHeaderOverride` (`src/components/layout/`) so a focused-workspace route can replace `AppShell`'s topbar title/subtitle and hide its "Study now" CTA.** Product feedback: the topbar kept showing "Decks — Organize decks and their cards" plus a "Study now" button while actively creating/editing a card — contradictory context, and a second solid-orange CTA competing with Save. A `PageHeaderOverrideProvider` wraps `AppShell`'s layout (split into `AppShell` + `AppShellLayout` so the provider sits above both the topbar and `<Outlet>`); `CardEditorShell` calls `useSetPageHeader({ title, sub: subtitle, hideCta: true })` on mount, reverting to the nav-item default on unmount. Scoped to only fire once a shell is mounted (i.e., once an interaction type is picked) — the bare chooser screen still shows the default "Decks" header, judged an acceptable, low-traffic edge case rather than adding a second override call site in `CardCreatePage` for it. The context hook no-ops (doesn't throw) outside a provider specifically so the six `*EditorShell.test.tsx` files, which deliberately render a shell standalone without `AppShell`, keep working unmodified.

**D87. Replaced the editor's live preview's use of `ReviewSessionScreen` with a new, deliberately simpler `InteractionAnswerPreview` (`cardsV2/shared/`), for five of the six types.** Product feedback: "the only thing that should be shown is the flashcard itself... a toggleable show front/question and back/solution" — `ReviewSessionScreen`'s `ReviewTopBar` (Exit session / "N of N" / keyboard-shortcut hint) and `RatingControls` ("Preview only — nothing recorded") are real-session mechanics with no place in an authoring preview, and each interactive type's own "Submit answer" button implies an attempt-then-grade flow the preview doesn't need. Rather than growing `ReviewSessionScreen` itself with a pile of caller-specific hide-flags (its own doc comment already says it's "deliberately free of" concerns beyond the core Review shell), `InteractionAnswerPreview` renders the real `definition.View` — same production component, preserving D71's "not a fake mockup" fidelity — but drives `phase` from one local boolean via a plain Question/Answer segmented toggle instead of the reducer/grading/rating machinery. `InteractionViewProps` gained one optional field, `hideActions` (default undefined, set only by this component), which the four interactive views (`MultipleChoiceView`/`WriteCodeView`/`OrderingView`/`MatchingView`) use to (a) suppress their own "Submit answer" button and (b) force `locked` regardless of phase — the latter so an author idly clicking an option while inspecting the front can't leave the "Answer" toggle showing a misleading graded state; response is `undefined` for the entire preview's lifetime, so "Answer" always reflects a clean, consistent unanswered-attempt feedback view. This also let the four types' `*LivePreview.tsx` drop their old resetKey/"Reset preview" button entirely (nothing left to reset). Recall's `RecallView` needed no `hideActions` handling (no submit button; `onPrimaryAction` already just flips, now wired to toggle both ways). TipPanel/ExplanationPanel are kept (author-relevant authored content, not session chrome).

**Walkthrough was deliberately excluded from D87** and keeps using `ReviewSessionScreen` directly, since its existing bespoke "Step N" jump preview (`initialResponse`, added for Walkthrough specifically — see the Phase F Walkthrough entry above) doesn't fit a plain front/back binary — each step has its own answer, and the shell's `phase` only ever reaches `feedback` once, at the very last step. Instead, `ReviewSessionScreen` gained two small optional props, `hideTopBar`/`hideRating` (both default false — every other caller, including real Review, omits them and is unaffected), which `WalkthroughLivePreview` sets to drop the same session chrome without disturbing its step-navigation model. The rating-phase keyboard shortcut (`1`–`4`) is now also gated on `!hideRating`, since the buttons it triggers are hidden there — closes off a latent path where a stray keystroke in the Walkthrough preview could have called the real `reviewService.submit`.

All 343 tests pass (one single-run failure during verification was confirmed flaky — reproduced green on immediate re-run of both the isolated file and the full suite). Verified end-to-end in a real running instance: header/content edges pixel-aligned in both preview-open and preview-closed states; Recall and Multiple Choice previews checked with real authored content in both Question and Answer states; AppShell topbar correctly swaps to "Create card" / "{Interaction} · {Deck}" with "Study now" hidden once a type is picked.

## 2026-07-27 — App Shell and Visual Foundation Convergence

Card-interaction work was frozen for this milestone (Walkthrough was already the sixth and final type shipped in the entry above). The goal: remove the last legacy application frame — `AppShell.tsx`'s left `Sidebar`/`BottomNav`/per-route topbar — and converge every standard route onto one Itera top-nav shell, per the locked IA (`Today · Library · Progress`, Search/Create/Profile, Review immersive/chrome-free). This substantially completes Phase H's "global shell rewrite" and promotes its Library/Deck UI from fixture-driven preview to production, but **deliberately without Phase G's Collection/Deck entity split** — see D88 below for why, and what that means for Phase G/H's remaining scope.

**D88. Collections remain a UI-only derivation over the existing `Deck.parentId` tree, not a real `Collection` entity — Phase G (the Collection/Deck migration) was not run.** Phase H's original file list (`docs/itera-redesign-plan.md`) specified `src/domain/collections/tree.ts` operating on a real, migrated `Collection` type, and named Phase G's preflight report/explicit migration as a hard dependency. This milestone's own instructions forbade any Dexie/Supabase schema migration, so `src/features/library/collectionTree.ts` instead defines "Collection" purely structurally: any deck with at least one child deck is a Collection node in the Library's nav; childless decks are the actual browsable Library decks; a leaf deck's `collectionId` is simply its `parentId`. This is a real, provable invariant (every ancestor of a deck-with-children is itself a deck-with-children, so a collection's own `parentId` always resolves to another collection or is absent) — not an approximation — but it is not what Phase G's migration would produce: there is still no `Collection` type, no `collectionId` field, and a deck cannot hold both its own cards and children simultaneously in the derived Collection view (visiting it directly at `/decks/:id` still shows its own cards exactly as before — nothing is hidden — it just isn't offered as a Collection's "leaf deck row" in the browser list). Phase G's preflight report has still never been run against real data; the "decks with both children and directly-attached cards" ambiguous case Phase G's step 1 exists to measure is entirely unmeasured. **Phase G remains fully open** — if/when a real `Collection` entity is ever migrated in, `collectionTree.ts`'s derivation is the thing that gets replaced, not extended.

**D89. Route paths are unchanged; only the shell and the `/decks`/`/decks/:id` components moved.** `src/features/library/{LibraryBrowserPage,LibraryDeckPage}.tsx` (promoted from `design-preview/library-browser/LibraryBrowserPreviewPage.tsx` and `library-deck/LibraryDeckPreviewPage.tsx`, wired to real `useDecks`/`useSearchCards`/`useSearchCardsV2`/`useCreateDeck`/`useSaveDeck`/`useDeleteDeck` instead of `library-shared/fixtures.ts`) now render at `/decks` and `/decks/:id` in `src/app/router.tsx`, replacing `DecksPage`/`DeckDetailPage` — no new `/library` path was introduced, since ~15 existing call sites (`/review?deck=`, `/preview?deck=`, every "back to deck" redirect in `CardEditEntry.tsx`/`CardCreatePage.tsx`) already hardcode `/decks/:id` and a rename would be pure churn for a shell-convergence milestone. The nav *label* is "Library" (`src/components/layout/primaryNavLinks.ts`); the *path* stays `/decks`. `src/features/decks/{DecksPage,DeckDetailPage}.tsx` are **not deleted** — left present but unrouted, kept only until full feature parity (in particular `DeckDetailPage`'s dnd-kit drag-reorder and per-card suspend/move/delete, both already ported into the new `LibraryDeckPage` via the same `CardRow`/`SortableCardRow`/`CardRowV2` components DeckDetailPage used) is independently re-confirmed, per this milestone's "no destructive route cleanup" boundary.

**D90. `/review` moved from an `AppShell` child to its own top-level route in `router.tsx`, matching the same "structurally separate, not CSS-hidden" pattern `/` and `/design-preview/*` already used before this milestone.** Contrary to what earlier redesign-plan prose implied, `/review` was **not** already chrome-free in production before this change — it was a plain `AppShell` child, rendering inside the full sidebar/topbar exactly like Decks/Browse (verified directly by reading `router.tsx`, not assumed). `ReviewPage.tsx`'s loading/"nothing due" branches, which relied on `AppShell` for `IteraSurface`/colors, now wrap themselves in `IteraSurface` directly; `ReviewSessionV2`'s main branch already self-wrapped and needed no change. `RouteError.tsx` (the shared `errorElement`) is now also wrapped in `IteraSurface`, since an uncaught error previously rendered in the raw un-scoped palette (`index.html` still defaults `data-theme="dark"` pre-paint) — a 1-line strengthening of the existing dark-mode suppression, not new dark-mode work.

**D91. `AppShell.tsx` was rewritten in place** (`IteraSurface` → new `TopNav` → `<main>` → `Outlet`, no sidebar, no bottom nav) rather than added as a second, parallel shell — `Sidebar.tsx`, `BottomNav.tsx`, and `navItems.ts` (the sidebar/bottombar's single source of truth, including `findActiveItem`) were deleted outright, not deprecated in place, since they were pure chrome code with no persisted state (categorically different from the V1-types/Roadmap-data/backup-support deletions this milestone's instructions forbid). **`PageHeaderOverride.tsx` (D86) was also deleted, reversing D86** — the new shell has no per-route topbar title/subtitle slot at all for a route to override; `CardEditorShell`'s `useSetPageHeader` call (its only call site) was removed along with it, since that call existed purely to suppress the old topbar's "Decks"/"Study now" leakage, which no longer exists structurally. `TodayShell.tsx` (the Today-only top-nav shell from the "Aside" whole-app-visual-pass entry above) was also deleted — its JSX is now `TopNav` — and `TodayPage.tsx` lost its own wrapper accordingly, rendering through the shared `AppShell` like every other route. `design-preview/library-shared/LibraryTopNav.tsx` (a third near-duplicate copy of the same nav JSX) was deliberately **left untouched** — `design-preview/*` is explicitly out of scope for this milestone, so the duplication there is accepted, not fixed.

New shared primitives, all under `src/components/layout/`: `TopNav.tsx` (the nav bar itself, generalized from `TodayShell`'s JSX), `primaryNavLinks.ts` (`Today`/`Library`/`Progress` — Roadmaps excluded per the already-settled D11/D17, reached only via Deck Settings-style Deck Detail links now, hidden from primary nav), `CreateMenu.tsx` (new: a deck-picker popover for the global "+Create" action — the one place this milestone made a genuinely new UI-shape call, since nothing upstream specified it further; picking a deck, or "+ New deck", navigates to the existing, untouched `decks/:deckId/cards/new` route), `ProfileMenu.tsx` (new: avatar + Settings link + conditional sign-out, gated on `isSupabaseConfigured` exactly like `SettingsPage`'s existing Account section). `useNavBadges.ts` was kept and repointed: its due-count badge, previously keyed to a `/review` sidebar item that no longer exists in primary nav, now surfaces on the `Today` link instead (studying is reached from Today's hero / Library's per-deck "Study now", not a dedicated nav destination).

**D92. Font packages were swapped from static per-weight `@fontsource/{inter,inter-tight,jetbrains-mono}` to the variable `@fontsource-variable/*` builds**, per this session's mandatory font-bundling instructions (the static packages already satisfied "self-hosted, no CDN request," but the instructions specifically preferred variable builds). Confirmed the installed packages' actual emitted family names before wiring tokens (`'Inter Variable'`, `'Inter Tight Variable'`, `'JetBrains Mono Variable'`) rather than assuming them. `--font-itera-sans`/`--font-itera-mono` (`src/index.css`) now alias `--font-sans`/`--font-mono` instead of repeating an identical literal stack under a second name — one font stack per family, two Tailwind utility names pointing at it. `--radius-card` (12px, legacy) now aliases `--radius-itera-card` (14px) instead of the two coexisting as a real, undocumented value conflict — this one-line change is what actually fixes every legacy page's (Browse/Drafts/Stats/Settings/old Decks) card radius, since they all already use the `rounded-card` utility and needed zero per-file edits.

**Incidental, unrelated one-line fix found during the build-verification step, not part of card-interaction work:** `src/features/reviewV2/interactions/ordering/OrderingView.tsx`'s `locked` variable was `boolean | undefined` (via `hideActions`, D87's optional prop), which `tsc -b`'s production build (not `tsc --noEmit`, which doesn't catch it) rejected. Confirmed pre-existing via `git stash` against a clean checkout before touching it. Fixed with `Boolean(hideActions)` — a type-safety coercion only, no behavior change, since `hideActions` is only ever `true` or `undefined` in practice.

**No Dexie/Supabase schema changed. No V1 card types, the V1 registry, `ReviewSession.tsx` (confirmed orphaned — no importer besides its own `useReviewSession.ts`), Roadmap data/repo/Supabase table, or backup/import support were touched.** `docs/itera-redesign-plan.md`'s Phase H/I status blocks and the "Aside" section were updated to match (see there); this entry is the append-only record of why.

## 2026-07-27 — Flashcard redesign: all six interaction types now flip, shared icon/width system, TopNav trimmed

Product feedback (with two reference mockups, `recall-card.png`/`recall-card-revealed.png`, plus an inspo-icons set and an `itera-reveal-hint` motion reference) drove a pass across every `reviewV2` interaction view, `FlipCard`, and the shared Tip/Explanation/label chrome. Recall's own redesign (pill label, centered prompt, Tip/Explanation restyle) had already landed in an earlier pass this same day; this entry covers the broader follow-up.

**D93. `InteractionLabel` now resolves its icon and text from `INTERACTION_META` (`cardsV2/shared/interactionTypeMeta.ts`) by `type`, instead of each view passing its own literal `text`/`icon`.** This is the single source of truth "Choose interaction" already used, so the flashcard's pill and the type-picker tile can never drift apart. Icons were changed to match reference art from the inspo-icons set (browsed and confirmed pixel-by-pixel, not guessed): `Brain` (recall, was `HelpCircle`), `ListChecks` (multiple_choice, unchanged), `CodeXml` (write_code, was `Code2`), `ListOrdered` (ordering, was `ArrowUpDown`), `Link2` (matching, unchanged), `Waypoints` (walkthrough, was `Grid2x2`). The inspo-icons themselves (1024px glow/blur raster PNGs) were deliberately **not** used directly — confirmed with the user first — since they'd be an indistinct blur at the ~16px pill size and don't share a visual language with the existing small flat lucide icons; the raster art was source material for icon *choice*, not an asset to embed.

**D94. `CardTypeChooser.tsx`'s color treatment was fixed alongside D93**, per explicit product feedback that "every icon is orange... weakens selection hierarchy": unselected-but-enabled tiles now use a neutral border/icon (`border-itera-border`, icon `text-itera-navy`) with a navy hover state (`hover:border-itera-navy hover:bg-itera-navy-soft`); only the *selected* tile is orange (`border-itera-accent bg-itera-accent`, white icon) — previously every enabled tile (selected or not) rendered with an orange border and orange icon, so the selected state barely read as different from any other enabled option.

**D95. The flashcard column is now `max-w-2xl` (42rem), matching `CardEditorShell`'s `EDITOR_COL` exactly** — product feedback that the card was "too wide," with an explicit target ("the width of the Choose interaction part"). Implemented as a single wrapper inside `ReviewSessionScreen.tsx` around `definition.View`/Tip/Explanation/RatingControls, deliberately **not** wrapping `ReviewTopBar` — the reference mockups show the top bar (Exit session / N of N / shortcut hint) spanning the full session width edge-to-edge while only the card column beneath it narrows, confirmed by pixel-measuring the mockup rather than assumed.

**D96. All six interaction types now use `FlashcardSurface`/`FlipCard` (front = question, back = answer/results), not just Recall** — product feedback: "all cards should be flippable, that's the point of flashcards," including that clicking Submit/Check-answer should flip the card rather than appending feedback inline below the same face. `CardPanel.tsx` (the old shared non-flipping surface) is now used by nothing; kept in place, not deleted, since deleting a still-referenced-by-name shared component on a UI-only pass felt like unnecessary churn beyond what was asked. **Walkthrough was deliberately excluded** and still uses `CardPanel` — its multi-step model has no single front/back moment (each step grades itself inline via `StepResponse`; the shell's `phase` only ever reaches `feedback` once, at the very last step), so there is no natural "answer face" to flip to. This is a scoping call made without asking, flagged here for the user to revisit rather than forcing an awkward flip onto a type it doesn't fit.

For the four newly-flipping interactive types (Multiple Choice, Ordering, Matching, Write Code), each view now computes two separate content trees keyed off `flipped = phase.kind !== 'presenting'`: front renders the live interactive controls (options/drag list/accordion/code editor + Submit button) and returns `null` once flipped; back renders a read-only results view (graded option/row coloring, correct-order/expected-answer reveal, frozen submitted code) and returns `null` until flipped. The `null`-until-relevant gating isn't just tidiness — `FlipCard` always mounts both faces (only one is `aria-hidden` at a time), so without it, both the interactive and graded renderings of the same list existed simultaneously in the DOM, which surfaced as real test failures (`getAllByText` doesn't respect `aria-hidden`, so duplicate content was found in queries that don't scope by role) and would have doubled up interactive elements (a second live drag-and-drop list, a second live code editor) sitting inertly behind the visible face.

**D97. Multiple Choice's option rows no longer render a visible checkbox/radio glyph** — product feedback: "they should not actually display the checkboxes... the behavior is visible by their background color changing." `role="radio"`/`role="checkbox"` + `aria-checked` are retained on the row for accessibility; only the little square/circle indicator element was removed, selection is communicated by the row's own border/background color exactly as it already was for the *selected* state, just without the redundant glyph.

**D98. A real bug in `FlipCard.tsx` was found and fixed while wiring up the four interactive types, not present before this pass because Recall's faces never contained interactive children.** The face `<div>`'s own `onClick`/`onKeyDown` (the whole-card "click anywhere to flip" affordance) fired on any bubbled event, including from nested controls — pressing Space while typing in Write Code's code editor, or Enter on an Ordering row's move-up button, bubbled up and re-triggered the card's own flip handler mid-interaction (reproduced first as two failing tests: a premature flip while typing a space character, and a submitted-then-immediately-hidden Submit button). Fixed with `isOwnActivation`, which walks up from the actual event target to find the nearest interactive ancestor (same selector concept as the shell's existing `isInteractiveTarget` guard) and only proceeds if that nearest interactive element is the face itself, not a nested button/input/`.cm-editor`/etc. Confirmed the fix doesn't regress Recall (clicking its plain prose content still flips — `closest()` walks past the non-interactive text straight to the face, which does match, since it's the boundary itself, not a nested control).

**D99 (point 7 of the feedback). Both faces are now click/keyboard-activatable, not just the front.** Previously only the front face had `role="button"`/`onClick`/`onKeyDown`; once flipped, there was no way to flip back except a control outside the card entirely (`InteractionAnswerPreview`'s Question/Answer tab toggle). The back face now mirrors the front exactly (`role="button"`, `tabIndex`, the same `isOwnActivation`-guarded handlers). In a real `ReviewSessionScreen` session this is a no-op past the `presenting` phase (`primaryAction()` is one-directional and returns early once `phase.kind !== 'presenting'`, by design — you don't un-submit mid-grading), so the practical effect is scoped to where the original complaint was: `InteractionAnswerPreview`'s toggle-based `phase`, and the `/design-preview/review/*` pages, where clicking the revealed back now genuinely flips back to the question.

**D100 (point 8 of the feedback). Card-to-card transitions now play a CSS entrance animation instead of an instant remount-and-blink**, inspired by the two-overlapping-rotated-cards `itera-reveal-hint` reference: `.itera-card-enter`/`.itera-card-enter-active` (`src/index.css`, respecting `prefers-reduced-motion` like `.itera-flip*` already does) rotate/offset/scale/fade the new card in from a slightly-off-stack resting pose to flat/full-size/opaque over ~0.5s. This is implemented as a mount-only effect inside `ReviewSessionScreen` itself (one `requestAnimationFrame` flip from the CSS's resting state to `-active`), relying on the fact that every caller already remounts the whole component per card via `key={card.id}` — no new id-tracking needed. **This is a scoped-down version of the full ask**: a true two-card crossfade (the outgoing graded card visibly sliding/rotating away *while* the next one animates in from behind) was considered and rejected for this pass, since it would require keeping the outgoing `ReviewSessionScreen` instance mounted during the transition (its own `window` keydown listener, rating buttons, etc. still live) purely for a ~400ms visual, which is a meaningfully larger and riskier change than an entrance-only animation. Flagged here as a known gap, not silently scoped down.

**D101. `TopNav`'s Search affordance and the global `+Create` action (`CreateMenu.tsx`) were removed outright**, per explicit product feedback ("What am I searching? That should belong in Library. Create - as well."). `TopNav`'s `showSearch` prop was deleted (no remaining callers passed `false`); `CreateMenu.tsx` (introduced in D91 above, same day) is now fully deleted, not deprecated — it had exactly one call site (`AppShell.tsx`) and the file is dead the moment that's removed. `AppShell`'s `rightSlot` is now just `<ProfileMenu />`. No replacement search/create affordance was added to the Library page in this pass — the feedback named where these belong, not a spec for what they should look like there, so building them into Library is left as its own follow-up rather than guessed at here.

All 364 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified visually via a real headless-browser run against `/design-preview/review/recall` (front/back, code-block alignment) both before and after the RichText `text-left` fix (D-adjacent: `RichText.tsx`'s fenced-code-block wrapper gained an explicit `text-left`, since a centered ancestor's `text-align` was inherited into `LazyCodeView`'s code text otherwise — code must never center regardless of caller).

## 2026-07-27 — Library redesign: sidebar drills to decks, Deck page gets tabs/toolbar/pagination, top-nav streak

Product feedback (with a reference mockup, `library-use.png`) asked for a large set of Library/Deck-page enhancements in one pass — icons throughout, a Cards/Insights split, the card-list toolbar (search/type/status/sort) with pagination, a restyled deck-header action group, a top-nav streak indicator, and moving a "Tip" aside from a page footer into the sidebar's empty space. All of it landed in `src/features/library/*` and `src/components/layout/{AppShell,ProfileMenu}.tsx` plus a new `StreakBadge.tsx`.

**D102. The Collection sidebar tree now drills all the way down to individual decks, not just Collection nodes — a genuinely new capability, not a restyle.** Previously `CollectionNav.tsx` only rendered Collections (decks-with-children); a leaf deck was only ever reachable from the main content list. Confirmed via the mockup's zoomed sidebar (`sidebar_zoom.png`, pixel-inspected) that nested items use a small dot indicator (filled accent when active) rather than a distinct icon, while Collections get a `Folder` icon and "All Decks"/"Unfiled Decks" get `Layers`/`Inbox`. `NavDeck` (`CollectionNav.tsx`) gained `name`/`cardCount` fields; a new `DeckLeafRow` renders each leaf deck nested under its owning Collection (or under "Unfiled Decks", now independently expandable via its own chevron, previously just a flat scope-selecting button with no drill-down). Clicking a nested deck row navigates straight to `/decks/:id` (via `useNavigate` inside `CollectionNav`, matching `DeckRow.tsx`'s own convention) — a materially different action from clicking a Collection row (which only re-scopes the main content list, unchanged).

**D103. `LibraryDeckPage` now renders inside `LibraryShell` (the sidebar), which it never did before.** This was necessary for D102 to be visible from the Deck page at all (previously the Deck page had no sidebar whatsoever) and is also what makes the sidebar's own empty space — where the new Tip panel (below) now lives — exist in that context in the first place. Clicking a Collection label while on a Deck page navigates to `/decks?collection=...` (reusing the existing `selectionToSearchParams` round-trip already used by the breadcrumb) rather than filtering in place, since the Deck page has no "browse this collection's decks" content area of its own; the `selection` passed to `CollectionNav` here is an inert sentinel (`{kind:'collection', id:'__none__'}`) so no row falsely shows as "selected" — only the current deck's own leaf row highlights, via a new `activeDeckId` prop threaded through `CollectionNav`/`CollectionNavDrawer`/`LibraryShell`.

**D104. Fixed a real, pre-existing bug found while wiring `LibraryDeckPage`'s metrics for the sidebar's per-deck counts: the Deck page's own `metrics.dueCount` (the "due today" stat) was always 0.** `computeDeckMetrics(allCards, dueCards, v2CountByDeck)` was being called with a hardcoded `[]` for `dueCards` (unlike `LibraryBrowserPage`, which correctly passes real `useDueCards()` data) — `dueCount` is deliberately not derived from anything else, so this silently zeroed it. Added the same `useDueCards({now})` call `LibraryBrowserPage` already had. Confirmed via `git stash` that this predates this session's changes, not introduced by it.

**D105. The big deck-header mark now shows the owning Collection's name (e.g. "C++"), not the leaf deck's own name — resolving what first looked like a data-model gap.** The mockup's large square icon reads "C++" beside an h1 of "Declarations and Definitions" (a *different* string), which only makes sense as the parent Collection's mark, not `markLabelFor(deck.name)`. (`deck.language` was briefly considered as the source for this and rejected once actually read — `src/domain/decks/languages.ts` is a spoken/content language for card text, e.g. "English"/"Serbian", entirely unrelated to a subject like "C++"; using it here would have been a fabricated meaning for an existing field.) `markLabelFor` gained an optional `maxLen` parameter (default 2, unchanged for every existing small-mark call site) so the large header mark can pass `3` — enough for short acronym-like Collection names ("C++", "SQL", "STL") to show in full instead of being clipped to 2 characters.

**D106. The Deck page's action buttons were restructured to match the mockup's two-tier grouping** (a top row of quiet actions, a stacked primary/secondary pair below) rather than one wrapped row of four equal-weight buttons. "Flip through" moved into a new overflow (`⋯`) menu (`OverflowMenu`, already used by `DeckRow`/`CardRowV2`) alongside "Deck settings"; "Study Now" (solid, full-width of its column) and "Add Card" (secondary, same width) stack below it, in that order, matching the mockup. Cards/due/last-studied stats each gained a small leading icon (`Layers`/`CalendarClock`/`Clock`); mastery keeps its existing ring.

**D107. Added a Cards/Insights tab split to the Deck page — Insights is new, built entirely from data the Cards tab already computes, not a new metric.** Two small breakdowns (status: new/learning/review/relearning/suspended; type: per-interaction-type count across both v1 and v2 cards), each rendered with the existing `MeterBar` primitive. No new domain concept, no new query.

**D108. The Cards tab gained a combined search/type/status/sort toolbar and pagination (page size 10) spanning both v1 `Card` and `CardV2Record` cards in one list — the two are genuinely different types/components (`CardRow` vs `CardRowV2`), unified only at the metadata level.** A `RowMeta` shape (`id`, `kind: 'v1'|'v2'`, `title`, `typeLabel`, `state`, `suspended`, `due`) is computed for every card regardless of origin (`cardTypeMeta[type].label` for v1, `INTERACTION_META[type].label` for v2), and search/type/status/sort/pagination all operate on that combined array; rendering still dispatches to the real `CardRow`/`CardRowV2` per id via a lookup map. **Pagination and the existing v1 drag-to-reorder feature were resolved, not left in conflict**: dragging only ever needs to work in the default view (no search/filter, `Sort: Manual`), so pagination and sorting are only active (`paginating`) when a filter/search is set or a non-manual sort is chosen — in that default manual+unfiltered state, the page falls back to the exact pre-existing DnD code path unchanged, full list, no page-size cap. Status filter options are New/Learning/Review/Relearning/Suspended, plus "All" meaning "any non-suspended state" (not literally everything) — chosen so the toolbar's default (`All`) reproduces the old default-hidden-suspended behavior instead of silently changing it once the toolbar shipped.

**D109. Added `LibraryTip.tsx`, reusing the review flashcard Tip's visual language (icon square + header + text), placed in the sidebar below the Collection tree** — product feedback explicitly asked for this instead of a page-footer bar (which doesn't exist in the real app today; `library-use.png`'s footer tip was mockup-only). Content is one fixed, real, generally-useful hint (the actual Space/1–4 Review shortcuts, cross-checked against `ReviewSessionScreen`'s own shortcut-hint text) rather than the mockup's "View keyboard shortcuts" link, since no keyboard-shortcuts page/modal exists anywhere in this app to link to — inventing one was out of scope for a Library-page pass.

**D110. Added a day-streak indicator (`StreakBadge.tsx`) to the top nav, and a chevron to `ProfileMenu`'s avatar button — both are visual-only additions, one of them explicitly not backed by real data.** The streak number is a static illustrative "7", deliberately matching Today's `MomentumPanel` ("7-day streak," already commented there as placeholder — no day-over-day review-log streak aggregation exists anywhere in the domain layer). A real computation was considered (`useReviewLogs()` exists and fetches all logs) and rejected for this pass: it would mean a global, always-on, full-history query mounted on every route just for a decorative nav element, a poor cost/value trade for what product feedback described as wanting "the fire icon and day streak" visually. `ProfileMenu`'s trigger button gained a `ChevronDown` next to the existing avatar circle (still one button, same `aria-label="Profile"`, so no test needed to change for this part) to visually signal it opens a menu — it already did structurally (`aria-haspopup`/`aria-expanded`), the affordance was just not visible before.

Two tests needed updating as a direct, correct consequence of D102 (not a regression): `LibraryBrowserPage.test.tsx` asserted `findByText('Type Deduction')` (singular), which now legitimately matches twice (once in the sidebar tree, once in the main content row) — changed to `findAllByText(...)` + `toHaveLength(2)`, asserting the new duplication explicitly rather than loosening the check.

All 364 tests pass; `tsc -b` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified end-to-end in a real running instance via a persistent-profile Playwright session (so seeded Dexie data survived across separate script invocations): created a nested Collection (C++ containing Fundamentals/Templates) and three cards across two interaction types, confirmed the sidebar tree/icons, the deck header (icon, stats, restructured actions, overflow menu), the Cards/Insights tabs, and the toolbar's sort transitioning cleanly from the default draggable view to the sorted/paginated one.

## 2026-07-28 — Library: opening a Collection (a Deck-with-children) now renders a matching identity view instead of the generic browser

Bug report: opening a leaf deck ("focused Deck page," `LibraryDeckPage.tsx`) matched the target visual system, but opening a top-level deck that has subdecks (e.g. "Arhitektura Računara") fell back to the plain, edge-to-edge `LibraryBrowserPage` composition — a bare "Library" title, a top-aligned `+ New Deck` button, and a flat admin-style deck table with column labels inside the bordered box — visually disconnected from the focused Deck page's centered identity-header/toolbar/list/footer rhythm. Root cause: `CollectionNav.tsx`'s Folder rows (`CollectionRow`) call `onSelect({kind:'collection', id})`, which only ever re-scopes `LibraryBrowserPage`'s existing "browse everything" composition — it never routed through anything that gave the selected Collection its own identity treatment.

**D111. Added `LibraryCollectionView.tsx`, rendered by `LibraryBrowserPage` when `selection.kind === 'collection'`, reusing the focused Deck page's shell primitives (`DeckMark`, the newly-extracted `shared/Stat.tsx`, `CardTableHeader`/`CardTableRowV1`/`CardTableRowV2`, `CardListFooter`, `RowFilterDropdown`, `DeckRow`/`DeckTableHeader`) instead of duplicating a second, near-identical composition.** The `selection.kind === 'all'`/`'unfiled'` root-browsing composition is unchanged in structure (still the plain "Library" page — there's no single selected entity to give an identity header to there), but its Sort control was also switched from a native `<select>` to `RowFilterDropdown` and its `DeckTableHeader` moved outside the bordered list box, for the same reason D108 already did this for the Cards table: column labels sitting inside the border read as a stray leftover, not deliberate. No Collection/Deck schema migration — this is presentation-only, exactly as before (`itera-decisions.md` D88); a "Collection" is still purely `Deck.parentId` derivation.

Specifics:
- **Identity header**: `DeckMark` (size `lg`, deliberately smaller than a leaf deck's `xl` — "modest," per feedback, and there's no single card-shaped entity being represented) showing the Collection's own initials, name, optional description, language badge, and four `Stat`s — child-deck count, total cards, total due, last studied (omitted entirely when there's no review history yet, not shown as "Never") — aggregated from the same per-deck `metrics` map `LibraryBrowserPage` already computes (`deckMetrics.ts` gained `aggregateMetrics`, a reduction over already-fetched data, not a new query). Mastery is deliberately not shown here — the spec driving this change explicitly listed only deck/card/due/last-studied as meaningful aggregate stats.
- **Actions**: an overflow menu (`Delete collection`, blocked with an alert if the Collection still has child decks or direct cards — mirrors the existing per-row deck-delete guard) plus a `Collection settings` toggle that opens `DeckSettings.tsx` unmodified (it already operates on a generic `Deck`, so it works verbatim for renaming/reparenting/language on a Collection node too). `+ New Deck` creates a deck with `parentId` set to the open Collection, nesting it directly — the root page's `+ New Deck` still creates a top-level deck, unchanged. No "Study Now" — the legacy scoped browser never had one either, and studying an entire Collection's subtree isn't a defined product behavior yet.
- **Decks section**: search/Due-only-filter/Sort toolbar (same visual language as the Deck page's Type/Status/Sort row) over the Collection's leaf-descendant decks (identical scoping logic to the pre-existing `getSubtreeCollectionIds` — intermediate sub-Collections are still flattened past, not shown as their own rows, exactly as the old composition already did; this was not a behavior this pass changed), paginated once a filter/search/non-manual sort is active (mirrors D108's `paginating` gate), rendered via the exact same `DeckRow`/`DeckTableHeader` components the root Library page uses.
- **Mixed legacy nodes** (a pre-Collection-model Deck that has both child Decks and cards of its own — never disallowed by the schema): surfaced as a second "Cards" section below Decks, using the same `CardTable*`/`CardListFooter` components the focused Deck page's Cards tab uses, labeled as legacy-compatibility rather than hidden. No pagination/search/sort was added to this sub-section (kept intentionally minimal — expected to be rare/nonexistent in practice) but suspend/delete/move mutations all work.
- **Deep-link consistency**: `LibraryDeckPage.tsx` (`/decks/:id`) now redirects (`<Navigate replace>`) to `/decks?collection=:id` whenever the resolved deck has children — closes the same bug for any entry point that still links straight to a deck id regardless of whether it's a leaf (`RoadmapCanvas.tsx`, old bookmarks), which previously rendered `LibraryDeckPage`'s "0 cards" leaf composition for a Collection instead of bouncing to the correct view.

Also extracted `LibraryDeckPage.tsx`'s local `Stat` and `collectionPath` into `shared/Stat.tsx` and `collectionTree.ts`'s exported `collectionPathFor`, and `LibraryBrowserPage.tsx`'s local `sortDecks`/`SortKey` into `shared/sortDecks.ts` (`DeckSortKey`), so both pages share one implementation instead of two copies drifting apart.

All 364 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit`, `tsc -b` (production build), and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified in a real running instance via headless Playwright: created a Parent Deck containing a Child Deck, confirmed the Collection view's header/stats/toolbar/list/footer match the focused Deck page's visual system, confirmed the leaf Deck page is unregressed, confirmed the delete-guard alert fires correctly for a non-empty Collection, and confirmed a direct `/decks/:id` navigation to the Collection's own id redirects to the Collection view with no console errors.

## 2026-07-28 — Progress page: `/progress` becomes the real Progress destination, replacing `/stats` in primary nav

Product brought a locked mockup (`progress.png`) for a new Progress page: a local sidebar (Overview/Decks/Activity/Review lag/Milestones/Achievements/Stats/Reports, grouped, plus an "Itera Pro" upsell), five KPI tiles, an activity heatmap, a retention-over-time chart, a deck-performance table, and a recent-milestones panel. An audit first established what's real: `ReviewLog` (via `repo.reviews`) is the only usable history source; `computeStats.ts` already had working streak/retention algorithms but nothing else in the mockup (sessions, accuracy, per-deck history, milestones) existed anywhere. Scope was resolved with the user before building (see below) rather than guessed.

**D112. `/progress` is a new route (`src/features/progress/ProgressPage.tsx`) added as a sibling of the existing `stats` child route in `router.tsx`; `primaryNavLinks.ts`'s "Progress" entry now points at `/progress` instead of `/stats`.** `StatsPage.tsx` and the `stats` route are completely untouched and stay mounted — a direct-URL safety net, not deleted, per the task's explicit "preserve `/stats` safely" constraint. `TopNav.test.tsx`'s active-link assertion was updated to render at `/progress` instead of `/stats` as a direct, correct consequence, not a regression.

**D113. Only "Overview" is a live sidebar destination; the other 7 mockup rows (Decks/Activity/Review lag/Milestones/Achievements/Stats/Reports) render as visibly disabled rows with a "Soon" pill, and the mockup's "Itera Pro" upsell card was omitted entirely.** Decided with the user: building the full sidebar composition/hierarchy (matching the locked mockup) without fabricating pages that have no real data source yet, and no paid tier exists anywhere in this product to honestly back an upgrade CTA. `ProgressNav.tsx` mirrors `CollectionNav.tsx`'s structure (grouped headers, a `Settings` link pinned to the bottom via `mt-auto`); `ProgressShell.tsx` mirrors `LibraryShell.tsx`'s local-sidebar-inside-workspace layout (own `useIsWideProgress.ts` hook, a deliberate copy of `useIsWideLibrary.ts` rather than a shared import, matching this codebase's existing feature-independence convention for library/design-preview).

**D114. New pure domain layer, `src/domain/stats/{dateRange,progressMetrics}.ts`, additive — `computeStats.ts` (legacy `/stats`) was not touched or refactored.** `dateRange.ts` has the preset/range/comparison-period/label-formatting logic; `progressMetrics.ts` has `clusterSessions`, `computeKpis`, `computeHeatmap`, `computeRetentionSeries`, `computeDeckPerformance`, and `deriveMilestones` — each pure and colocated with a `.test.ts`. Every function takes real `ReviewLog`/`Card`/`Deck` arrays; nothing is fabricated or hardcoded.

**D115. Two metrics needed new, non-obvious definitions since neither existed in the codebase before this page. "Total sessions" is reconstructed by gap-clustering `ReviewLog.reviewedAt` (>30min idle = new session)** — decided with the user as the approach, since no session boundary is persisted anywhere (`StudySession`/`ReviewEvent.sessionId` in `cardV2.ts` are unused spec types) and this task couldn't add schema. **"Avg. accuracy" is new: the "not Again" rate over *all* reviews in range (any card state), deliberately broader than "Retention rate," which keeps `computeStats.ts`'s existing definition (mature review/relearning-state reviews only) just re-parametrized to an arbitrary window instead of a fixed trailing 30 days** — accuracy reads higher than retention because early-stage (new/learning) reviews are easier to answer correctly than mature recall checks.

**D116. "Recent milestones" is entirely derived from `ReviewLog` history, not a persisted entity** — decided with the user. `deriveMilestones` scans for the first date three threshold families are crossed: cumulative review count (100/500/1000/2500/5000/10000), streak length (3/7/14/30/60/100 days), and trailing-30-day retention (70/80/90%). Session-count milestones were deliberately skipped — stacking a derived threshold on top of an already-heuristic session count wasn't worth the extra surface for this pass.

**D117. Every chart is hand-rolled SVG/CSS, matching this codebase's existing "no charting library" convention** (`PaceChart.tsx`, `MasteryRing.tsx`) — no charting dependency was added. `RetentionChart.tsx` follows `PaceChart.tsx`'s gradient-line-plus-dashed-baseline structure; `ActivityHeatmap.tsx` is a Mon–Sun/week-column grid with a 5-level intensity scale; `Sparkline.tsx` is a small shared stroked-polyline component used by both the Total Sessions KPI tile and the deck-performance table's trend cell. **Colors deliberately use Itera's existing locked navy/orange/success/warning tokens (`--itera-accent`, `--itera-success`, `--itera-warning`, `--itera-navy`), not the mockup's blue/purple hues** — the mockup was treated as the reference for composition/hierarchy/spacing/density/typography (as the task specified), not for introducing a second color system alongside the app's already-locked one.

No Dexie/Supabase schema changed, no new entities. `docs/itera-redesign-plan.md` and `docs/features.md` were updated in the same pass to reflect `/progress` as the real Progress destination.

All 387 tests pass (364 pre-existing + `TopNav.test.tsx`'s route fix, plus 23 new tests across `dateRange.test.ts`/`progressMetrics.test.ts`); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified in a real running instance via headless Playwright with synthetic seed data (4 decks, ~1,080 review logs spanning 60 days, imported through Settings' existing JSON import): confirmed the empty-data state (no review history) before seeding, then the populated KPI row/heatmap (including the 7D/30D/3M/1Y toggle)/retention chart/deck-performance table/recent-milestones panel all render correctly with no console errors, and that `/stats` still renders unregressed at its old URL.

## 2026-08-11 — Matching review card: a connected two-column board (mockup `matching-card.png`)

Product brought a reference mockup (`itera-mockups/webapp/matching-card.png`): terms in a left column, values in a right column, each pair joined by a drawn line ending in a round badge — grey lines with a navy check while answering, success/error lines with a check or X once the answer is shown. Everything below is scoped to `src/features/reviewV2/interactions/matching/`; the response shape, `gradeMatching`, `isMatchingResponseReady`, and the card model are unchanged, so nothing about grading, scheduling, authoring, or persistence moved.

**D118. Matching now has two bodies, chosen by column count, behind one unchanged `MatchingView`.** A card with exactly one value column (the shape every legacy v1 non-`triple` matching card migrates to, and the mockup's case) renders `MatchingBoard.tsx`. A card with two or more value columns keeps the previous accordion, moved verbatim into `MatchingAccordion.tsx`. A board only reads as "connected" between two facing columns — three columns side by side would need lines from column 1 to column 3 running straight through column 2 — so the accordion stays as the honest 3+-column layout rather than being deleted or force-fit. `MatchingView.tsx` keeps the header, the flip surface, the submit button, and the result banner for both.

**D119. Connectors are measured geometry (an absolutely-positioned SVG of cubic paths), not a CSS or layout trick, because the right column is presented shuffled.** Its authored order mirrors the relationship rows, so presenting it unshuffled hands over the answer; that means a pair is usually *not* two facing neighbours and the line has to be drawn between two arbitrary rows. Positions come from an `offsetLeft`/`offsetTop` parent chain rather than `getBoundingClientRect`, deliberately: this renders inside `FlipCard`'s rotated container, where client rects are foreshortened mid-flip while offsets stay in layout space. Measurement re-runs on every render (bailing out via an equality check unless geometry actually moved) plus a `ResizeObserver`, so wrapping text, a revealed "Should be …" line, or a late font swap can't leave a line pointing at a stale position. Where there is no layout at all — the `happy-dom` test environment, where every offset reads 0 — no edges are produced and the board renders as two plain lists, which is why the component tests assert on accessible names rather than on drawn lines.

**D120. The badge sits at ~30% along its line (near the term end), not at the midpoint the mockup shows.** Every connector spans the same gutter, so two lines share a midpoint whenever their endpoint pairs sum to the same y — which is exactly what a crossing (rotated) set of pairings produces, and it stacked all three badges on one point in the first implementation (caught in a headless screenshot, not by a test). Anchoring near the term instead gives every row exactly one badge at its own height, which is also where the post-grading "Should be …" line appears. The mockup's centered look still holds whenever the lines happen to run straight across; this is a deliberate, documented deviation for the crossing case.

**D121. The right column's presentation order is owned by `MatchingView`, not by `MatchingBoard`.** `FlipCard` mounts front and back as separate positions in the React tree, so a board that shuffled internally shuffles *again* on reveal and shows the learner a different layout than the one they answered on (reproduced in a screenshot before the fix). `MatchingView` survives the flip, so the `useMemo`d order lives there and is passed to both faces. Fixed columns (a shared option list) are left in authored order — that order carries no answer information.

**D122. On the board, pairing works from either side and a claimed value can be taken, which is a deliberate divergence from the accordion.** Pick a term then a value, or a value then a term; re-picking a term's current value unpairs it; tapping a connected value with nothing selected detaches it. Assigning a value another term already holds *moves* it (the unique-column invariant still holds — the previous holder is cleared) instead of the accordion's "claimed values are disabled and marked (used)". The accordion disables them because there you cannot see what you would be taking; on the board the existing connection is drawn on screen, so a click that silently does nothing would be the worse behavior. Pairing state is still stated in text for assistive tech via each item's accessible name (`"const, paired with cannot be modified"` / `"…, not paired"`), and after grading every wrong row states its correct value in text under the term — the drawn line and badge shape carry the state visually, never hue alone.

**D123. The board is two columns at every width, which supersedes the mobile claim in the 2026-07-27 Matching-editor entry above** ("`MatchingView` renders as a single fluid accordion list at every viewport width — it never lays out columns side by side", offered there as this codebase's answer to spec §20.2/§29.4's "stepwise pairing flow or stacked assignment controls" on mobile). That remains true for 3+-column cards; a two-column card now does lay out side by side on a phone. Checked live at 390px rather than assumed: the columns hold, values wrap to two or three lines, and the connectors and badges still land correctly, so it is usable — but it is a narrower layout than the spec's mobile wording, and a width-triggered fall back to the accordion was deliberately **not** built, since it wasn't asked for and would swap the layout out from under a learner mid-answer. Flagged here as a known, revisitable gap.

`MatchingView.test.tsx` was rewritten around the board (keyboard-only pairing, value-first pairing, unpair, steal, submit gating, graded feedback) and keeps two tests for the accordion path against a three-column card. All 391 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified visually in a real headless-browser run (front, partially-answered, and graded faces) against a temporarily two-columned preview fixture — `/design-preview/review/matching`'s fixture itself is deliberately three-part (see the fixture's own comment) and was left alone, so that preview route still shows the accordion, not the board.

## 2026-08-11 — Matching, same day: the board generalizes to three columns, capped there, with shared many-to-one values

Follow-up product decisions on the entry directly above, taken after seeing it run. Two asks: don't leave 3-column cards on the old layout, and make sure a value can serve more than one term.

**D124. Supersedes D118's split.** There is no longer a per-shape body choice: `MatchingBoard` renders every Matching card, and `MatchingAccordion.tsx` (created in D118 an hour earlier, itself the old `MatchingView` body moved verbatim) is deleted rather than left unrouted — it was a fallback for a case that no longer falls back. A relationship across three columns is drawn as a **chain**: term → its value in column 2 → its value in column 3, one hop per gutter. That is what a relationship row literally is (a tuple across the columns), and it keeps one visual language for every Matching card. D118's reasoning for *not* doing this still stands for the layout it was rejecting — every column connecting back to the term can't be drawn, since those lines cross straight through the intervening column's cards — the chain sidesteps it by connecting each column to the previous one instead. A hop whose preceding column is unset starts from the term, so a half-filled row stays legible. Column labels render as headers once a card has more than two columns (they were the only thing the accordion showed that the board didn't); a two-column card still has none, matching the mockup.

**D125. Three columns is now a hard cap (`MAX_MATCHING_VALUE_COLUMNS = 2` value columns), not just a layout preference.** Product call: settle the shape at three and build everything around it. Enforced in the pure form layer (`addMatchingColumn` returns the form untouched at the cap, `validateMatchingForm` rejects anything over it so pre-cap data can't be saved forward), with the editor's "Add column" button replaced by a plain cap notice at three. Nothing existing is stranded: a legacy v1 `triple` matching card migrates to exactly three columns, which is the cap, not past it.

**D126. "Several terms connect to one value" is the existing `fixed` column, surfaced properly rather than modelled again.** A shared column already stores one option list for the whole card and grades by item id, so any number of terms may land on the same value and nothing is taken from anyone — the board draws that as converging lines, verified live with three terms sharing one value. What was missing was that this is *findable* and *legible*: the editor toggle now reads "One shared list of values (several terms can connect to the same value)" instead of "Pick from a fixed list (shared across rows)", and a value's accessible name lists **every** term connected to it (`"Yes, paired with std::vector, std::list"`), not just the first. Unshared columns keep one-to-one semantics, where assigning a claimed value moves it (D122).

**D127. With three columns, tapping a connected value means different things in the middle and the last column.** In the last column it detaches the pairing (D122's undo affordance, and the only one that doesn't require re-picking the term). In a middle column it is instead the anchor for the next hop, so it becomes the pending pick — which is how you fill column 3 without naming the term again. Found by a test, not by reasoning: the chain test failed because the middle value detached itself instead of continuing the chain. A shared column is never a detach target either way, since its value can belong to several terms and there'd be no saying which one to drop.

All 395 tests pass (`MatchingView.test.tsx` gained three-column tests for chain continuation, per-column accessible state, and one shared value serving several terms; `matchingForm.test.ts` and `MatchingFields.test.tsx` gained cap tests); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified visually in a headless run of `/design-preview/review/matching`'s real three-column fixture — front and graded faces, including three lines converging on one shared value and a single wrong hop reading as a red X plus "O(1) random access?: Should be No" — and the two-column board re-checked afterwards for regressions. The mobile caveat in D123 applies more strongly at three columns.

## 2026-08-11 — Matching, third pass: /preview was rendering a second, untouched Matching renderer

Product report: "it's literally the same accordion style one." Investigated before changing anything, by importing a three-column matching card and screenshotting every screen that renders one. The board was fine on `/review`; the screen being looked at was `/preview`.

**D128. `/preview` (`src/features/preview/PreviewPage.tsx`) now renders the real v2 card — `migrateCard` + `ReviewSessionScreen` with `hideTopBar`/`hideRating` — instead of the v1 `CardView`/`getCardDefinition` registry.** This was the actual complaint: the app had *two* independent Matching renderers, and this page kept using `src/features/cards/renderers/matching/Question.tsx` (rows of `Choose…` dropdowns, old panel styling, green type badge) while `/review` used the redesigned board. Nothing about the redesign could ever show up here. The same is now true of all eight v1 types, not just Matching — which is the point: `/review` (`ReviewSessionV2`) and `cards/:id/study` (`CardStudyPreviewPage`) already migrate-on-read into the shared shell, and this page was the last one that didn't. Deleted along with the switch: the page's own `FlipCard` branch, "Check answer"/"Show answer" button, auto-result banner, and `RevealToggle` — the shell owns reveal, submit, grading, tip, and explanation. Kept local: the back link, the tag row, the "Card N of M" jump input, prev/next, and left/right arrow keys (reveal/rating shortcuts belong to the shell's own listener). The v1 renderers stay in the tree and are still used by `ReviewSession.tsx` (unrouted) and `CardEditorPage`'s own preview.

**D129. An interaction type can now ask for a wider card column: `InteractionDefinition.widthFor?(interaction) -> 'default' | 'wide'`.** Matching returns `'wide'` for a three-column board, everything else leaves it unset and keeps D95's 42rem/`max-w-2xl` flashcard column. Three tracks plus two gutters inside 42rem left each column too narrow to hold a line of text — "for 2 columns it's okay, but for three it's bad" — so at three columns the card takes `max-w-4xl` (56rem) and values stop wrapping three deep. Implemented as one optional registry field read by `ReviewSessionScreen`, not a Matching-specific branch in the shell, and not a change to the default width any other card sees. `PreviewPage`'s own column was widened to match so the wide variant isn't re-clamped by its container.

All 395 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified in a real browser against an imported three-column legacy v1 matching card: `/preview` now shows the board (wide, three columns on one line each, connectors and badges drawn), and the same card in `/review` is unchanged.

## 2026-08-11 — Library rows: preview-first click, portaled menus, and no more `window.*` dialogs

Three product reports against the Deck page's card table, all about the row rather than the card: clicking a row jumped straight into the editor, the kebab menu on lower rows opened somewhere far below the list (screenshot supplied), and destructive/creation actions used OS alert boxes.

**D112. Every popover in `OverflowMenu` and `CardTable`'s `MovePopover` now renders through a new `src/components/ui/FloatingPanel.tsx` — a portal into `document.body` positioned `fixed` against its trigger — instead of being an `absolute` child of the row.** The reported symptom had a specific cause: the card and deck tables sit inside an `overflow-x-auto` wrapper, and a box with `overflow-x: auto` computes `overflow-y: auto` too, so the menu was being clipped by that box and turning it into a vertical scroller rather than simply opening. Two more in-flow hazards were latent in the same place: a suspended row carries `opacity-55` and a dragging row a transform, both of which open a stacking context that a `z-20` inside the row cannot escape. Portaling sidesteps all three at once, and gives somewhere to put the behavior a fixed panel needs anyway: flip **above** the anchor when the panel does not fit below, clamp to 8px from every viewport edge, re-place on scroll (capture phase, so an ancestor scroller keeps the panel attached) and resize, close on outside `mousedown` or Escape. Verified in a real browser at 1400x800 (opens downward, fits) and forced into the flip case at 300px tall (anchor at y=230, panel drawn at y=88–226, i.e. above it).

`document.body` is outside `.itera-scope`, so each portal re-applies the `itera-scope` class on its own root and cancels that class's canvas `background` with an inline `background: transparent`. This is a real constraint on any future portal in this app, not a detail of these two: without the class the `itera-*` tokens resolve to nothing, and with the class but no override the overlay paints the page ground.

**D113. Clicking a card row opens the card in preview; Edit moved into the kebab menu.** Both row kinds previously linked to `/cards/:id/edit` from the title, with Preview demoted into the overflow menu (v1) or the row already opening the study preview (v2 — so the two row kinds actually disagreed with each other). Reading a card is the common case and it is non-destructive, so it is what a plain click should do; editing is now one deliberate step away. v1 rows go to `/preview?card=…`, v2 rows to `/cards/:id/study` — the two existing preview surfaces, unchanged. The `from` param is no longer the hardcoded `/decks` but the table's own current location (`useReturnTo()`), so the preview's back link returns to the deck or collection the user actually came from. This also settles `CardStudyPreviewPage`'s docstring claim ("the Deck-page row's primary click target"), which had stopped being true for v1 rows.

**D114. `window.confirm` / `window.prompt` / `window.alert` are replaced across the Library by a promise-based in-app dialog: `<DialogProvider>` (in `src/app/providers.tsx`) + `useDialogs()` in `src/components/ui/dialogs.tsx`.** The builtins are OS chrome — they ignore the design system entirely and, in an installed PWA, read as a browser security prompt rather than part of the app. The API is deliberately promise-shaped (`if (await dialogs.confirm({ title, description, danger }))`) so call sites stay as straight-line as the blocking builtins they replace; the only mechanical change at each site is `async`. Converted: `LibraryBrowserPage` (new deck / rename / delete / non-empty deck), `LibraryCollectionView` (the same four plus delete-collection), `LibraryDeckPage` (delete card, new deck), and `CardTable`'s v2 row (delete card). `Button` gained a `danger` variant (`bg-red`, which `.itera-scope` re-points to `--itera-error`) and `ComponentPropsWithRef` typing so the dialog can focus its confirm button.

Deliberately **not** converted in this pass: `SettingsPage`, `CardStateMigrationSection`, `RoadmapsPage`/`RoadmapEditorPage`, `CardEditorPage`, `BrowsePage`, and the unrouted `DecksPage`/`DeckDetailPage`. The provider is global, so those are a mechanical follow-up whenever each screen is next touched — the report was about the Library and widening it further would have buried the fix.

All 395 tests pass (`LibraryBrowserPage.test.tsx` now wraps its render in `DialogProvider`); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified end to end in a headless browser against imported seed data: the last row's menu opens attached to its own trigger, row click lands on `/preview?card=…&from=%2Fdecks%2Ffund`, the menu's Edit lands on `/cards/:id/edit`, and delete/new-deck render the in-app dialog — with a Playwright `dialog` listener armed to fail loudly if any native `window.*` prompt still fired. None did.

## 2026-08-11 — Ordering review card redesigned to the `ordering-card.png` mockup

**D130. `OrderingRow` is now a card-shaped row: no leading position number on the front face, a grip on the right edge, and the Move up/down buttons hidden until hover or focus.** The mockup's resting row is label + grip and nothing else, but the up/down buttons are the *only* required reordering path (drag is additive — see the module comment in `OrderingRow.tsx` for why dnd-kit's `KeyboardSensor` was rejected), so they could not simply be deleted. They stay in the DOM, in tab order, and hit-testable at all times, and are faded with `opacity-0` (never `hidden`/`invisible`/`display:none`) — `group-hover` and `group-focus-within` bring them back, so a keyboard reaching a row reveals its controls. A boundary control (Move up on row 1) fades from 0 to 30% rather than sitting at 30% permanently, or it would be the one thing visible on an otherwise resting row.

The position number moved to the feedback face only. On the front it is redundant — the order *is* the answer, and it is already visible — but on the back it is what makes "Belongs at #3" readable against the row it labels. It stays a sibling of the label span, never a wrapper around it, so a row's visible text remains exactly the item's own text (the first attempt nested it and turned the row's text content into `"1.First"`, which `OrderingView.test.tsx`'s label assertions caught).

Also changed on this card, all presentational: rows go to `rounded-itera-card` with `px-5 py-4` and a 1px hairline shadow (from `rounded-itera-control`/`px-3 py-2.5`), row gap `space-y-2` → `space-y-3`, section gap `gap-4` → `gap-6`, the front subtitle takes the mockup's copy ("Drag items into the correct sequence.", `text-sm text-itera-muted-light`, replacing the smaller "Drag to reorder, or use the up/down controls." — the buttons still carry their own `aria-label`s), and Submit answer is centered with `px-8 py-3` instead of left-aligned. Graded rows now tint the whole border and fill (`border-itera-success/40 bg-itera-success-soft` / the error pair, matching `MatchingBoard`) instead of carrying a 4px left edge, which rendered as a crescent at the row's new 14px radius. Color is still never the only signal: every graded row also states "Correct" or "Belongs at #N".

**Untouched:** `gradeOrdering`, the response shape (`ID[]`), `isResponseReady`, the aria-live announcement, the card model, and `InteractionLabel`. The label is shared by all six types, and the mockups draw it orange (a soft-orange pill in `matching-card.png`, plain orange text here) rather than the current grey pill — deliberately left alone, since changing it is a six-card decision, not an Ordering one.

All 395 tests pass; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Verified visually in a headless run of `/design-preview/review/ordering` — front, hover (one row's chevrons revealed), keyboard focus (focus ring on the control, boundary sibling at 30%), and the graded face.

## 2026-08-11 — Account menu: the avatar opens a real anchored popover, and `/settings` becomes the Account settings page (mockups `profile-menu.png`, `profile.png`)

**D131. `ProfileMenu.tsx` is replaced by `AccountMenu`/`AccountMenuContent`, and the popover is built on the existing `FloatingPanel` — no popover dependency (Radix, Floating UI, Headless UI) was added.** The old menu was a 48px-wide `absolute` child of its trigger with two rows, predating the App Shell convergence: wrong visual language, no menu keyboard semantics, no focus return, and structurally the exact shape D112 already established as clip-prone (an `absolute` panel cannot escape an ancestor's `overflow`/stacking context). `FloatingPanel` already does portal-to-body, `fixed` placement against the trigger's rect, flip-above-when-it-does-not-fit, an 8px viewport clamp, capture-phase re-placement on scroll, and outside-click/Escape dismissal — everything the task's positioning requirements ask for. Verified in a real browser at 1536x1000: panel width exactly 300px, panel right edge at 1408.0px against a trigger right edge of 1408.0px, 4px below the trigger, `z-index: 60`, and `main`'s bounding box byte-identical before and after opening (no layout shift, because the panel is portaled and `fixed`).

**D132. Focus management is an opt-in prop on `FloatingPanel` (`manageFocus`, `returnFocusTo`), default off, rather than logic inside `AccountMenu`.** Putting it in the primitive means the next menu that needs real keyboard semantics inherits them, and it avoids nesting a wrapper `div` between `role="menu"` and its `role="menuitem"` children purely to hang a key handler on. Default-off keeps `OverflowMenu` and `CardTable`'s `MovePopover` — pointer-driven menus shipped without it — behaving exactly as before.

**The focus-in effect is gated on the panel having been *measured*, not merely mounted, and this was a real bug caught only in a browser.** `FloatingPanel` renders `visibility: hidden` for the frame between mount and measurement (it has to lay out before its own size can be read), and `HTMLElement.focus()` on a `visibility: hidden` element is a silent no-op. The first implementation focused on mount and passed every `happy-dom` test — `happy-dom` has no visibility semantics — while in Chromium focus never left the trigger and no arrow key did anything. The effect now depends on `pos !== null`. Worth remembering: **component tests in this repo cannot catch focus bugs that depend on real layout or visibility**, so anything focus-related needs a browser pass.

**D133. The popover deliberately does NOT reproduce `profile-menu.png`'s full list** (which also has Study settings, Spaced repetition (FSRS), Import / Export, What's new). Confirmed with the user before implementing. It is a quick-navigation menu: Account settings · Preferences — Keyboard shortcuts · Help & documentation — About Itera — Sign out. Every settings *subpage* belongs inside the Account settings page, or the popover becomes a second settings sidebar that has to be kept in sync with the first.

**D134. Everything that does not exist yet renders as a focusable `aria-disabled` row with a "Soon" pill, not a `disabled` button and not a hidden row.** `disabled` removes an element from the focus order, so a keyboard user would never discover a row they can plainly see; `aria-disabled` keeps it reachable and announced as dimmed. The "Soon" pill exists because greying alone communicates the state only through color. Account settings is the single live row; Sign out activates only when `isSupabaseConfigured && session` (local mode has no session to end), and it uses the existing `useAuth().signOut` — no new auth path. Sign out is styled with restrained orange (`text-itera-accent`), not the red `--itera-error` this design system reserves for destructive Delete actions, matching how the mockup draws it.

**D135. `/settings` is now `AccountSettingsPage` with its own `/settings/:section` sub-navigation; the pre-Itera `SettingsPage.tsx` was deleted rather than left unrouted.** Unlike `DecksPage.tsx` (kept pending parity re-confirmation), nothing was left behind to verify: its content moved wholesale. Export/Import JSON became `sections/ImportExportSection.tsx` (verbatim, except the Replace confirmation now goes through `useDialogs` per D114 instead of `window.confirm`), and the Phase D migration panel became `sections/CardSchedulingSection.tsx` wrapping the untouched `CardStateMigrationSection`. **These two sections stay fully functional** — the user's call, and the right one: greying out a working backup/restore path to match a mockup would be a regression. Every other section (Profile, Email & password, Appearance, Notifications, Privacy, Connected devices) is an inert placeholder that says what it will hold and why it is empty.

**D136. Billing and Plan & usage, both present in `profile.png`, were dropped entirely rather than added as placeholders.** There is no billing, subscription, or usage-quota concept anywhere in this product and none is planned; a settings page should not advertise one. Every remaining nav row is at least a real intended destination.

**D137. Statistics on the Profile section show em dashes, not numbers.** Real values exist (`/progress` computes them from `ReviewLog`), but wiring them into a section whose surrounding form is inert would make the page ambiguous about what works. The caption points at `/progress` instead.

**Identity is a greyed placeholder in local mode** ("Your name" / "Not signed in"), filling in the real Supabase email once a session exists — rather than hardcoding the mockup's sample person, which would put fabricated personal data in the product.

**Responsive:** below 480px (`useIsNarrowShell`, matching `useIsWideLibrary`'s shape) the identical `AccountMenuContent` renders in a bottom sheet, following `CollectionNavDrawer`'s overlay pattern turned to the bottom edge. Dismissed by the same rules as the popover; explicitly not a persistent mobile sidebar.

**Untouched:** `/review` (a top-level route with no `AppShell` ancestor, so it has no account menu by construction — re-verified: 0 account triggers, 0 nav links on the page), `AuthProvider`, `CardStateMigrationSection`, the backup domain layer, `OverflowMenu`, `CardTable`, and every `design-preview/*` route.

406 tests pass (395 before, +11 new across `AccountMenu.test.tsx` and `AccountSettingsPage.test.tsx`); `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` are clean. Browser-verified in Chromium: open/close, outside click, Escape, route-change close, Enter and Space to open, ArrowDown/ArrowUp/Home/End across items, focus return to the trigger on dismissal, the 390x844 bottom sheet, and the Account settings page after navigation.

## 2026-08-12 — `/login` exists, and the app has a real session boundary (mockup `login-v3.png`)

**D138. Local mode is gated now.** Confirmed with the user before implementing. Until this entry, `AuthGate` only ever rendered a login screen when `isSupabaseConfigured`; the default local-first mode had no session, no sign-out, and no `/login` route, which is why `AccountMenuContent`'s Sign out row was permanently greyed (D134). A fresh browser now lands on `/login` and must sign in or continue with the demo workspace. **No data is touched** by this — IndexedDB is where it always was; only reachability changed, so existing installs see the login screen once.

**D139. `src/auth/localSession.ts` is the single storage seam, and nothing else may open one.** One key (`itera.session`), `localStorage` when Remember me is checked and `sessionStorage` otherwise, every access in `try/catch` because storage throws outright in some privacy modes, and a corrupt value reads as signed out rather than crashing the shell. A write always clears the other store first: `readLocalSession` prefers `localStorage`, so without that a stale remembered session would silently shadow a newly-created un-remembered one (found while writing the test, not in review). The point of concentrating this is that "am I signed in?" has exactly one answer to change when a real local account system arrives.

**D140.
