The guard is one pathless layout route, not a per-page check.** `RequireAuth` (`src/auth/RequireAuth.tsx`) wraps the whole `AppShell` tree *and* `/review` in `router.tsx`; `/login` and `design-preview/*` are outside it — design-preview deliberately, since it is dev fixtures with no product data. `AuthGate` was narrowed to what it is actually needed for (blocking on the Supabase session bootstrap so a signed-in user is not bounced to `/login` on a refresh) and no longer decides what renders, because it wraps `RouterProvider` and therefore cannot redirect. The requested route is carried in `location.state.from`, so signing in returns there rather than always dumping the user on Today.

**D141. Supabase mode keeps magic-link; no password authentication was added.** Confirmed with the user. When `isSupabaseConfigured`, the password field, Remember me and the demo divider are hidden, the button reads "Send magic link", and success swaps in the check-your-email panel — the previous `LoginPage`'s behavior, moved into `SignInPanel`. The alternative considered and rejected was `signInWithPassword`: the visual design would be identical in both modes, but this project's Supabase users were created OTP-only, so it would have shipped a button that cannot work. `src/auth/LoginPage.tsx` was deleted outright (unlike `DecksPage.tsx`, kept pending parity) because its content moved wholesale.

**In local mode the password is a development shell and the code says so.** It is never stored, transmitted, or checked against anything; the only validation is "present, and the email is well-formed". This is stated in `SignInPanel.tsx`'s header rather than left for a reader to infer from the absence of a network call. Forgot password is a focusable `aria-disabled` button that reveals an explanatory line — the D134 honest-placeholder pattern — not a link to a route that does not exist, and no reset backend was built.

**D142. `AuthValue` grew rather than changed shape.** `session` still means "the Supabase session" and still returns `null` in local mode, so every existing consumer kept compiling; `isAuthenticated` and `identity` are the new things to read. A real Supabase session outranks a local one when both exist, since the cloud identity is whose data the app is actually reading. Account settings shows "Demo workspace — everything is stored in this browser." rather than the demo's synthetic `demo@itera.local`, which would read as a real account.

**D143. The illustration's overlap is constrained, not styled.** `LearningCardsIllustration` is three absolutely-positioned CSS cards, each with one literal `style.transform` rotation (the CLAUDE.md composed-transform gotcha, and it keeps the geometry readable in one place). The rule the numbers encode: **a card may never cover the next card's title.** That is tighter than the `left` values suggest, because a leaning card's right edge at its neighbour's title height sits further right than its own box by `sin(rotation) x` the vertical distance from its center — the first two passes both clipped "SQL Joins" and then "System Design" this way, visible only in a browser screenshot. Anyone widening a card or nudging one left has to re-check that in Chromium, not in the numbers.

**Deliberate deviations from the brief where it conflicted with the locked mockup**, all resolved toward the mockup: the panel heading is left-aligned (the brief said centered); the three principles are icon + label only, with no descriptive sub-copy (user's call); the principle icons are navy linework with no orange accent. Going the other way, the demo button carries a small `FlaskConical` icon the mockup does not draw, because the brief asked for one explicitly. The email placeholder is the mockup's `you@example.com`, not the brief's sample personal address. Sign-in redirects to `/`, which is this app's actual Today route; there is no `/today` path.

**Responsive:** below `lg` the two columns stack (brand block, then form) and the illustration drops out; below `md` the principles row drops out too. The form is not hoisted above the brand block on mobile — the logo and headline are compact, and a login page whose first pixel is a bare form field with the mark below it reads worse than the alternative. Verified at 390x844: no horizontal overflow.

447 tests pass (428 before, +19 across `localSession.test.ts`, `RequireAuth.test.tsx` and `LoginPage.test.tsx`); `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` are clean. Browser-verified in Chromium at 1440x900: `/`, `/decks` and `/review` all redirect to `/login` while signed out; `/design-preview` does not; demo workspace and credential sign-in both reach the app (the latter returning to the originally-requested `/decks`); `/login` while signed in redirects to Today; the account menu's Sign out is enabled and returns to `/login`, and a reload stays signed out; Remember me lands the session in `localStorage`; tab order runs email -> password -> show/hide -> Remember me -> Forgot password -> Sign in -> demo, the eye toggle has a 2px `#FF6902` focus ring and operates from the keyboard, and Enter submits.

## 2026-08-12 — Documentation reconciliation: an agent-neutral doc hierarchy, and product truth moved out of the Claude master spec

Context: the repository is now worked on by both Claude Code and OpenAI Codex. No product implementation work was done in this pass; only documentation, three archived files, and doc-path references inside source comments changed.

**D144. Product truth no longer lives in an agent-specific file.** `itera-claude-master-spec.md` was the Claude-addressed master specification, and it still held the only copy of a large amount of agent-neutral truth: the locked logo rules, the orange rule's full statement, typography rules, shape language, page widths, top-bar heights, the global-navigation blueprint, the Library sidebar and deck-row rules, the Review-shell "never add" list, the motion language and timing table, the accessibility checklist and Review shortcuts, and the responsive matrix. All of it was consolidated into `design-system.md` (visual/interaction), `architecture.md` (ontology, local-first, layering, scheduler boundary) and `features.md` (Tip vs Explanation, grading model, study sessions, deferred scope) before the spec was archived. The spec itself is kept **whole and unedited** under `docs/archive/` because `itera-decisions.md` cites it by section number throughout; editing it would break those citations and violate the append-only rule indirectly.

**D145. `CURRENT_STATE.md` is the entry point, and it is agent-neutral by construction.** Moved from `docs/itera/CURRENT_STATE.md` to `docs/CURRENT_STATE.md`. It owns implementation status, known problems and the single recommended next milestone; the reference docs own mechanism, rules and rationale respectively. The rule that follows: `architecture.md` describes how the system is structured, not what shipped last week, and status commentary belongs in `CURRENT_STATE.md`.

**D146. The redesign plan is history, not a plan.** `itera-redesign-plan.md` was archived. Reality had already deviated from its A-M sequence in ways the file itself records with strikethroughs (Phase H shipped without Phase G; Today shipped ahead of Phase I; Progress shipped without touching `computeStats.ts`), and keeping it labelled "authoritative, continuously updated" in the index meant two documents claimed to define what happens next. The still-open forward work it described is preserved: Phase G in `itera-migration-plan.md` sections 0 and 6, Phase D's remaining steps in section 4, and Phases K/L/M as the "Planned" rows in `features.md`. **The next milestone is now defined in exactly one place**, `CURRENT_STATE.md` section 17.

**D147. Archived documents may never override canonical ones.** Each archived file opens with a visible HISTORICAL warning naming what supersedes it. `docs/README.md` states the source-of-truth order explicitly (repository implementation, then `CURRENT_STATE.md`, then the canonical doc that owns the topic, then archive), and both `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` repeat it. Nothing was deleted in this pass.

**D148. No `docs/references/` directory was created.** The target structure suggested one, but no visual reference is tracked in git - the mockups live at `C:\Users\SK\Desktop\itera-mockups\`. Creating the directory would have meant inventing paths that resolve to nothing. Instead `design-system.md` section 14 documents the external location and introduces three explicit tiers for how a reference is treated: **LOCKED** (authoritative for composition, hierarchy, spacing, density, typography), **DIRECTION** (feel only, never structure) and **CONCEPT** (history only, never implement from one). The two standing exceptions were already settled and are restated there: colors always come from the Itera token palette, and a mockup element with no backing feature is omitted or rendered as an `aria-disabled` "Soon" row.

**D149. `ai-card-prompt.md` moved to `docs/prompts/` rather than being folded into a product doc.** It is an active, user-facing tool referenced from the root `README.md`, and it describes the v1 8-type backup schema - which is still correct, since `BACKUP_VERSION` is still `1`. It will need updating when that bumps.

**Corrections made against repository evidence**, each verified in the working tree rather than inferred: `AccountMenu`'s Sign out is live whenever any session exists, not only a Supabase one (`architecture.md` still said the latter); a `CardV2`-backed store does exist, so the claim that "v2's storage model isn't live" was wrong - the accurate statement is that v2 records are absent from the **due queue**; `CardRowV2` is rendered only by the unrouted `DeckDetailPage`, not by production's deck page, which uses `CardTable`; `useCardsV2` exports six `useSave*Card` hooks, not two; the migration plan's claim that "no migration files exist yet" was false (`supabase/migrations/0001_card_states.sql`), and the same check surfaced a genuine gap - **`cards_v2` is declared in `schema.sql` with no matching migration file**, so a project migrated file-by-file would not have it.

**One measured contradiction left standing.** The 2026-08-12 login entry above records "447 tests pass"; a measured `npx vitest run` on this tree reports **63 files, 429 tests**. The decision log is append-only, so that entry was not edited; `CURRENT_STATE.md` section 16 now states the measured number and says explicitly that it supersedes the logged one.

Verification for this pass: `npx vitest run` 429/429 passing, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run lint` clean except the pre-existing `.scratch-shot.cjs` warning. Only doc-path strings inside comments changed in `src/` (`router.tsx`, `reviewService.ts`, `PreviewShell.tsx`, `fixtures.ts`, `TodayPage.tsx`, `index.css`, `types/cardV2.ts`, `types/index.ts`); no behavior, routes, schema, persistence or UI changed.

This is structural layout, not a viewport-unit breakout, so D157's scrollbar fix remains intact.

**Revealed guidance describes the available action rather than restating keys.** A feedback state with rating controls now says **Rate your answer**. A non-committing preview with `hideRating` says **Answer revealed**. The former `1–4 to rate` keycap/hint is removed from the strip; the four rating buttons still carry their own visible numbers and keyboard behavior.

Verification: Chromium at 1440x900 measured both the navbar and Review-strip outer boxes at 1440px, both inner frames at x=80/right=1360, Exit and the Itera logo at x=80, and the revealed hint/profile control at right=1360. ## 2026-08-15 — Matching connection badges return to the visual midpoint

**D161 (supersedes D120). A Matching check/X badge belongs at the midpoint of its own connector.** A connection from row 1 to row 3 therefore places its badge horizontally between the two columns and vertically on row 2, matching `matching-card.png`, instead of anchoring every badge 30% from the source item. This applies to every hop of a two- or three-column board and to both answering and graded states.

Crossing connections can still share the same mathematical midpoint. `placeMatchingBadges` groups those collisions and tries the nearest symmetric position along the involved cubic curves until all 24px badges retain 4px of clearance. The group shares one curve parameter so the result stays visually balanced, every badge remains on its own line, and no screen-space nudge disconnects a marker from the relationship it describes. Non-colliding connections remain at the exact 50% midpoint.

Verification: Chromium at 1440x900 measured an isolated row-1-to-row-3 badge at the exact connector midpoint (x 536, y 431, aligned with row 2). A pair of crossing connections that previously shared one midpoint rendered 31.5px apart, clearing two 24px badges; the 390x844 check remained overflow-free. `npx vitest run` passed 441/441 across 65 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean, and `npm run build` completed with only the existing chunk-size advisory.

## 2026-08-15 — Walkthrough guidance becomes step-aware; code rendering stays crisp

**D166. Walkthrough has two additive guidance scopes.** `CardV2.tip` and `CardV2.explanation` remain the walkthrough-wide fields: the tip remains available while the sequence is unfinished, and the explanation appears after the final step before the single card-level rating. Each `WalkthroughStep` may now additionally carry its own optional `tip` and `explanation`. The active step shows its tip only before its first submission, then swaps to its explanation; revisiting an answered step shows the explanation. This preserves the locked semantic distinction between pre-answer help and post-answer context at both scopes without changing Walkthrough's one-card, one-rating phase model.

The step fields are optional properties inside the existing opaque JSON blob, so no Dexie version, Supabase migration, backup-version bump or persisted-row rewrite is required. Older v2 Walkthrough records and lazily migrated v1 Story cards omit them and hydrate into blank authoring fields. The editor labels the existing fields **Overall tip/explanation** and adds **Tip/Explanation for this step** to every step card, making the scope explicit.

**Code-backed Walkthrough cards use stable entrance geometry.** Chromium reproduction showed that `ReviewSessionScreen`'s 500ms ancestor `scale(0.97) rotate(-2.5deg)` temporarily rasterized CodeMirror's monospace glyphs off-pixel; waiting for the transform or scrolling to force repaint made them sharp. Walkthrough cards with shared code therefore retain the entrance opacity fade but skip the scale/rotation. `CodeView` and `CodeEditor` also select the actually registered self-hosted `JetBrains Mono Variable` family first and call `requestMeasure()` after `document.fonts.ready`, so a late font swap cannot leave stale line wrapping until a scroll or edit. Other interaction entrance motion is unchanged.

Verification: Chromium at 1440x900 observed `transform: none` while the code-backed Walkthrough entrance opacity was still in flight, the intended variable font stack, correct line 2 highlighting on step 1 and lines 3–4 on step 2, the step tip disappearing after submission, and the corresponding step explanation appearing. At 390x844 the step guidance stacked cleanly and `scrollWidth === clientWidth` (390/390). The browser console was clean. `npx vitest run` passed 444/444 across 65 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean, and `npm run build` completed with only the existing chunk-size advisory.

## 2026-08-16 — The card editor's action header is pinned, not just the interaction chooser

**D167 (completes D81's sticky treatment). `CardEditorShell`'s header — Preview card / ← Back to deck / Save — is `position: sticky`, so the whole action group stays reachable from anywhere in a long authoring form.** Product feedback: D81 pinned "Choose interaction" but left Save at the top of the document, so committing a card meant scrolling back up past the entire editor. The header keeps its D85 role as the shell's one aligned action anchor; only its position behavior changed.

**The offset is measured, not hardcoded.** Two sibling sticky elements do not auto-stack — each resolves `top` against the scrollport independently (the same trap D81 recorded) — so the header has to know the chooser's height. `CardCreatePage` observes its own sticky wrapper with a `ResizeObserver` and publishes the height as a custom property; the header's `top` is `var(…, 0px)`. Measured rather than hardcoded because the chooser's grid reflows from six columns to two (180px wide, 344px at 390px), and the D81 entry's own 77px constant is exactly the kind of figure that silently rots. The property name is exported from `CardEditorShell` as `STICKY_TOP_VAR` and imported by the page, so publisher and consumer cannot drift on a string; the `0px` fallback is what the `/cards/:id/edit` route uses, where no chooser exists and the header pins to the scrollport top.

**Two layout consequences, both deliberate.** The header carries `bg-bg` plus a `-mx-2 px-2` bleed so editor content scrolling beneath it is covered a couple of pixels past the column's own border, and `z-[4]` keeps it under the chooser's `z-[5]`. Separately, **the header no longer stacks title-over-actions below `sm:`** — it is one `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto]` row at every width, with the title and subtitle truncating. A wrapped second row would have cost 48px of permanently occupied viewport on a phone, on top of the chooser's 344px; single-row brings the combined sticky region back from 456px to 408px of an 844px viewport.

No component test can observe any of this — happy-dom has no layout and discards `top: var(…)` outright (D132). `CardCreatePage.test.tsx` guards only the half that is observable: that the page still measures its chooser and publishes a px offset, since deleting that wiring degrades silently to an overlapping header.

Verification: Chromium drove the real create flow at 1440x900 and 390x844. The header's computed `top` matched the live chooser height exactly (180px / 344px) and its bounding box stayed pinned there at scrollY 784 / 1425; `Save card` was hit-tested to itself with the form filled and was really clicked from the scrolled position, saving the card and navigating to the deck. `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` throughout, including mid-transition with the preview drawer open (where the header widened to 1132px in step with the shell, per D85). The `/cards/:id/edit` route resolved `top: 0px` from the fallback. The browser console was clean in every run. `npx vitest run` passed 445/445 across 65 files, `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean.

## 2026-08-16 — The Today hero's card stack builds itself on load

**D170 (refines the mount reveal only; the pixel-tuned resting composition is untouched).** `SuggestedSessionHero.tsx`'s four layers previously faded in on a 0/50/90/140ms stagger while sitting in their final positions, which read as an appear, not as a stack. They now visibly stack: each layer starts lifted 46px above the pile and scaled to 1.05, then settles onto its resting box back-to-front (#4, #3, #2, front card #1) over 460ms per layer on a 110/210/310ms stagger, with the design system's standard decelerating curve (§11).

**The lift is a transient prefix on each layer's literal `transform` string, not a new resting value.** Every layer's tuned `left/top/width/height/rotate/translate` is passed through unchanged and the lift is prepended (so it applies in the parent's coordinate space, before the layer's own rotation); the settled matrices measure exactly the pre-change ones. This keeps the reveal from becoming another place the approved geometry is encoded, and it keeps using the literal-transform-string mechanism the file already relies on for the reason recorded in §11's second mechanic.

**Below `sm` the stagger compresses, because the stack is only two layers deep there.** Cards #4 and #3 are `hidden sm:block`, so the full stagger spent its first two slots on nothing — measured as ~300ms of blank hero before the front card began appearing at 390px. A local `matchMedia('(min-width: 640px)')` hook switches the delays to `0 / 110` for the two layers that actually render. `settled` (which forces every layer's delay to 0ms and its duration to the shorter hover timing, so hover zooms all four in sync) moved from 480ms to 820ms to clear the longer sequence. Reduced motion is unchanged: the existing `prefers-reduced-motion` check still initializes the component already-mounted, so nothing animates.

Verification: Chromium sampled every layer's computed transform and opacity per frame from the hero's first paint. At 1440x900 the four layers started their drops at ~0/110/210/310ms from a `ty=-46` lift at scale ~1.05 and landed on the unchanged resting values (`ty=0` / `ty=0` at cos5° / `ty=-20` / `ty=-28`, all scale 1); mid-reveal screenshots show the front card semi-transparent and oversized above already-placed layers. At 390x844 the first drop began at ~50ms instead of ~290ms, with no horizontal overflow. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` all four layers read final values with opacity 1 on the first sampled frame. Once settled, all layers reported `transition-delay: 0s / duration: 0.2s` and hover moved all four to the same interpolation point. `npx vitest run` passed 445/445 across 65 files; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean.

Study settings and What's new have no backing surfaces, so they join Preferences, Keyboard shortcuts, Help & documentation and About Itera as focusable `aria-disabled` rows with visible **Soon** pills. `GraduationCap`, `TrendingUp`, `ArrowUpDown` and `Gift` provide the reference-like line icons without adding assets or dependencies. The popover gains a viewport-height cap and internal scrolling so the longer menu cannot escape a short desktop viewport; the existing bottom sheet already had its own height cap and scrolling.

Verification: Chromium at 1440×900 showed all five divider groups in a 300px panel, with the live and Soon states matching the list above; Import / Export navigated to the real section and route navigation closed the menu. At 390×844 the identical content rendered as a 524px-tall bottom sheet, fit without internal clipping or body overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`, 375/375), and retained the full Sign out row. `npx vitest run` passed 451/451 across 67 files; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean.

## 2026-08-17 — Progress iconography and KPI hierarchy converge on `progress.png`

**D177 (refines D112–D116). The locked Progress reference's icon concepts now drive the production page, while the Itera palette and truthful placeholder rules remain unchanged.** The sidebar uses server, rising-activity, scan, flag, trophy, column-chart and clipboard concepts instead of the earlier generic layer/heartbeat/clock/ribbon set. The KPI row uses scan, reviewed-card, refresh, filled-star and flame marks; light KPI badges use the existing navy/success/warning/accent soft tokens rather than copying the mockup's blue/purple hues. Panel titles regain the reference's compact info glyphs, Recent milestones uses divided rows, and Deck performance gets the reference's full-width centered footer.

Lucide remains the default icon source, but the streak flame is now one deliberate shared exception: `src/components/icons/StreakFlameIcon.tsx` uses a heavier outer flame and solid inner lick matching the mockup's silhouette. The top-nav streak, Current streak KPI, streak milestones, Today's Momentum and the Profile statistic all use that component, so the brand mark cannot drift between surfaces.

**KPI values are deliberately quieter than the mockup.** Product feedback found the initial 34px/700 treatment too aggressive, especially for values such as “0 days.” All five values now use 30px/500, while their uppercase labels drop from 700 to 600. This is a user-directed refinement of the locked reference rather than an accidental typography mismatch.

Verification: populated Progress data was imported only into a separate `localhost:5174` test origin. At an exact 1440px document width, the two dashboard rows remained paired as 472px panels, the 7D and 30D controls both updated their pressed state, the document had no horizontal overflow, and the browser console was clean. At 390×844 the complete populated page remained contained (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`, 390/390). `npx vitest run` passed 451/451 across 67 files; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` were clean.

## 2026-08-17 — The v1 legacy surface is deleted

**D178 (supersedes the preservation rationale recorded in `CURRENT_STATE.md` §15/§19). The pre-redesign pages are deleted rather than kept as a direct-URL safety net, on the product owner's explicit instruction.** This overrides `CLAUDE.md`'s standing "do not delete legacy code or routes" guard, which exists precisely so an agent would not do this incidentally. It was not incidental: it was asked for, after an audit established what would be lost.

The problem it fixes: the redesign had converged, but the retreat had not. `/browse`, `/cards/new`, `/drafts` and `/stats` stayed mounted and unlinked, so a bookmark reached a pre-redesign screen inside the new shell — and, because `.itera-scope` re-points the legacy semantic tokens, those screens looked roughly on-brand while keeping pre-redesign layout and density. That made the transition look more finished than it was. Two redesigned surfaces still linked *into* the dead ones: Review's "Nothing due" empty state offered **New card → `/cards/new`**, and `/preview`'s single-card back link defaulted to `/browse`.

Closure: **78 production files and 7 test files.** Beyond the four pages, that is the entire v1 card registry and all 8 renderer families, `CardRow`/`CardTypeBadge`/`CardView`, the unrouted `DashboardPage`, `decks/{DecksPage,DeckDetailPage}`, `review/{ReviewSession,GradeBar,useReviewSession}`, `components/ui/FlipCard.tsx`, `layout/ThemeToggle.tsx`, `code/{CodeBlockField,lineRanges}`, `grading/normalize.ts`, `stats/computeStats.ts`, and `cardsV2/{CardRowV2,shared/InteractionTypeBadge}`.

Four things were deliberately **kept**, and each would look deletable to a future reader: `cards/cardTypeMeta.ts` (live — `library/shared/rowVisuals.ts` reads its per-type icon/label/tile), `domain/search/searchableText.ts` (both repositories call it inside `cards.query`), `app/theme.tsx` including `Theme`'s `'dark'` member (`CodeView`/`CodeEditor` select the `oneDark` syntax palette from it), and **all drafts data** — `repo.drafts`, the Dexie store and the `drafts` array in backup import/export survive intact, with `src/hooks/useDrafts.ts` retained deliberately unused. Only the drafts *UI* was removed. Deleting a persisted entity as a side effect of a UI change is exactly what `CLAUDE.md`'s safety boundary forbids.

Six capabilities became unreachable and were **explicitly not reimplemented**, by direction: cross-deck full-text card search, tag filtering, mid-session undo, flip-back-to-question, the 7-day due forecast, and the drafts quick-capture inbox. They are recorded as redevelopment candidates in [`features.md`](features.md) against the redesigned surface that would host each, rather than against the deleted file. Recording them there rather than here is deliberate: this log says *why*, `features.md` owns *what is planned*.

Tests went 67 files / 451 tests → 60 / 409 → 62 / 415. The 42 removed tests covered the v1 renderers; `domain/grading/*.test.ts` already covers the v2 equivalents that production actually runs, so this is not a coverage loss. Six tests were added for the new behavior.

**D179. `CardEditEntry`'s fallthrough becomes a "Card not found" state, so `/cards/:id/edit` has no v1 escape hatch.** All 8 members of the v1 `CardType` union are handled by v2 editor-shell branches above it, so the final `return` was only ever reachable for an id resolving to neither model — it had been rendering the v1 registry editor for what is really a not-found condition. Establishing that is what made deleting the v1 editor safe.

**D180. Only the six `/design-preview/review/*` routes survive; the `/design-preview/library*` fork (18 files, 3 routes) is deleted.** The two halves were never equivalent, and that asymmetry is the whole argument. The review previews *import* the production `ReviewSessionScreen`, so they cannot drift from `/review` by construction. The Library previews were adapted-not-imported per `CLAUDE.md`'s "keep them decoupled" rule — and decoupling is exactly what let them rot: production had roughly doubled past them, gaining real data, `useDialogs`, the All-Decks reference composition, `RowFilterDropdown`, pagination, dnd-kit reorder, the Collection identity view and `DeckSettings`, while the fork's buttons stayed inert stubs and its private top nav (a copy of the deleted `TodayShell`) still labeled `/stats` as "Progress". `CLAUDE.md`'s decoupling rule now applies to the review previews only.

**D181. The app declares itself light-only, and the unreachable dark machinery is deleted.** `index.html` said `data-theme="dark"` and ran a pre-paint restore script, and `getInitialTheme()` fell back to `'dark'` — so `ThemeProvider` wrote dark back onto `<html>` on mount. With no dark palette this was invisible on every routed surface (all wrapped in `.itera-scope`) but real on the pre-router `AuthGate` screen, which renders outside the scope and reads `--bg` from `:root`. Both now say light. Deleted with them: the `dark` custom-variant (zero `dark:` utilities exist anywhere), the `[data-theme='dark']` token block (no selector could match it), the `.flip*` rules (their only consumer was the deleted v1 `FlipCard`), `--itera-orange` (duplicated `--itera-accent`'s literal, referenced nowhere), and five `@theme inline` mappings whose utilities had no consumers. Re-adding dark mode means re-authoring 16 tokens — cheap, and honest, where unreachable CSS claiming a capability was not.

**D182. The last four `window.confirm`/`prompt` call sites move to `useDialogs()`, and `RoadmapsPage` gains an `<h1>`. Cites D114 as precedent.** `window.confirm`/`prompt`/`alert` now appear nowhere in `src/`, so `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is enforceable by grep instead of by vigilance. The highest-value conversion was `CardStateMigrationSection`'s Apply gate: it guards a real migration write on a fully redesigned route, where an OS modal reads as a browser security prompt in the installed PWA. Its tests now drive the real dialog rather than stubbing a global, and the component was migrated off the legacy tokens it was the last redesigned surface to use. `RoadmapsPage` had no heading at all — it opened at a `<p>`, because the deleted topbar used to supply the title. **The full Roadmaps reskin remains deferred**; it is now the only reskinned-only surface left, recorded as such in `CURRENT_STATE.md` §14.

**D183. `RouteError` distinguishes a 404 from a crash.** It told every visitor "The app may have just updated. Reloading usually fixes it." — false advice for a removed route, where reloading only lands there again. With five bookmarkable routes just deleted, arriving from a stale link is an expected path rather than a failure, so an unmatched route renders "Page not found" with a link to Today, while genuine errors keep the reload affordance and the existing chunk-error reload-once recovery.

**A tooling correction that invalidates evidence cited in earlier entries.** `npx tsc --noEmit` — cited as passing in many entries above, including the one immediately preceding this heading — **checks nothing in this repository.** `tsconfig.json` is solution-style (`"files": []` plus `references`), so the command compiles zero files and is trivially clean. The real gate is `npx tsc -b --force`, which is what `npm run build` runs. Separately, `tsconfig.app.json` excludes `*.test.ts(x)`, so test files are never typechecked at all and a dangling import in one surfaces only as a Vitest resolve error — which is why the 7 doomed test files had to be deleted *before* the modules they covered. The earlier entries are left untouched because this log is append-only, but their typecheck claims should be read as unverified; `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `CURRENT_STATE.md` §16 have been corrected.

Verification: Chromium at 1440×900 and 390×844 against the real dev server. `/browse`, `/drafts`, `/stats`, `/cards/new` and `/design-preview/library` all render the new "Page not found" screen at both widths — not a blank page. `/`, `/decks`, `/progress`, `/roadmaps`, `/settings/card-scheduling` and `/preview` all render with no horizontal overflow at 390px (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`), and Roadmaps now reports `<h1>Roadmaps</h1>`. All six review previews still render. First paint is light at both widths (`data-theme="light"`, `html` background `rgb(244,245,248)`). Every converted dialog was driven end to end: roadmap create (Create disabled while the field is empty), rename (seeded with the current title, dismissed by Escape), delete (danger variant, row actually removed), and the migration Apply gate (Cancel wrote nothing). Zero console or page errors across the sweep. `npx vitest run` passed 415/415 across 62 files; `npx tsc -b --force`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` were clean, the build carrying only the pre-existing chunk-size advisory.

One caveat recorded rather than hidden: a single suite run during this work reported one failure that four consecutive re-runs could not reproduce and that the summary output did not name. It is an unidentified flake, not a known-failing test.

## 2026-08-18 — Review history (`/progress/history`)

The app wrote a complete `ReviewLog` on every grade but had no surface showing it: the only consumer was `/progress`, which aggregates logs into KPIs and charts. This adds the chronological per-review record behind those aggregates. The entity, its repo, both backends, the Dexie store and the backup array already existed, so no entity-addition checklist applied.

**D184. The page is "Review history" at `/progress/history`, and the "Activity" sidebar row is deliberately *not* consumed.** D113 established Activity as one of seven placeholder destinations; it never decided that Activity *means* review history. "Activity" reads as a broader feed (cards created, decks edited, imports, sessions), so taking that name for a per-review log would spend it for no reason. Review history is a new ninth row in the Learning group; Activity stays a "Soon" placeholder.

**D185. `ReviewLog` gains one optional field, `dueAfter`, and deliberately not `intervalDays`. Extends D7.** D7 committed to *extending* `ReviewLog` rather than restructuring it. The resulting interval was the one requested column with no backing data — only stability/difficulty before/after were logged — and it is genuinely unrecoverable for existing rows, so the field is optional and forward-only; older rows render an em dash rather than a fabricated number. A second field carrying FSRS's `scheduledDays` was considered and rejected: `fromCard` maps `scheduled_days` straight through and ts-fsrs sets `due = last_review + scheduled_days`, so `dueAfter - reviewedAt` recovers it, and `dueAfter` additionally covers learning steps, where `scheduledDays` is 0 but the card is due again in minutes. Browser-verified: a real Again grade logs a 10-minute interval that a day count would have rendered as "0d". "Maybe useful later" was not treated as sufficient reason to persist a second field. Populated at the single `buildReviewLog` chokepoint, so both write paths get it. No Dexie `version()` bump and no SQL migration: rows are opaque JSON blobs in both backends and the Supabase generated columns only project `cardId`/`reviewedAt`.

**D186. Deck attribution is a runtime join that must span *both* card stores — and this closed a live bug in Progress Overview.** `ReviewLog` has no `deckId` (per `architecture.md`'s "stats derived purely from logs, never denormalized"), so deck scoping joins `cardId -> deckId` at query time. `progressMetrics.ts`'s private `cardDeckMap` was built from v1 cards only, while saving a legacy card through a `save*Card.ts` module deletes its `cards` row and writes a `cardsV2` record under the same id — so every deck-scoped statistic silently dropped those cards' entire review history. Rather than write a correct resolver for history and leave Overview wrong, both now use `src/domain/stats/cardDeckIndex.ts`'s `buildCardDeckMap(cards, cardsV2)`; `computeRetentionSeries`/`computeDeckPerformance` take the prebuilt map instead of a card array, which also removes a duplicate map build per render. Verified in the browser: a deck holding one v1 and one v2-only card now reports 2 reviews in Deck performance where it previously reported 1. The resolver is deck-attribution only — `getCardTitle`/`rowVisualFor` live behind lucide-importing feature modules, and `src/domain` does not value-import `src/features`, so titles and type icons are resolved in the page.

**D187. Review records are immutable; the card and deck metadata shown beside them reflects the card's *current* metadata.** A card reviewed in Deck A, later renamed and moved to Deck B, appears in old history under its current name and Deck B. This is a history view, not a forensic audit trail. Snapshotting titles and deck identities onto each log is a deliberate non-goal until historical fidelity is actually wanted. Reviews whose card no longer exists in either store are **kept**, shown as `(deleted card)` with an em-dash deck — the opposite of `computeDeckPerformance`, which must skip them because it groups by deck. Dropping them here would misrepresent what was studied.

**D188. Review history gets its own local range presets (30D/3M/1Y/All); the shared `DATE_RANGE_PRESETS` is untouched.** Overview's presets feed period-over-period deltas, where an all-time window is meaningless — but a history page that cannot show reviews older than a year is wrong for an SRS app, where multi-year use is the desirable outcome. `ActivityHeatmap` already set the precedent for a local range union, so `buildHistoryRange` returns `DateRange | null`, with `null` meaning no date filter. 7D was skipped as too narrow for a chronological list.

**D189. Rating pills are neutral, with one restrained accent on Again only — not a danger/warning/success/accent set.** Again/Hard/Good/Easy are FSRS self-assessments, not error/warning/success states: a Hard review is not a warning, and this product already separates objective correctness from the rating a card is scheduled by. A paginated table of four saturated chips would reintroduce exactly the Anki-rainbow feel the Review UI has been moving away from. Rank is carried by ink weight, surface depth and the numeral. The single exception was found in the browser: with four fully neutral pills, Again — the one thing a review log is actually scanned for — was unfindable at a glance, so it takes the palest accent tint (`--itera-accent-soft`) with neutral ink, reading as emphasis rather than alarm.

**Noted, not done: the v1/v2 card split should be retired.** v1 is already a storage format rather than a UI concept (its registry and renderers are deleted; `migrateCard` renders it through the v2 registry), yet two stores, two types and the duplicated attribution logic D186 patches all persist. Converging on one card model would delete this whole class of bug. It is out of scope here because it touches the Repository seam, backup, and a real migration over persisted rows, which needs explicit approval and a dry run per `CLAUDE.md`. This entry records it as a follow-up to schedule, not as an accepted end state.

Verification: `npx vitest run` 432/432 across 64 files, `npx tsc -b --force` and `npm run lint` clean. Chromium at 1440×900 and 390×844 against the real dev server, seeded with a nested deck pair, a v2-only card, a pre-`dueAfter` row and a review whose card was deleted. Confirmed: newest-first ordering; 1Y hides and All reveals a 400-day-old review; deck scoping includes a child deck; rating filter; both empty-state branches (no logs at all, with CTA; no filter matches, without); the legacy row rendering an em-dash interval beside real "10m"/"12d"/"2d" values. Grading two cards through the **real** `/review` flow wrote `dueAfter` on every log. At 390px the nav stacks above content and the table scrolls inside its own container with no horizontal body scroll (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`). Toolbar controls are all keyboard-reachable, Review history is the only active nav item on its route, and Activity still renders as a "Soon" row. Zero console or page errors.

## 2026-08-18 — Single card model

Itera carried two live card models: v1 (`src/types/card.ts`, 8 types) and v2 (`src/types/cardV2.ts`, 6 interactions), with two stores, two repos, two hook families, two row components and `migrateCard` bridging them on read. v1 had had no authoring path since D178 deleted its registry and renderers; it was storage residue. The duplication was not inert — it produced D186's live bug, and it taxed every new feature with "which model am I in?".

**D190. The prototype card data was reset, not migrated — and that decision made most of the planned work unnecessary.** The ~250 existing cards were generated content imported to have something to build against, and the product owner stated explicitly that none of it (cards, review history, decks) needed preserving, instructing that architectural cleanliness be optimized for over backward compatibility. A migration-based plan had already been drafted — a `MigrationRunner`, a preflight report, a Settings dry-run/apply surface, a quarantined `LegacyCard` module, a raw-snapshot rollback artifact, a two-pass sequence — and **all of it was deleted rather than built**, because every piece existed to protect data that did not need protecting. Recorded because the discarded plan was the more defensible one for real data, and a future reader should know the difference was the data's status, not the engineering.

**D191. Supersedes D6 and D65.** D6 kept the two models "alongside … during Phases C–D" so exhaustive-switch safety would catch incomplete migration work; three of the four safety nets it named (the v1 registry, `seedContent`) were themselves deleted by D178, and the coexistence outlasted its own justification. D65 chose opportunistic per-card cutover "not in bulk, not automatically"; the split is instead resolved by deleting v1 outright. `architecture.md`'s "Two card models coexist on purpose" entry is removed, per the 2026-08-18 Review history note that recorded this as "not as an accepted end state".

**D192. One `Card`: the v2 shape, with `CardV2`/`CardV2Record` collapsed into it.** The content-vs-record split existed only because `migrateCard` produced content without scheduling; with no migration there is nothing for it to serve. `src/types/card.ts` is now `{id, schemaVersion, deckId, prompt, tip?, explanation?, interaction, tags, createdAt, updatedAt, suspended, scheduling, order?}`. `CARD_V2_SCHEMA_VERSION` becomes `CARD_SCHEMA_VERSION` and **keeps the value 2** — it describes the payload shape, and renumbering it to 1 would have invalidated the shape marker on stored rows. `ReviewEvent`/`StudySession` moved to `types/review.ts` unchanged: still unimplemented sketches, but not card-model scaffolding and so out of scope. `Draft.intendedType` retyped from `CardType` to `InteractionType`, which the data reset made free.

**D193. `CardState` was removed entirely, superseding two documented rules.** It was the half-built content/scheduling split (Phase D steps 1–3): dual-written by every write path, **read by nothing**, and justified solely as a safe migration path for data now being discarded. Carrying a write-only store, its backfill runner, its Settings section and its Supabase table into a deliberately clean prototype is exactly the unfinished scaffolding this pass existed to remove. This supersedes `CLAUDE.md`'s "do not drop either side of the dual write" and weakens `architecture.md` principle 4 ("content is separate from learning state") from a description of the code to a stated future goal. Both are recorded rather than quietly dropped. Reintroduction is cheap and would be designed against real requirements, with real data to protect — which is the only condition under which the separation actually earns its complexity. `src/domain/migration/runner.ts` is **kept**: it is a contract for future migrations rather than scaffolding for an abandoned one, `CLAUDE.md` names it as the required mechanism, and the Collection/Deck split still expects it. It now has no implementors.

**D194. The Dexie database was renamed `code-srs` to `itera`, with a single `version(1)`.** A rename rather than an in-place wipe: it guarantees a clean schema, makes a stale store structurally impossible, collapses the entire `version(1)`..`version(4)` upgrade ladder into one declaration, and matches the rebrand. `db.ts` fires a one-time `Dexie.delete('code-srs')` so the superseded database does not linger. Supabase gets `0002_single_card_model.sql`, which drops `card_states` **before** `cards` — that FK is `on delete cascade`, so the reverse order would have cascaded through `card_states` mid-migration — then drops `cards_v2` and recreates one `cards` table. This also closes the §8 gap where `cards_v2` had never had a migration file at all.

**D195. Old backups are refused, not migrated, and `parseBackup` gained a lower bound to make that safe.** `BACKUP_VERSION` is 2. The existing guard only rejected versions *newer* than supported, so a version-1 file would have passed validation and had its v1 cards written into the unified store as though they were the new shape — silent corruption, and the one outcome this change could not permit. `MIN_SUPPORTED_BACKUP_VERSION` now refuses them with a message naming the version and the reason.

**D196. Two adjacent fixes were folded in, because the convergence rewrote the exact code they lived in.** `src/domain/search/searchableText.ts` became the single implementation, replacing two duplicated private copies in the Dexie and Supabase backends that indexed only recall answers, MC options and write-code answers — text inside ordering, matching and walkthrough cards was silently unfindable. **This is currently unreachable from the UI**: no surface passes `text` to `repo.cards.search`, and the Library's own search filters card titles client-side. It is correct and de-duplicated, not a user-visible win, and is recorded that way rather than claimed as one. Separately, `buildCardDeckMap` collapsed to a single card array — D186's bug class ceasing to exist is the clearest single measure of what the convergence bought.

Verification: `npx vitest run` 390/390 across 61 files, `npx tsc -b --force`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` all clean. `grep -ri "cardv2|cardsV2|card_states|cardStates"` over `src/` and `supabase/schema.sql` returns only intentional historical comments. Chromium at 1440x900 and 390x844 against the real dev server, starting from the fresh empty `itera` database: `indexedDB.databases()` reports only `itera`; the empty states on Today, Library, Progress, Review history and Review all render with no console or page errors. With one card of each of the six interaction types seeded, the deck page lists all six **once each** (6 unique row links for 6 cards — the old un-deduplicated concat could not have produced that), each with the right icon and label, "6 due today" where v2 cards previously never counted toward due, and each id opens its correct editor. All six were then graded end to end through the real `/review` UI — Recall (Space to flip), Multiple Choice, Write Code (typed into CodeMirror), Ordering, Matching (paired by clicking), Walkthrough (Reveal answer then Finish) — with reps incremented, due dates moved into the future, and a `ReviewLog` written carrying `dueAfter` in every case. Progress and Review history then populated with real deck attribution. At 390px the deck table scrolls inside its own container with no horizontal body scroll.

One correction to an earlier claim in this pass: the browser sweep initially reported the six interaction labels as missing and search as returning nothing. Both were faults in the verification script (wrong label casing; and searching for content the Library's title-only filter was never going to match), not in the application.

## 2026-08-18 — MVP integrity cleanup

A contained pass over five MVP-adjacent surfaces that were telling the user something untrue, plus the documentation contradictions the single-card-model convergence left behind. No page was redesigned and no new feature system was added.

**D197. `docs/prompts/ai-card-prompt.md` was rewritten against the real v2 contract, and the prompt is now part of the import contract rather than adjacent documentation.** It still emitted `version: 1`, the deleted 8-type taxonomy (`basic`/`mcq`/`codeReading`/`codeCompletion`/`bugFinding`/`ordering`/`matching`/`story`), bare-string prose fields and a `type` + `content` card envelope. Every one of those is now rejected by `parseBackup`, so the feature was not merely stale — it produced files the app refuses. The rewrite emits backup version 2, the single `Card` (`schemaVersion: 2`, `prompt`/`tip`/`explanation` as `RichContent`, embedded `scheduling` with all nine fields and `state: "new"`), and only the six current interactions with their real field shapes: correctness as a boolean **on** each `multiple_choice` option (not a separate id list), `ordering.correctOrder` mirroring `items`, `matching.relationships` as columnId→itemId rows capped at three columns, `walkthrough.steps[].response`. Two traps are called out explicitly because they are invisible from the type alone: `Deck.language` is a natural-language code and must be omitted, and `write_code.acceptedAnswers` are compared against the **whole** editor contents, not a missing fragment. Recorded as a decision because the alternative — treating the prompt as documentation to fix "when convenient" — is what let it drift into producing unimportable files. It must now be updated in the same pass as any change to `src/types/card.ts` or `src/domain/io/backup.ts`.

**D198. Import validation is split across two layers by what each layer can know, and neither is a schema framework.** `src/domain/io/validateBackupEntities.ts` is pure and dependency-free (no validation library was added, per the hand-built rule): it checks each deck and card's own structure, and stops at container shape inside an interaction payload. `src/data/backup.ts` applies the single rule that needs repository state. The split is the point — referential integrity is **mode-dependent**, and putting it in the pure parser would have forced one of two wrong answers: rejecting every legitimate Merge top-up file that references an existing deck, or checking nothing.

**D199. `card.deckId` is validated referentially; `deck.parentId` deliberately is not.** Under Merge the known set is the file's decks **plus** the decks already in the library, so an AI-generated file containing only cards imports correctly against an existing deck. Under Replace it is the file's decks only, because Replace discards the library first — an existing deck cannot rescue a reference that will not exist a moment later, and the error message says so. `parentId` is excluded because a dangling parent is a *producible and tolerated* state today: `useDeleteDeck` does not reparent children, and `collectionTree.ts` already falls back to top-level. Rejecting it would refuse a legitimate export of a real library, which is the opposite of the goal. `drafts`/`reviewLogs`/`roadmaps` keep list-presence validation only, and `ReviewLog` rows intentionally outlive their cards (Review history renders `(deleted card)`), so a referential rule there would be actively wrong.

**D200. Validation runs before the replace-mode `clear()`, and the scheduling/interaction tables are compiler-enforced.** A rejected import must leave storage untouched; running the deck-reference check after `clear()` would have turned a bad file into data loss, which is the failure mode `CLAUDE.md`'s data-safety section exists to prevent. There is a test asserting exactly that. Separately, `SCHEDULING_FIELDS` is typed `Record<keyof Required<SchedulingState>, FieldKind>` and `INTERACTION_CHECKS` is typed `Record<InteractionType, …>`, so adding a scheduling field or a seventh interaction fails the build until it is classified here — the validator cannot silently fall behind the model the way the prompt did.

**D201. `card.schemaVersion` is checked against `CARD_SCHEMA_VERSION`, not merely for being a number.** A card written for a different card model would otherwise be stored as though it were the current shape. That is the same silent corruption D195's `MIN_SUPPORTED_BACKUP_VERSION` guard prevents at the envelope level; this closes it at the entity level, which matters because a hand-assembled or LLM-generated file can carry a correct envelope around stale cards.

**D202. The backup envelope's `app: 'code-srs'` is now named in code and docs as a legacy format identifier, not the product name.** It is extracted as `BACKUP_APP_MARKER` and documented in `architecture.md`, `docs/README.md` and the AI prompt with an explicit "do not rename this during Itera branding cleanup" — because it is exactly the kind of string a future rebrand sweep would change, and doing so would invalidate every previously exported file with no error at write time. The user-facing rejection message was updated to say "Itera backup", which is copy and carries no compatibility weight.

**D203. `src/domain/io/backupFixtures.ts` is a non-test module on purpose.** `tsconfig.app.json` excludes `*.test.ts(x)` from the typecheck, so a fixture written inline in a test can rot into a shape that no longer exists — `src/data/backup.test.ts` was still asserting against a v1 card (`type: 'basic'`, `content: {front, back}`) and passing, because the repository stores opaque JSON and nothing typechecked it. Putting the fixtures where the compiler sees them makes "this is a valid `Card`" an actual build-time claim.

**D204. The account menu's Spaced repetition (FSRS) row became an `aria-disabled` "Soon" placeholder; the smallest honest fix was removing the link, not building the section.** It pointed at `/settings/card-scheduling`, which is not a slug in `settingsSections.ts`, so `resolveSection` silently returned `profile` and the row opened an unrelated page while looking live. Both alternatives were rejected: hiding the row contradicts the established convention that unbuilt destinations stay visible and focusable, and building a real FSRS settings section is a feature this milestone explicitly excluded. **Supersedes D176's claim that "Spaced repetition links to `/settings/card-scheduling`, the existing page that explains the current FSRS defaults and hosts the real CardState migration tool"** — that page was removed with `CardState` (D193), which is what left the link dangling. The live rows are now Account settings, Import / Export and Sign out.

**D205. The account menu's identity block shows only what a live session actually knows, replacing the hardcoded "Your name".** **Supersedes the 2026-08-11 entry's "Identity is a greyed placeholder in local mode ('Your name' / 'Not signed in')"**: that treatment held only while signed out. With a session live, the email filled the second line and "Your name" rendered above it in dark bold, reading as real account data — the fabricated-personal-data outcome the original decision existed to avoid. It now renders the session email, or **"Demo workspace"** for the demo identity (reusing `AccountSettingsPage`'s existing reasoning that the synthetic address would read as a real account), over a line stating where the data lives: "Stored in this browser" / "Synced with Supabase" / "Local data only". No profile record, no profile persistence, and no name inferred from an email address — the two-line composition from `profile-menu.png` is kept, filled with facts instead of a placeholder person.

**D206. The Library empty state no longer promises an automatic "Inbox" deck.** The copy read "or just make a card — an 'Inbox' deck is created automatically", and no such behavior exists anywhere in the repository: cards are only created at `/decks/:deckId/cards/new`, which requires a deck. It now describes what the app does ("Create your first deck, then add cards to it. Decks can nest, so a broad subject can hold subdecks."). Automatic Inbox creation was **not** implemented — the promise was removed rather than fulfilled, because an inbox deck is a product concept belonging with the Drafts surface, not a copy fix.

**D207. The audit's reported `MatchingEditorShell.test.tsx` full-suite timeout does not reproduce, and nothing was changed to "fix" it.** Three full `npx vitest run` passes on 2026-08-18 - one before this milestone's changes, two after - completed in 36.0s, 35.5s and 54.7s with zero failures. The spread is machine load, not a test approaching its limit. No per-test timeout was raised and Vitest's global timeout was not touched. Recorded so a future reader does not mistake a silently-raised timeout for the resolution; if it resurfaces it is unexplained and should be investigated rather than accommodated.

Documentation reconciled in the same pass, all verified against the working tree first: `CURRENT_STATE.md` (stale `useSearchCardsV2`/`useCardsV2` hooks that do not exist, the contradictory 390-vs-451 test counts, the `/cards/:id/edit` "all 8 v1 types" route wording, the `CardV2Record` next-milestone sentence, and the missing §18 — sections 19–23 renumbered to 18–22, with `CLAUDE.md`'s one `§19` reference updated); `architecture.md` (the "database name is still literally `code-srs`" line that contradicted the code block below it, deleted `cardMigration.ts`/`cardStateBackfill.ts`/`cardState.ts` in the repository map, the incomplete `stats/` listing, the deleted `design-preview` Library routes, and the account-menu description); `design-system.md` (the same "Spaced repetition is live" claim); `features.md` (`migrateCard` in `/preview`, `StudySession` cited as living in the deleted `src/types/cardV2.ts`); `itera-migration-plan.md` §2 (its body still described auto-migrating a v1 backup, which D195 reversed); `docs/README.md` (the claim that `BACKUP_VERSION` is 1 and the v1 8-type prompt is "correct today"); and `README.md`'s AI-generation section.

Verification: `npx vitest run` 425/425 across 62 files, `npx tsc -b --force`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` all clean (the pre-existing chunk-size advisory only, unchanged). Chromium at 1440x900 and 390x844 against the real dev server, 27 scripted checks, all passing. The import fixture was **hand-written by following the rewritten prompt**, not generated from the app's own types, so it tests the prompt rather than the exporter: three nested decks and one card of each of the six interaction types imported through the real **Import JSON → Merge** UI ("Imported 6 cards, 3 decks, 0 drafts"), the deck page listed them, the matching and walkthrough cards each opened in their correct editor, `/review` showed "1 of 6", and grading one through the real UI dropped the queue to "1 of 5" — so the generated `scheduling` block really is consumed by the due query and FSRS. The malformed fixture was refused with *Card 2 ("bad-card-02") has an unsupported interaction type "mcq". Supported types: recall, multiple_choice, write_code, ordering, matching, walkthrough.*, and its valid first card was **not** written; a refused **Replace** import left the existing library fully intact, confirming validation precedes `clear()`. The account menu was checked in all three identity states — demo ("Demo workspace" / "Stored in this browser"), a local email session (the real address / "Stored in this browser"), and signed out ("Not signed in" / "Local data only", covered by `AccountMenu.test.tsx`) — with no "Your name" anywhere. The Spaced repetition row is a `BUTTON` with no `href`, `aria-disabled="true"`, a visible SOON pill, still keyboard-focusable, and clicking it leaves the URL on `/decks`; Import / Export still resolves to `/settings/import-export`. At 390px the same content renders as a bottom sheet fully inside the viewport with no horizontal body scroll. Zero console or page errors throughout.

## 2026-08-18 — Today becomes real, and the Review queue becomes a snapshot

Milestone 2 ("Make Today Real"). Today's composition, grid, typography and the pixel-tuned `SuggestedSessionHero` geometry are unchanged; every learning value behind them is now computed. **Supersedes D51 and D171's "content is illustrative on purpose" status** — that was the deliberate first cut, and this is the pass it deferred to. D170's hero reveal and resting geometry are untouched and were re-measured to prove it.

**D208. One canonical streak serves Today, the top nav and Progress.** `calcStreaks` was already correct but private inside `progressMetrics.ts`, while Today's Momentum row and `StreakBadge` each hardcoded `7`. It was **extracted, not rewritten**, into `src/domain/stats/streak.ts` as `computeStreak(logs, now) -> { current, best, activeToday }`; `computeKpis` now calls it and Progress's UI is untouched. The grace rule is preserved verbatim: studying through yesterday keeps the streak alive, and it only reaches 0 after a full local calendar day is actually missed. The surfaces still *present* it differently (Today: current; nav: compact current; Progress: current + best) — what is shared is the calculation, and `dateRange.ts` now exports `startOfDay`/`DAY_MS` so streaks, the heatmap and the pace series cannot drift on where a day starts.

**D209. Weekly Goal was deleted, not computed.** "4 of 5 sessions" plus its progress bar had no backing concept anywhere: no goal entity, no target, no setting, no definition of what counts. Any number in that row would have been invented, so the row is gone and **Retention took its slot**, which keeps Momentum's four-row rhythm and divider structure intact. A real Weekly Goal needs a persisted target, a settings surface and a counting rule; it is recorded as future work in `features.md` and `TODO.md`, and nothing was pre-built for it.

**D210. Next milestone stays in Momentum, but becomes a derived in-progress-deck continuation.** The fabricated "Finish Type Deduction / 12 of 18 topics mastered" is replaced by `selectNextMilestone`, which answers "help me finish the deck I am already working through". A deck is **in progress** when `learned > 0 && learned < total`; the winner is the **most recently reviewed in-progress deck**, so a deck that happens to hold the single latest review cannot win once it is complete. With nothing in progress it falls back to the most actionable due deck (Start/Review), and with nothing suitable at all it says "All caught up" rather than inventing an objective. **No milestone or achievement entity was created** — this is recomputed each render, like Progress's Recent milestones. Richer milestones (mastery, topic completion, streak and volume awards, custom goals) stay out of scope, and no framework was built in anticipation.

**D211. "Learned" means a current, non-suspended card with at least one `ReviewLog` — and the copy says exactly that.** Unique cards, never a count of review logs; suspended cards are excluded from the total; a card moved between decks is attributed to the deck it is in **now**, matching `buildCardDeckMap`'s convention for every other deck-scoped statistic. The row reads "18 of 24 cards learned" and the word **mastered** is deliberately absent, because one review is one review. The definition is stated in `todayMetrics.ts`, in `architecture.md`, and asserted by tests that would fail if it silently became "reps > 0" or a log count.

**D212. Pace is reviews completed per local calendar day, and "You're on track" is gone.** Seven buckets ending today, zero-review days included, real counts, a data-derived axis with a floor of 4 so a one-review day does not fill the panel. `durationMs` is **not** consulted here — it backs the session-duration estimate and nothing else. The status line was removed rather than recomputed: "on track" claims progress toward a target, and there is no target. No goal line, no chart dependency; the hand-rolled SVG is the same one.

**D213. Retention is consumed from Progress's definition, and cautiously labelled.** `retentionOf` is exported as `computeRetention` and Today calls it over a trailing 30 days rather than defining a second percentage. That calculation reads each review's **post-grade** FSRS state and is semantically imperfect; the `stateBefore` correction is the next milestone's job, and sharing one function means it lands on both surfaces at once. Until then the row says **Retention**, not "Recall rate", and shows an em dash — never `0%`, which would read as "you failed every card" — when there is nothing mature to measure.

**D214. Adjust session is a transient deck scope plus a card count, and nothing else.** It had no behavior at all; it now offers **All due cards / One deck** and **All due / 10 / 20 / 30 / Custom**, and starts `/review?deck=<id>&limit=<n>`. **No `StudySession` entity, no persisted preference** — a later plain "Start session" is the default queue again. Deck scope reuses `subtreeIds`, the same helper `/review` scopes with, so the count shown is exactly what the session will contain, and the picker lists **every** deck (parents included, with its full path) precisely because `?deck=` is a subtree scope. Presets are never disabled: the session caps transparently and the summary states the truth, including refusing to name a count when the custom value is unusable. The advanced controls in `TODO.md` — time-boxed sessions, weak-cards-only, new-vs-review, difficulty / interaction / tag filters, custom FSRS — are all deferred, and **nothing was abstracted in anticipation of them**.

**D215. The Review queue is a snapshot taken when a session starts, keyed by an explicit transient session id.** This was found while wiring `limit`, and it was broken for a plain `/review` too: grading invalidates the `cards` query key, `useDueCards` refetches, the graded card leaves the due array, and `ReviewPage` keyed `ReviewSessionV2` on `cards.length` — so every grade remounted the session. Measured before the fix, a five-card session counted `1 of 5` -> `2 of 5` -> `2 of 4` -> `2 of 3` -> `2 of 2`, lost its undo stack, and ended in the pre-session "Nothing due" state instead of "All done". `src/features/review/useSessionQueue.ts` now freezes the resolved queue; `ReviewSessionV2` is keyed on the snapshot `id`, never on a length. **Query-parameter identity alone was explicitly rejected as insufficient**: `deck|limit` distinguishes configurations but cannot distinguish a later session from the same one, so the snapshot is per-mount, and leaving `/review` (both exits do) unmounts the route and ends it. That is what makes a second session at an identical URL resolve a fresh queue. `limit` is parsed permissively — `0`, negatives, decimals and text mean "no limit" — because a malformed URL should still start a usable session.

**D216. `deckMetrics.ts` moved to `src/domain/stats/`, and the deck leaf/parent rule moved to `src/domain/decks/tree.ts`.** Today needs the same per-deck due/last-studied/mastery numbers the Library shows, and `src/domain` may not import from `src/features`. `collectionTree.leafDecks` now delegates to the shared `parentDeckIds`/`leafDecks`, so "which decks are Collections" has one implementation. **Continue Learning lists leaf decks only** — a ranked list where a parent row contained a child row's work would be unreadable — with the accepted tradeoff, documented in code, that cards filed directly on a deck-with-children get no row of their own. They are still counted wherever the total matters (the hero, Due today) and are reachable from the Adjust session dialog, which does list parents because that is an explicit choice rather than a ranking.

**D217. Continue Learning ranks continuity among actionable decks.** Ordering is: decks with due cards before decks with none; among those, most recently studied first; then the larger backlog; then name for determinism. Due-count-first was considered and rejected — it lets a neglected hundred-card backlog permanently outrank the deck the learner was in yesterday, which is the opposite of what a section called "Continue learning" should answer. A deck with nothing due can never outrank one with real work waiting, and such a row's action opens the deck ("Open" -> `/decks/:id`) rather than starting a session that would immediately land on "Nothing due".

**D218. Today does not render against half-loaded data.** An empty `dueCards` result is indistinguishable from "not fetched yet" at the panel level, so a grid painted on the first tick claimed "All caught up" and a zero streak to every user on every visit before correcting itself. The page holds a `Loading…` line until its four queries resolve. Its three real states are then: **no decks and no cards** -> one intentional empty state (Create a deck / Import; no global new-card link, since card creation requires a deck); **cards but nothing due** -> the same hero box reading "All caught up / Nothing due" with the real next-due time and **no Adjust control at all**, because no scope or size can conjure due work; **due cards** -> normal Today. Continue Learning's heading link also changed from "View all topics" to **View all decks**, because there is no topic concept in this product.

Verification: `npx vitest run` 535/535 across 68 files (was 425/62 — 110 added, none removed); `npx tsc -b --force`, `npm run lint` and `npm run build` clean, the pre-existing chunk-size advisory only. Chromium against the real dev server at 1440x900 and 390x844, seeded through IndexedDB with nested decks, suspended and future-due cards, and a review history with a deliberate gap so current (4) differs from best (5). The hero's four settled layers were re-measured and match D170's recorded resting values exactly at both widths (`tx=35 ty=-20` for card #2, `tx=5 ty=-28` for the front card, `sin(-5°)` and `sin(0.15°)` skews for #3 and #4, cards #3/#4 `display: none` below `sm`). Today's streak, the nav badge and Progress's KPI tile all read **4** against the same history, with Progress additionally reporting Best 5. The Adjust dialog was exercised end to end at both widths: subtree counts (MLIR Basics 5, Compiler Architecture 7 including its child), a capped 30-preset, an invalid custom value blocking Start with a visible message, and a started session landing on `?deck=d-compilers&limit=3` showing "1 of 3". The queue snapshot was verified by grading a real five-card session in the browser — `1 of 5` through `5 of 5`, then the real "All done" screen, then completion-screen Undo returning to `5 of 5` with four logs left — plus a mid-session reorder behind the session's back that did not move it, and a second session at an identical `?deck=&limit=` URL correctly resolving a fresh one-card queue. Phone: `scrollWidth === clientWidth` at 390, four Momentum rows, the milestone bar ending 37px inside the viewport, and the dialog as a bottom-anchored full-width sheet with its validation message visible. Zero console or page errors throughout.

---

## 2026-08-21 — Progress correctness and KPI definitions

Milestone 3. No Progress composition, planned sidebar page, StudySession entity, achievement system or new dependency was added. This pass changed the meaning and actionability of existing data surfaces, then verified that Today and the app shell still agree with them.

**D219. `ReviewLog.stateBefore` is required and is written only at the existing log-construction choke point.** `buildReviewLog` receives the explicit scheduling state before and after FSRS. It now writes `stateBefore: before.state` and keeps the existing `state: after.state`; no UI, hook or repository attempts to infer either value later. Tests cover new → Easy, learning → Good, review → Again/Good and relearning inputs so a future scheduler refactor cannot quietly swap the two meanings.

**D220. Prototype ReviewLog history is discarded, not reconstructed or given a fallback.** The product owner explicitly classified it as disposable. Dexie version 2 clears only `reviewLogs`, preserving cards, decks, drafts and roadmaps. `0003_review_log_state_before.sql` deletes `review_logs` before adding a JSON check that requires a valid `stateBefore`, then restores authenticated CRUD grants. The SQL has not been applied or verified against a live Supabase project. This is intentionally a clean semantic epoch: current analytics never mix pre- and post-milestone retention rules.

**D221. Retention means success among attempts that were mature before grading.** One exported `computeRetention` still serves Progress and Today's trailing-30-day Momentum row. Eligibility is `stateBefore ∈ {review, relearning}`; Hard, Good and Easy (`rating >= 2`) succeed; Again fails; no eligible attempts returns `null`, displayed as an em dash rather than `0%`. Therefore new → Easy and learning → Good are excluded, review → Again is an eligible failure, and review → Good is an eligible success. There is no branch that consults post-grade `state` when `stateBefore` is absent.

**D222. Learned has one implementation, not merely one written definition.** `src/domain/stats/learned.ts`'s `computeLearned` counts unique current, non-suspended cards with at least one ReviewLog. It never reads FSRS state and never counts logs. Progress, Today milestones and Deck Performance call it; deleted-card logs do not count, and a moved card follows its current deck. The word is Learned, never Mastered.

**D223. The headline KPI contract is exactly Learned · Due · Reviews · Retention · Current streak.** Total Sessions, Avg. accuracy and the misleading Cards reviewed label left the row; `clusterSessions` remains a tested domain helper. Learned and Due are current library/queue state, Current streak is the all-history canonical `computeStreak`, and none receives a fabricated period delta. Reviews is the ReviewLog count in the selected range, including repeated reviews of one card, with a time-bucketed count sparkline and a valid previous-period percent delta. Retention follows the range and its comparison is rendered in percentage points (`pp`), not percent.

**D224. Progress Due reuses the due query; it has no analytics-side approximation.** `ProgressPage` calls `useDueCards({now})` with one mount-stable timestamp, the same repository semantics used by Today, the nav badge and `/review`: due now, current scheduling, not suspended. This makes it an operational snapshot rather than "cards that became due in this period" and is why the date picker does not control it.

**D225. Deck Performance lists leaf/actionable decks, and every column has the same direct-card leaf scope.** Parent plus child rows with subtree totals would double-count the same work in a flat, non-hierarchical table. Leaf-only rows match Continue Learning's established clarity rule and make `/review?deck=<leaf-id>` exactly the scope the row describes. The accepted tradeoff is also the same: cards filed directly on a deck-with-children have no Deck Performance row, though global KPIs and an explicitly parent-scoped Review session still include them. Columns are Deck, Learned, Due and Retention.

**D226. Actionable due work determines row visibility, order and navigation.** Every leaf with active cards appears even when it has zero selected-period logs. Sort order is due-present first, larger due count, weaker valid retention, latest review, then name. A row with due work opens `/review?deck=<id>`; a caught-up row opens the deck. Current-card attribution means moved cards follow their current leaf and deleted cards contribute to no current row.

**D227. The misleading Deck Trend was removed, and retention-chart nulls are real gaps.** Trend's six points were review-count chunks, not time buckets, so its line looked chronological while often reducing to individual attempts. Building a second honest series added no MVP value beside the existing time-based retention chart, so removal was the smaller correct result. That chart now emits one SVG path per consecutive non-null run; it never bridges a bucket with no mature observations.

**D228. Backup version remains 2, with current ReviewLog validation.** The envelope already carried `reviewLogs`, and the AI-card workflow correctly produces an empty array, so bumping every otherwise-current card import would add compatibility cost without protecting anything. Instead `parseBackup` now validates the complete current ReviewLog shape, including required `stateBefore`, before any write. Version-2 files with empty history remain valid; incompatible prototype histories are rejected. Export naturally includes the required field because it serializes current typed rows.

**D229. The functional MVP is complete enough to move to polish / demo readiness.** This does not declare every planned surface complete: the seven disabled Progress destinations, Collection/Deck Phase G, Roadmaps reskin, full Settings and other deferred work remain deferred. It means the primary create/import → review → Today/Progress loop now has one card model, one due queue, one streak, one learned definition and one semantically correct retention definition; another feature milestone is no longer the highest-value next step.

Verification: `npx vitest run` 569/569 across 73 files, `npx tsc -b --force` and `npm run lint` clean; `npm run build` succeeds with the unchanged chunk-size advisory. Chromium against the real dev server at 1440×900 and 390×844 used seeded current and previous periods plus new/learning attempts. The five exact KPI labels rendered; changing to 7D updated Reviews and Retention; no mature history rendered `—`; retention used `pp`; heat map and milestones remained; at least two SVG retention segments proved the null gap; due rows appeared and opened their review scope. Cross-surface values were Due **4** on Today/nav/Progress/Review, Streak **2** on Today/nav/Progress, and trailing-30-day Retention **67%** on Today and Progress despite new → Easy and learning → Good logs in the seed. At 390px all KPI and Deck Performance columns remained usable with no horizontal body overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 390`). Zero console or page errors.
