## 2026-08-23 - Phase 3.0 adds the Expo physical-device bootstrap and nothing else

**Context.** The project owner explicitly authorized a bounded infrastructure milestone before approving the separately-designed mobile GUI: create the Expo workspace, prove standard workspace consumption of `@itera/core`, and establish the Expo Go physical-device loop. This supersedes only `CURRENT_STATE.md`'s previous "do not scaffold Expo yet" checkpoint. It does not authorize the older Phase 3 plan's auth, Supabase, storage, fonts, navigation, or screens.

**D346. SDK 54 is selected for this bootstrap because the device loop, not the newest SDK number, is the requirement.** Current official Expo documentation says that during the SDK 57 transition, `create-expo-app@latest` without a template remains on SDK 54 and physical-device Expo Go projects should use SDK 54. The scaffold therefore used the explicit official `default@sdk-54` TypeScript/Expo Router template. Node 22.17.1 satisfies SDK 54's Node 20.19.4 minimum, so no development-machine upgrade was made. This is a bootstrap tooling choice, not a permanent refusal to upgrade.

**D347. The generated example was reset, not adopted as mobile design.** Expo's own reset script deleted the tab routes, demo components, hooks, constants, and reset script. The generated icons and splash artwork were also removed, and `app.json` keeps only the name/slug/version, the Expo Router plugin, and typed routes. The resulting app has one neutral route and one `Slot` layout, with no tabs, header, brand palette, custom font, card, product screen, or interaction presentation. The nested Expo guidance remains subordinate to the root instructions and only points agents at the exact SDK 54 docs.

**D348. Metro consumes the normal workspace package with zero resolver configuration.** `@itera/mobile` declares `@itera/core: "*"` and the TanStack Query peer that core's public barrel requires. The smoke route imports `parseRichText` from that public barrel, calls it at module load with `mobile-bootstrap`, asserts the returned `RichBlock.kind`, and renders `@itera/core: OK` only after that call succeeds. `expo export --platform android` bundled all 1,097 modules to Hermes through the ordinary npm workspace junction. There is no `metro.config.js`, alias, `watchFolders`, `resolver.nodeModulesPaths`, or symlink workaround. The device still has to execute that bundle before the runtime proof is complete.

**D349. Expo's React version wins, and npm must physically deduplicate it.** The first install showed why the Phase 0 warning was load-bearing: Expo SDK 54 pins React 19.1.0 while the web manifest allowed 19.2.7, and npm's initial layout let Expo's transitive modules resolve 19.2.7 beside the app's 19.1.0. The web manifest was aligned to React/ReactDOM 19.1.0 and npm's lock was deduplicated, so core, web, mobile, Expo, and React Native now resolve one physical React 19.1.0; TanStack Query remains one 5.101.1 instance. React Native stays Expo's 0.81.5 and is not made to match anything web-side. Existing direct web package resolutions were preserved when the root lockfile was regenerated; no audit fix ran.

**D350. Scaffold health is proven; physical-device success is not yet claimed.** Expo Doctor passes 18/18 checks, public config reports SDK 54.0.0, Expo's TypeScript 5.9.3 project and Expo lint are clean, and Android Metro/Hermes export succeeds. The unchanged web/core gates also pass: 111 files / 1,064 tests, root `tsc -b --force`, repository oxlint, and the production web/PWA build with 24 precache entries. Device status remains pending until the owner scans the live LAN QR and confirms the neutral three-line screen. No EAS, development build, Supabase, credentials, repository registration, auth, native module, or product GUI was added.

**D351. Physical-device execution completes Phase 3.0; the smoke route is not the home page.** The owner opened the development build through Expo Go on a physical iOS device and observed the exact expected result: `Itera mobile bootstrap`, `Expo runtime: OK`, and `@itera/core: OK`. The third line is guarded by the module-load call to `parseRichText`, so this is runtime evidence that Metro resolved and the device executed the normal `@itera/core` workspace package, not merely that the Expo shell opened. The LAN development loop worked and no tunnel was needed. Phase 3.0 is therefore complete. The route remains disposable infrastructure proof and must be replaced, not evolved into a product home page; the next separately authorized milestone begins only after the owner approves the mobile GUI/design direction.

---

## 2026-08-24 - M1A gives mobile a real cloud/auth/data foundation, and nothing above it

**Context.** The 2026-08-24 mobile parity audit found `apps/mobile` to be a high-fidelity GUI prototype: no `configureRepository`, no `QueryClientProvider`, no Supabase client, no auth, no route protection, no session persistence, no shared-hook call sites and no tests. The audit's proposed remedy was one large "auth + Recall vertical slice" milestone, matching master-plan Phases 3 and 4. The project owner split that into three, and authorized only the first: **M1A** is the foundation, **M1B** is the native RichText renderer plus its security regression, **M1C** is the real Recall end-to-end Review gate. This entry covers M1A only.

**D352. The audit's M1 was split because a Review slice cannot honestly be built on hand-tokenized fixtures.** The master plan's Phase 4 assumes a native `RichText` renderer exists as part of the slice. It does not, and the six review screens currently render pre-tokenized `parts[]`/`codeLines[]` arrays authored by hand in `src/fixtures/` - a parallel content model that no real `Card` can produce. Wiring a live queue to those screens would either render nothing legible or require inventing a tokenizer inside the Review milestone. Splitting the gate lets the renderer be built and security-audited on its own terms, and keeps the foundation reviewable without a half-finished renderer inside it. The master plan's Phase 3/4 content is unchanged and not rewritten; M1A/M1B/M1C are a sequencing decision layered on top of it.

**D353. Mobile is cloud-only, and an unconfigured build refuses to run rather than falling back.** Master plan D11 already decided that mobile targets Supabase while web keeps both backends. The consequence is now implemented literally: `createMobileAuthConfig()` always returns `mode: 'supabase'`, there is no native Dexie/SQLite/demo backend to fall back to, and a build with no `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` renders a configuration notice instead of the product. Web resolves a `cloudEnabled` boolean and configures repository and auth from that one value so the two cannot disagree; mobile has only one option, so they cannot disagree by construction. The stated consequence stands and is now surfaced in the UI: a learner whose web workspace is browser-local sees an empty account on the phone, and the sign-in screen says so in one line.

**D354. The native session lives in SecureStore, chunked, rather than encrypted into AsyncStorage.** Master plan D4 chose "SecureStore with a chunking adapter" over AsyncStorage. Supabase's own React Native tutorial takes the third path - AES-encrypt the session, put the ciphertext in AsyncStorage, keep only the key in SecureStore - and that path was **not** taken. Chunking keeps the refresh token itself out of plain application-sandbox storage, which is what D4 asked for, and it costs zero dependencies where the vendor pattern costs three (`async-storage`, `aes-js`, `react-native-get-random-values`). The adapter stores a versioned manifest under the caller's key and the payload across numbered sibling keys, splitting on Unicode code points with a UTF-8 byte budget so an astral character can never be halved. Ordering carries the correctness: chunks are written **before** the manifest, so an interrupted write leaves the previous manifest describing a consistent set; stale chunks are deleted **after** it, so a shorter value cannot orphan a tail; and `removeItem` deletes the manifest first, so a partial delete leaves unreachable inert chunks rather than a manifest pointing into a hole. A set that is incomplete for any reason reads as signed out and self-cleans - half a JWT is not a credential. This is the Supabase client's storage seam and is deliberately **not** core's `LocalSessionStore`, which is a different concept (the local/demo record) that Supabase mode never reads; mobile supplies an inert implementation of that one, so no native local record can ever become a second way to be authenticated (audit P1-3).

**D355. Route protection uses `Stack.Protected`, and the bootstrap renders no navigator at all.** Expo Router 6 ships a first-class protected-routes API, so the historical `(auth)`/`(app)` redirect-guard example was not copied. Two mechanisms do two different jobs. While `useAuth().loading` is true nothing but a status screen renders: a guard evaluated against `isAuthenticated: false` mid-bootstrap would mount the sign-in screen for one frame and replace it, and not rendering is the only way to be certain there is no such frame. Once decided, `Stack.Protected` **removes** the other group from the navigator rather than redirecting away from it, so a protected route that does not exist cannot be reached by a deep link, a stale history entry or a `router.push` from code that forgot to check - which a redirect-based guard only corrects after the fact. Expo Router restores a route the visitor asked for once its guard opens, so the requested destination survives sign-in with no redirect parameter. The root layout became a `Stack` instead of a `Slot` because Protected requires it; that also closes the audit's MOB-P1-09 as a side effect, giving `/notifications` a real push transition and the iOS back-swipe it never had.

**D356. `src/app/` is a reserved name in an Expo Router project, and using it silently corrupted the bundle graph.** The composition modules were first placed in `apps/mobile/src/app/`. Expo Router treats `src/app` as an alternative app directory, so it built a second `require.context` route root there and pulled `*.test.tsx` - and through them `@testing-library/react-native` and Node's `util` - into the production bundle. `expo export --platform ios` failed on it. The directory is now `src/composition/`. Recorded because the failure was loud only because an export was run: with Metro's dev server alone it would have shipped test code into a development bundle unnoticed. Do not create `apps/mobile/src/app/`.

**D357. The proof that the seam is live is a development-only probe, not a wired product screen.** M1A has to demonstrate more than a session object: an authenticated native client reaching the shared `Repository`, RLS scoping the read, and a write landing under the right owner. Doing that by pointing Today or Library at real data would have put real and fixture values on the same reviewed surface - exactly the dishonest half-state this milestone exists to remove - and would have started Phase 5/6 work under an M1A label. So `app/(app)/diagnostics.tsx` reads decks and creates/deletes one disposable deck through the ordinary `useDecks`/`useCreateDeck`/`useDeleteDeck` hooks, sits off the tab bar, is reachable only from a Profile row rendered under `__DEV__`, and refuses to render otherwise. It names no backend and builds no client, so what it proves is the real path rather than a special one. It is disposable infrastructure and must be deleted when the surfaces it stands in for are wired.

**D358. Profile now shows real identity and can sign out; every other section stayed a placeholder, and three of them stopped lying.** Account identity and Sign out are genuine product behavior and were wired. The six unbuilt settings sections are web placeholders and remain `NOT REQUIRED FOR PARITY`, so none was implemented. But three of their notices had become false the moment auth landed - "Authentication is not connected in the mobile app", "Mobile has no profile record or authenticated account composition", "there is no device registry, and mobile sync is not connected" - and were corrected to describe what is now true. A placeholder may say a feature is absent; it may not state a fact about the system that is no longer the case.

**D359. Mobile tests are a second runner with zero overlapping assertions.** Master plan D10 chose `jest-expo` + RNTL for mobile and Vitest for core/web. The mobile suite covers only what is native: the SecureStore adapter's chunk arithmetic and failure ordering, the auth error classifier, the sign-in screen's state machine, the route-guard decision, and repository composition against a stub client. No core or web test was moved, duplicated or re-asserted, and nothing here re-tests product semantics core already owns. `jest.config.js` extends `jest-expo/jest-preset`'s `transformIgnorePatterns` rather than replacing it, adding only `@supabase` and `ts-fsrs`; rewriting that list by hand loses entries at every SDK upgrade. `@itera/core` needs no entry because it resolves through the workspace symlink to `packages/core`, outside `node_modules`, and is transformed like our own source. Root `npx vitest run` is unchanged at 111 files / 1064 tests and never sees these files.

**D360. `onlineManager` was deliberately left unbound, and no connectivity dependency was added.** TanStack Query's React Native guidance pairs `focusManager` with `AppState` and `onlineManager` with NetInfo. Only the first was wired. Without a connectivity module React Native reports permanently online, which for a cloud-only client with no offline cache is the honest behaviour: a request made with no signal fails and surfaces as an error, rather than being paused against a cache that does not exist. Binding NetInfo would change how a failure is presented without changing what can be done about it, and that presentation belongs with the offline milestone (Phase 11) that gives a paused query somewhere to wait.

**D361. The sign-in screen is functional infrastructure and was explicitly not designed.** The owner did not authorize a new mobile design exercise in this milestone, so the screen is the Itera mark, one field and one action, built from existing tokens with no illustration and no new visual language. Web's login illustration, principles row and typographic work are a finished design that reinterpreting on a phone would be harder to undo than to skip. Two behavioural rules are enforced instead of visual ones: `busy` is cleared in a `finally` on every path, so no action can leave its button spinning; and no thrown value is ever rendered - `authErrorCopy.ts` classifies by status and code and returns one of six fixed sentences, so GoTrue wording, URLs and transport text cannot reach a learner even when they change. A dedicated design pass remains available later.

**D362. `schema.sql` is the fresh-project bootstrap; migrations 0001-0004 are the upgrade path for a database that already exists.** This needed establishing before touching any database, and it is not obvious from the filenames. `supabase/schema.sql` already contains the **post-0004** state: the single `cards` table from 0002 (no `cards_v2`, no `card_states`), the required-`stateBefore` check constraint from 0003 inline in the `review_logs` definition, and both `commit_review`/`revert_review` functions with their revoke-then-grant from 0004. It is fully re-runnable. On a fresh project the correct action is therefore to run `schema.sql` **alone**: 0001 creates a table 0002 drops, and 0002/0003 are marked DESTRUCTIVE and would only delete rows that do not exist yet. Running them on a fresh project is not dangerous, merely pointless - but reaching for a file marked DESTRUCTIVE when a re-runnable canonical schema exists is a habit worth not forming.

**D363. No database was touched and no live verification is claimed.** No Supabase project exists for this repository - the previous one was deleted mid-development (`CURRENT_STATE.md` §15) and no `.env.local` is present anywhere in the tree. Per the milestone's own stop condition, every part of M1A that does not require credentials is implemented and every automated gate is green, and the milestone stops at the live-project checkpoint. `expo export --platform ios` bundling the full graph is the strongest static evidence available without a device: it proves `@supabase/supabase-js`, `expo-secure-store`, `@itera/core` and `ts-fsrs` all resolve and compile under Metro/Hermes, and that no `import.meta` survives into a native bundle. It proves nothing about Postgres, RLS, grants, OTP delivery, token refresh or session survival across a cold start. Those remain open and are listed in `TODO.md`; the three "unverified against a live database" entries for migrations 0002/0003/0004 are therefore **retained**, not closed.


---

## 2026-08-24 - market validation takes precedence over live cloud integration; mobile gains an explicit Demo mode

**Context.** M1A left `apps/mobile` with a real cloud foundation and no way to run it: no Supabase project exists, there is no `.env.local`, and the composition root refused to mount without one, so the app opened on "Itera is not configured" and showed nothing at all. Above that foundation the 2026-08-24 parity audit measured the product surfaces at roughly 70% presentationally and 18% functionally. The project owner has changed the priority: the goal is now a convincing, coherent, functional demo state to show potential users and gather demand, and only then to decide whether to invest further in production cloud infrastructure. This entry covers milestone M-DEMO-1, which implements that decision and nothing above it.

**D364. Market validation comes before live cloud integration, and Demo mode is a temporary product-demo path rather than the future persistence architecture.** Every part of M1A stays in the tree and none of it was weakened; what changed is which path a default build takes. Demo mode exists to be shown to people - an interactive demo, deterministic screenshots and video, and physical-device UI testing - and its data is deterministic demo data. It is never synced, cloud-backed, a persisted account or production data, and the code and documentation say so in those words. When demand is validated, the work is to point the existing screens at the shared `Repository`, not to grow this layer into one.

**D365. This supersedes D353 for the default build, and only for the default build.** D353 recorded that mobile is cloud-only and that an unconfigured build refuses to run rather than falling back. That refusal is now scoped to cloud mode: `EXPO_PUBLIC_ITERA_MODE=cloud` still requires valid `EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` and still renders the same configuration notice without it. What is no longer true is that a build with no configuration is a broken cloud build; it is a demo build, which is the honest reading of a repository that has no project behind it. The web app's rule is untouched.

**D366. The mode is one value, read once, and cloud is opt-in.** `src/config/mobileRuntimeMode.ts` resolves `'demo' | 'cloud'` from a static `process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_ITERA_MODE` member expression, for the same reason `supabaseClient.ts` reads its two variables that way: Expo inlines those textually at build time and a computed lookup is `undefined` in a production bundle. Only the exact string `cloud` selects cloud; everything else, including a typo or a half-configured build, is demo. The composition root reads that one value for both the repository and auth, so backend mode and auth mode cannot disagree - the same single-value rule `apps/web/src/main.tsx` follows, and the shape of audit P1-3's fix. In demo mode `configureRepository` is not called at all, so an accidental `getRepository()` throws instead of silently appearing to work against demo content.

**D367. Demo mode uses core's existing local-session seam rather than adding a second way to be authenticated.** Core already defines `AuthMode: 'local'` with a `LocalSession` of kind `'demo'`, documented there as "a development/demo shell, not authentication". `createMobileAuthConfig()` returns that mode with a `demoSessionStore` whose `read()` returns one fixed record and whose `write` and `clear` are inert. Core's engine leaves `loading` false in local mode, reads the store while it is being constructed, and returns a no-op from `start()`, so the app is authenticated on its first rendered frame with no OTP, no bootstrap and no Supabase client - and `RootNavigator`, `AuthProvider` and the `Stack.Protected` groups were not modified at all. Cloud mode still supplies `inertLocalSessionStore`, so the two stores can never both be live and no native local record can become a second admission ticket into a cloud build.

**D368. There is one demo workspace, and deck numbers are derived from it rather than authored per screen.** Every screen used to manufacture its own fixture snapshot, which is why one deck carried three ids - `fixture-distributed-systems` in Library, `fixture-systems` in Today, which was also a collection id, and `fixture-systems-distributed` in Progress - and four different card counts. `src/demo/` now holds one dataset, pure selectors over it, and a provider for the only mutable demo state there is. The rule is explicit: anything that is a roll-up of deck data (card counts, due counts, mastery, last studied, collection totals, Today's due total) is derived from the demo card list, so two screens cannot disagree about the same deck; anything that would need real review history (streak, retention, the activity map, the retention series, milestones) stays an authored deterministic constant, because inventing those is not this milestone's work. The layer is mobile-only, nothing was added to `@itera/core`, and it is deliberately not a `Repository` implementation - that contract represents real persistence infrastructure, and demo presentation state must not masquerade as one.

**D369. Demo state is not persisted, on purpose.** No AsyncStorage, no SQLite, no dependency. Demo state lives for the life of the process and resets on a full app restart, which is what a demo needs and what makes every screenshot reproducible. A `__DEV__` reset helper exists on the provider; no product "reset demo" control was added. This is a current-stage decision recorded here, not deferred debt, and it is deliberately absent from `TODO.md`.

**D370. Both Library routes now resolve their parameter, and an unknown id says so.** `library/deck/[deckId].tsx` handed `deckId` to a factory that ignored it and always returned Modern C++, and `library/[collectionId].tsx` collapsed every id to `collectionId === 'fixture-languages-cpp'` and rendered Interview Core for everything else. A deep link, a stale history entry or a typo therefore displayed the wrong entity with nothing to indicate it. Both routes are now thin bindings over a lookup, and a miss renders a not-found state rather than some other entity. The resolution lives in `src/demo/demoSelectors.ts` rather than in the route file because a test file may not live under `app/` - Expo Router's `require.context` would pull it into the bundle (D356) - so logic worth proving has to sit in `src/`.

**D371. Sorting is core's `sortDecks`, called verbatim, not a mobile comparator.** Web's Library offers four keys - Last studied, Name, Due soon, Card count - implemented once in `packages/core/src/library/sortDecks.ts`. Mobile projects its presentation rows into the `Deck` and `DeckMetrics` shapes that function takes and puts them back in the order it returns, so all four keys mean exactly the same thing on both platforms and there is no second comparator to keep in step. The projection is a real map rather than a cast, so the day a real `Deck` field starts mattering to the sort becomes a compile error rather than a silent divergence. The control label now renders the active sort; it previously read "Sort: Last studied" while no sort occurred at all.

**D372. The All Decks Filter control was removed rather than implemented, because web's entire filter surface is the checkbox already beside it.** `FilterMenu.tsx` on web contains exactly one option, a "Due only" checkbox, and the mobile screen already had that checkbox live and working immediately to the right of a decorative Filter dropdown. Building a sheet whose only option duplicates the adjacent control would have been two controls driving one piece of state. Removing the dead one and widening Sort into the freed space keeps the row's height, tokens and geometry unchanged, and leaves mobile with exactly web's filter capability.

**D373. Deck favorites were removed as an invented feature.** The mobile Deck screen had a working star that toggled component state and nothing else. There is no favorites concept anywhere in `apps/web/src` or `packages/core/src` - no `Deck` field, hook, mutation, filter or sort key - and web's Deck page has only a static muted glyph with no handler. No decision approved a Favorites feature, and the audit (MOB-P2-12) asked for it to be removed or recorded deliberately. It is removed, and this is the record. Building one would be a product decision about a capability neither platform has, not a mobile detail.

**D374. Every control on a touched screen is functional, clearly unavailable, or absent, and the fourth state is gone.** The audit's worst visible defect was that All Decks rows were a plain `View` with no press handler at all, so the only route into a deck screen in the whole app was one hard-coded row on the Collection screen. Deck rows, collection scopes, Today's Continue Learning rows, Progress's Deck performance rows, both sort controls and the deck card filter are now functional. Two glyphs that read as controls without being any were removed: the Collection row's overflow icon, a bare icon in a `View` that became indistinguishable from a control once the row itself navigated, and the deck favorite. Controls that remain out of scope - New Deck, Import, Study Now, Add card, deck and card actions, Collection settings, the longer Progress ranges - stay visibly disabled, and the two that sit inside a now-interactive row were recoloured so that "unavailable" is visible rather than merely announced.

**D375. Notification read state moved into the workspace, and the destinations are honest.** The inbox held its own copy in `useState`, so the header bell's dot was painted unconditionally and stayed lit after everything had been read. Read state now lives in the demo workspace, the bell renders from the real count, and a cloud build - which has no notification source at all, by design - correctly shows no dot. "Mark all as read" rendered only in the Today heading and was therefore unreachable whenever the only unread items were under Earlier; it now appears on the first visible group that has any, which is the same single affordance in the same place and never missing. A notification naming a demo deck marks itself read and opens that deck; one referring to a feature that does not exist marks itself read and says so in its accessibility hint. No push, no APNs, no scheduler, and no backend notification functionality was invented.

**D376. No Supabase work was performed, and the M1A checkpoint is preserved intact.** No project was created, `schema.sql` was not applied, no OTP was sent, RLS was not verified, no shared-`Repository` data was wired into Today, Library or Progress, and no metric was replaced with a cloud value. The `TODO.md` entry for the live Supabase project and the M1A checkpoint is unchanged, as are the three "unverified against a live database" migration items. The development diagnostics probe now refuses to render outside cloud mode rather than querying a registry that demo mode deliberately leaves unconfigured. Device verification of demo mode is pending the owner and is not claimed here.

## 2026-08-25 - the demo Review session: a native RichText renderer, shared behavior end to end, and in-memory demo scheduling

**Context.** M-DEMO-1 (D364-D376) made the demo workspace browsable and its navigation coherent, and the owner has since confirmed it on a physical device. Review remained the one surface a viewer would reach for that led only to a preview: six visually finished interaction screens, each a standalone route over a hand-written fixture, five of them unreachable, with the objective grade computed and discarded, rating buttons that were local `useState` over four hard-coded interval strings, and a fabricated `X of 10` position. This entry covers milestone M-DEMO-2, which makes that session real, locally.

**D377. The native content path is the shared parser plus a native renderer, and the parallel content model is deleted.** Every review screen rendered hand-tokenized `parts[]` and `codeLines[]` arrays declared in `apps/mobile/src/types/review.ts` - a second content representation that no real `Card` could produce, which is why the audit called it a prerequisite rather than a follow-up. `RichTextNative.tsx` now maps the nodes `parseRichText`/`parseRichInline` return onto React Native elements and parses nothing, exactly as `apps/web/src/components/text/RichText.tsx` parses nothing. The rules that matter - underscores are never emphasis markers, inline code beats emphasis, `**` beats `*` - are therefore decided once, in core, and a native renderer cannot invent a second reading of the syntax. `src/types/review.ts` and all six `src/fixtures/review*.ts` files are gone, and the interaction payloads they carried were rewritten as real card content in the demo workspace.

**D378. All eighteen demo cards became real core `Card`s, and the two authored presentation booleans were deleted rather than maintained.** `DemoCard` was a presentation record: a prompt string, an interaction type, a `status` label and a `due` flag. That is enough for a screenshot and useless the moment a card is reviewed, because grading produces a new `SchedulingState` and two hand-maintained flags beside it would immediately contradict it. `DemoCard` now extends core's `Card`, carrying an authored `CardInteraction` and a real `SchedulingState`; `status` and `due` are derived from that state in `demoScheduling.ts`. A card stops being due because its due date moved, not because something set a flag. Every id, deck assignment, prompt and tag from M-DEMO-1 is unchanged, so the device-verified numbers did not move: 18 cards, 12 due, Algorithms 6/5, Compilers 6/4, Modern C++ 6/3.

**D379. Demo scheduling is seeded as an offset from the workspace's own `now`, and that instant travels with the workspace.** An absolute seed would have to be anchored to `DEMO_EPOCH`, a fixed date in the past, which makes every card permanently overdue against the real clock and makes "due in three days" inexpressible. `createDemoWorkspace(now)` resolves each authored day offset against one instant, so the dataset stays deterministic per run - the point of a demo - while remaining correct against `Date.now()` during grading. That instant is exposed on the demo context and advances only when the workspace does, because due-ness is a comparison against an instant and Today's count, Library's per-deck counts and the queue must not disagree by however many milliseconds passed between their renders.

**D380. Native code blocks are literal monospace text, and the per-token colouring is a recorded loss rather than a silent one.** The preview screens coloured code from hand-authored `tone` values on each fixture token. No shared tokenizer exists that could produce those from a `code` node, master plan D5a names a WebView carrying CodeMirror an escape hatch rather than a default, and inventing a native syntax highlighter is not this milestone's work. Fenced blocks therefore render as literal monospace lines with line numbers - which is also the only rendering that is guaranteed to be faithful to arbitrary card content. Re-adding colour means a shared tokenizer in core that both platforms consume, not a native-only one.

**D381. The native session shell mirrors web's semantically and owns no per-type logic, and the phase machine stays platform-side.** `ReviewSessionScreen.tsx` owns the phase machine, the response, the timing, the FSRS interval preview and the objective-result-to-suggested-rating mapping; which View renders, when a response is submittable and what it scores all come from the shared `InteractionBehavior` the caller binds. `architecture.md` already records that `ReviewPhase` and the interaction View props are platform-specific by decision, so the native machine is a second one on purpose - it has four phases rather than five, because web's `persistFailed` exists for a `Repository` write that can reject and a demo write cannot. Per-card state resets by remounting on `card.id`, the same rule web follows.

**D382. There is one native interaction registry, `Partial` by design, and its binding is asserted by reference.** `interactions/registry.ts` is `{ ...<type>Behavior, View }` per type, mirroring web's `WebInteractionDefinition`. `registry.test.ts` compares `isResponseReady`, `autoGrade` and `widthFor` by function identity rather than by behaviour, because a native View that re-implemented one of them would look perfectly reasonable in review and would silently teach the learner something different from what web teaches. The registry stays `Partial` so a seventh interaction type added to core without a native View fails loudly at runtime instead of rendering nothing - the same deliberate choice web made, and not to be "improved" into a compile-time guarantee.

**D383. Demo review state is written in memory, and Undo restores a recorded value rather than running the scheduler backwards.** `applyDemoReview` takes the immutable `{ after, log }` that `reviewService.submit` returned and writes exactly that: the new `SchedulingState` onto the card, the canonical `ReviewLog` onto the workspace. It is idempotent by log id, so a double press on a rating cannot record two reviews of one card. `undoDemoReview` restores the pre-grade state that was recorded when the grade was taken, because FSRS is not invertible and re-deriving `before` from `after` would be a second, wrong implementation of scheduling on the platform least allowed to have one. Nothing is persisted: no `Repository`, no Supabase, no Dexie, no SQLite, no AsyncStorage, and the session resets on a full app restart. That is D369 applied to review state, and it is deliberately not recorded as debt.

**D384. The demo queue is a per-mount snapshot with a deterministic tie-break, and it is not a reimplementation of `useSessionQueue`.** Web's version exists to turn a repository query plus a deck subtree into a stable snapshot, and neither exists here. What is shared is the semantics it encodes: due cards only, most overdue first, snapshotted once so grading a card cannot reshuffle the session under the learner. The tie-break on `id` is what makes a demo reproducible - the seeded due dates land on whole and half days, so without a deterministic second key two runs of the same demo would show the same cards in a different order and a recorded walkthrough could not be repeated.

**D385. Objective correctness recommends a rating and never chooses one.** Itera's locked rule is unchanged and is now enforced on a second platform: an auto-graded result maps to a suggestion using web's exact rule - correct suggests Good, partial credit suggests Hard as a quieter hint than a flat Again, and nothing objectively right suggests Again - and that suggestion is a visible marker only. No rating is preselected, no other rating is disabled, and the session never advances on objective correctness alone. `ReviewLog.autoGraded` records whether the learner's choice matched the recommendation, computed as web computes it. Rating buttons now show the real next-due interval per rating from `reviewService.previewNextStates` over that card's own state, replacing four fixture strings that were identical on all six screens and asserted a scheduling outcome the app was not producing.

**D386. Walkthrough produces exactly one rating and one ReviewLog for the whole card.** The response threaded through the session is core's own `WalkthroughState`, so there is no second notion of where the learner is; per-step results come from `gradeWalkthroughStep`, readiness from `walkthroughBehavior.isResponseReady`, and the card-level outcome from `walkthroughBehavior.autoGrade`. The first submitted answer for a step is the one that counts, so stepping back through an answered walkthrough is inspection rather than a second attempt. A genuine `null` aggregate - an all-Recall walkthrough, where nothing was objectively checked - survives as `null` and is never coerced into "incorrect", because suggesting Again for content that was never checked would be a lie about the learner's answer.

**D387. The Review tab gets a small entry surface, and the tab bar hides only for the session.** `IteraTabBar` returned `null` for the whole `review` tab, which was correct while the tab opened a card directly and made the tab a trapdoor once it should not. The bar now hides only when the focused nested route is `session`. The tab lands on `ReviewStartScreen.tsx`, which is deliberately the smallest thing that works: a heading, the due count, the contributing deck names, one primary Start action, and an honest caught-up state. It is a functional entry surface, not a second design language - it reuses Today's tokens, card surface, header and primary action, and carries no charts, streak tiles, filters, session customisation or illustrations. Due cards are never silently repopulated to keep the demo interesting.

**D388. Two Ordering defects and one submission defect were fixed, because real content found them.** The drag step was a hard-coded 82px, true of the five short fixture items and wrong for any wrapping real item, so every drop landed on the wrong index; rows are now measured through `onLayout`. Reordering was reachable only through an `accessibilityAction` on an `adjustable` role, where master plan D7 asks for always-visible up and down controls precisely because native has no equivalent of web's Space-arrows-Space; they are now rendered. The third surfaced only once readiness was real: a learner who agreed with the presented order had recorded no response, so `orderingBehavior.isResponseReady` said no and Submit could never be pressed. Agreeing is an answer, and the presented order is now seeded on mount exactly as Write Code seeds its starter code.

**D389. The native content-security regression is complete, and the standing TODO is closed.** Step 1.6 audited the web content boundary and recorded that a future native renderer would need its own pass, since inheriting a safe node tree is not the same as rendering it safely; that item was explicitly not actionable until a renderer existed. One now does, and it has had the pass: core's own `attackPayloads.ts` fixture is driven through the native renderer at block level, inline level and inside fenced blocks, and every string that must survive literally does. React Native not executing DOM HTML was treated as a reason to enumerate the sinks that do exist rather than as a reason to skip the audit. `Linking` and `WebView` are absent from the entire component tree and a source scan fails the build if either appears; no link support was introduced, and the node union has no `url` node to hand a renderer one. `WalkthroughInteraction.image` - the one URL sink in the card model - is rendered only through `SafeCardImage.tsx`, which calls core's shared `isSafeImageSource` rather than re-implementing the policy, and a scan fails the build if any other module builds an `Image` source from data. No styles, module names or requests are derived from card text. **No native content-security defect was found.**

**D390. Demo reset is a development affordance on Profile, and deliberately not on the caught-up screen.** Reviewing demo cards changes their scheduling in memory, so a second demo run starts from wherever the first ended. `resetDemoWorkspace()` - which already existed and already rebuilds the whole workspace, restoring card scheduling, clearing ReviewLogs and restoring notification state - is now reachable from a `__DEV__`-only row beside the existing diagnostics row, and still refuses at runtime outside `__DEV__`. It was not put on the caught-up Review screen, where it is most convenient during a recording, because that would present a developer and marketing convenience as a learner feature: a demo whose empty state offers to refill itself is not showing anyone how the product behaves.

**D391. No cloud work was performed, and cloud Review persistence stays deferred.** No Supabase project, no schema, no OTP, no RLS check, no `Repository` write, and M1A was not modified. The demo session has no persist-failure state because a synchronous in-memory write cannot fail and fabricating retry UX during normal demo grading would be dishonest; the awaited `onGraded` seam on the session shell is where a cloud session adds web's `persistFailed` phase and its identical-result retry, and it is awaited today for exactly that reason. `TODO.md`'s live-Supabase, cloud-review-transaction, offline-sync, Notifications and Vercel items are all preserved unchanged. Device verification of the demo session is pending the owner and is not claimed here.

---

## 2026-08-25 - mobile Demo history becomes the single input to Today and Progress

**Context.** M-DEMO-2 (D377-D391) already advanced canonical SchedulingState and appended canonical ReviewLogs, but only Due-based surfaces reacted. Today streak/Retention/session estimate and most of Progress still came from authored display constants, so the app could complete a real local session and then show a dashboard that denied it happened. M-DEMO-3 closes only that seam and stops before cloud or authoring work.

**D392. The demo authors ReviewLogs, never statistics.** `demoReviewHistory.ts` contains 25 readable review inputs over existing cards: relative local-day offsets, time of day, rating, duration and valid before/after state. `createDemoWorkspace(now)` materializes them through core's calendar helpers, so the pattern stays current and crosses DST safely instead of becoming a 2026 screenshot fossil. Streak, mature Retention, Learned, Reviews, activity, retention buckets, deck performance, milestones and duration are all derived. The old constants and per-deck retention/last-studied fields were deleted, so there is no second value available for a screen to accidentally prefer.

**D393. Mobile selectors compose the same core statistics as web and own formatting only.** Today calls `computeStreak`, `computeRetention`, `estimateSessionMinutes`, `computeDeckMetrics` and `buildContinueLearning`; Progress calls `computeKpis`, `computeHeatmap`, `computeRetentionSeries`, `computeDeckPerformance` and `deriveMilestones`. No formula moved and no web/core file changed. The screen boundary still receives native view models, but those contain formatted shared outputs rather than mobile-calculated learning semantics.

**D394. The current mobile Progress hierarchy keeps one real 30D range.** The 30D control already reads as the selected range and 3M/1Y are explicit unavailable controls. M-DEMO-3 makes that one range truthful - label, Reviews, mature Retention, 30 activity cells and one-day retention buckets share `buildRange('30d')` - and does not turn unavailable controls into a larger feature. The pure selector accepts another core preset for wiring tests, but the product surface exposes only the owner-approved 30D hierarchy.

**D395. Missing retention buckets remain missing on native.** The prior graph joined every authored number and assumed retention was at least 50%, so it could neither show a gap/singleton nor safely plot lower data. It now renders null-aware adjacent runs only, isolated observations as points and a data-derived vertical domain. This is rendering geometry, not a retention calculation; bucket values and gaps still come exclusively from core. Today pace remains omitted by the existing mobile hierarchy decision.

**D396. Undo and reset are data operations, so every derived surface reverses naturally.** Undo first checks that its log still exists, then restores the recorded pre-grade SchedulingState and removes that exact log. A duplicate call is a no-op rather than a second restoration. Reset rebuilds from the workspace's original anchor, restoring seeded history, scheduling and notifications byte for byte; it no longer clears the seed history M-DEMO-3 requires. There is no UI compensation for Today, Progress or Library and no per-screen refresh call.

**D397. This remains demo/local, in memory, and is not promoted to production parity.** `platform-parity.md` marks these capabilities `implemented (demo/local)`: shared semantics are functional on the phone, persistence is not. No Repository was registered, no Supabase project/schema/OTP/RLS work occurred, and no SQLite, AsyncStorage, sync, Library CRUD, authoring or notification backend was added. The live-cloud, transaction, offline-sync, Notifications and deployment TODO items remain unchanged. Physical-device verification is pending the owner and is not claimed.

## 2026-08-25 - M-DEMO-3 corrections after independent verification

**Context.** An independent read-only verification pass over M-DEMO-3 confirmed the milestone: every mobile learning metric routes through a canonical `@itera/core` function, the seed history uses the shared calendar helpers rather than 86,400,000 ms stepping, and grade/Undo/reset are data operations with no UI-side compensation. It also found four issues worth fixing before handover and five minor ones worth carrying. D392-D397 stand; these four entries record what changed.

**D398. Expo tooling belongs to `apps/mobile`, and the root keeps no `.expo` ignore rule.** M-DEMO-3 had rewritten the repository-root `tsconfig.json` to `extends: "expo/tsconfig.base"` and left a generated root `.expo/`, which is what Expo writes when it is run from the repository root. Both are reverted: the root is the four-project workspace solution again, unchanged since Phase 2. The root deliberately does **not** gain a `.expo` ignore rule - `apps/mobile/.gitignore` already covers the legitimate one, and ignoring a stray root directory would hide the next occurrence rather than prevent it. Nothing depended on the extended config (`"files": []` means the root typechecks nothing itself), so this is boundary hygiene rather than a bug fix, but the root inheriting `noEmit`, `jsx: react-native` and `customConditions: ["react-native"]` from a mobile-only package is exactly the coupling `architecture.md` says the root does not have.

**D399. The demo authors review inputs; the scheduler produces every outcome.** D392 said the demo authors ReviewLogs and never statistics, and that was right about statistics and wrong about scheduling: each seed still carried its own `stateBefore`/`state`, and the cards carried hand-authored mature/learning `SchedulingState` beside them. The two records disagreed for six of the twelve reviewed cards - `fixture-card-smart-pointer-code` claimed `learning` while its own last log said it had graduated to `review` - and three cards carried a `lastReview` contradicting their latest log. `demoReviewHistory.ts` now authors only what a learner does (card, local day, time, rating, duration) and replays it through core’s `reviewState` + `buildReviewLog`; the card takes the state the replay leaves it in, and only the log id is substituted, because `newId()` is random and the fixture must be reproducible. `demoCardContent.ts` can no longer express a mature or learning card at all - the `matureDue`/`matureLater`/`learning` helpers are deleted and `fresh` is the only one left - so the contradiction is unrepresentable rather than merely corrected. A fixture invariant test guards state, `lastReview`, due, stability, difficulty, reps, lapses, log-to-card references and per-card log chaining.

**D400. Today’s Retention is the trailing 30 calendar days, matching web.** Today averaged all of history while Progress averaged thirty days. The definition was never in question - both call core’s `computeRetention`, mature attempts only, Hard or better - but the window differed from web’s `TodayPage`, and the two mobile surfaces would have diverged as soon as demo history outgrew thirty days. `demoTodayRetention()` applies `buildRange('30d')` and is the one definition Today and the seeded retention notification share. The cross-surface test that previously passed by coincidence is now proved against mature failures 40 and 45 days old whose ratings would change the all-time figure.

**D401. Truthful derived numbers were accepted over the previous demo mix.** Replaying the history causally moved the numbers, because the old ones were the product of an inconsistent fixture: Due is 9 rather than 12, Reviews 56 rather than 25, Learned 12 of 18, mature Retention 84%, current streak 4 (which the previous `CURRENT_STATE.md` §26 wrongly recorded as three). Recent milestones show a 3-day streak plus the 70%/80%/90% retention thresholds, three sharing a date because the first replayed day was perfect. That is core’s `deriveMilestones` being honest about this history, and the fixture was deliberately not reshaped to spread them - prettifying a derived surface by editing its inputs is the failure mode this milestone existed to remove. Five minor findings are carried unchanged and none affects behaviour: the retention chart re-deriving core’s run/isolated rule (core’s `buildRetentionGeometry` is hard-coded to web pixel dimensions), the single-bucket chart NaN edge (unreachable through `buildRange`), seeded `dueAfter` (now a real scheduler output, so this resolved itself), reset rewinding `now`, and two coverage gaps (a DST-boundary seed test, and the "Not enough data" deck-retention branch).

---

## 2026-08-25 - mobile Deck/Card browsing becomes a learner flow (M-DEMO-4)

**Context.** After M-DEMO-3 the mobile demo could review, and every number on it was derived from canonical Cards and ReviewLogs. Two learner-facing dead ends remained on the Deck screen, and they were the ones that still made the app read as a browse-only prototype: the card rows were disabled Pressables carrying a decorative kebab, and Study Now - the screen's one orange primary action - was disabled. M-DEMO-4 closes exactly those two, and stops before any CRUD, authoring or cloud work.

**D402. Deck scope means the deck and its subtree, resolved by core's `subtreeIds`, on both platforms.** Web's `?deck=` has always meant a deck plus every descendant, and the mobile queue meant "exactly this deck" through an equality check of its own. That is a second definition of what studying a deck is, and it would have been a silent divergence rather than a visible one, because demo decks are flat and the two answers agree today. `createDemoQueue` now builds its scope with core's own helper, so the meaning is shared and stops being "the deck itself" for the right reason if demo decks ever nest. The helper stays pure: an id naming no deck resolves to itself and therefore covers nothing, and refusing the navigation is the route's job.

**D403. Card study is a mode on the one session shell, not a second card presentation.** The master plan already specified web's `/cards/:id/study` as "the same review screen, hideRating, fresh baseline", and the alternative - a native card screen that rendered the interaction View, the tip and the explanation itself - would have been a second copy of the phase machine and the reveal/submit rule, on the platform least allowed to have one. `ReviewSessionScreen` now takes a discriminated `mode: 'review' | 'study'` union rather than a set of optional props, so a study surface cannot be handed a grade handler by accident and a session cannot forget one. Everything that decides what the learner sees - `interactive`, `isResponseReady`, `autoGrade`, `widthFor` - is used unchanged, which is what makes the preview faithful rather than merely similar. All six interaction types therefore work in study for the same reason they work in a session, through the one registry, with no per-type route.

**D404. Card rows open cards by their canonical id, and the row kebab was removed rather than disabled.** The rows resolve through `findDemoCard()`, so an unknown id gets an honest not-found state instead of some other card - the same rule the deck and collection routes already follow (D370, D374). The decorative kebab glyph at the right of each row was deleted, not left `disabled`: inside a dead row it was merely inert, but inside a live row it reads as an overflow menu and a tap on it opens the card, which is a worse lie than the one it replaced. Nothing took its place, because card management does not exist on this platform yet. The deck-level actions and Add card controls keep their existing visibly-disabled, honestly-labelled state, and the Insights tab stays visibly unavailable - only its copy was corrected, because it claimed insights were waiting on mobile data that M-DEMO-3 had already composed.

**D405. Study records nothing, by construction rather than by suppression.** There is no `schedulingBefore`, no `onGraded`, no rating control, no interval preview and no `reviewService.submit` in study mode - the props that would carry a write do not exist, so no `ReviewLog` is appended, no `SchedulingState` moves and no due date changes. The notice is shown from the first frame rather than after an answer, because a learner decides how honestly to answer based on whether it counts, and saying so afterwards would be saying it too late. The regression is asserted against the live demo workspace *after* each of the six card types has actually been answered, by reference identity on the logs array and on the card's scheduling: "no write happened" only means something once the code path that would have written has run.

**D406. A deck with nothing due says so on the deck, and no due date was moved to avoid it.** Study Now does not launch a session that immediately declares itself over; the Deck screen states the caught-up condition in the Study Now slot instead, distinguishing a deck that is caught up from a deck that has no cards at all. Five demo decks are honestly empty and demonstrate this immediately, and the three populated decks reach it live once a scoped session is finished - which is the more useful demonstration, and the reason the fixture was left alone. A `deckId` that names no deck is refused at the route with a not-found state rather than presented as a caught-up session for a deck that does not exist, because an empty queue and an unknown scope are different facts and must not share a screen.

**D407. Session origin is the navigation stack, not a parameter.** Today, the Review tab and the deck all *push* the one session route, and both exits - the header exit and the completion screen's Done - go back. A deck-scoped session therefore ends on its originating deck with no origin metadata, no second completion component and no per-entry-point exit rule. The card preview follows the same logic one level up: it sits at the `(app)` stack level rather than inside the Library tab, so the deck stays mounted underneath and returning to it preserves the search text and status filter the learner left, without any state being lifted or restored.

**D408. No authoring and no cloud work occurred.** New/Edit/Delete deck, New/Edit/Delete/Duplicate/Move/Suspend card and import/export are all still unavailable and still honestly labelled; no Supabase project, schema, OTP, RLS or `Repository` write exists, and M1A is untouched. Demo state remains in memory and resets on a full app restart, which is D369 unchanged. `TODO.md` was deliberately not given entries for card authoring, persistence or cloud: those are planned or deferred product work with recorded decisions, and filing them as generic TODOs would turn a decision into a chore. Physical-device verification is pending the owner and is not claimed here.

---

## 2026-08-25 - the mobile demo is prepared for market validation (M-DEMO-5)

**Context.** After M-DEMO-4 the mobile app worked: Today, Library, Deck, card study, global and deck-scoped Review across all six interactions, real shared grading and FSRS, reactive statistics, completion and Undo. What remained was not another subsystem but the scaffolding from the mockup phase - eleven controls that existed because a reference image had a button in that position, a placeholder Insights tab, an inert range group, a Profile whose seven rows all led to "not available yet", three notifications with no destination, and learner-visible strings carrying implementation vocabulary. The owner's goal is now to record the app and put it in front of prospective users, so this milestone is a breadth pass over already-designed surfaces with one rule and no new product scope. The four judgement calls it needed were taken by the owner before implementation and are recorded below as D410, D411, D413 and D415.

**D409. On the market-validation build, an unavailable control is removed, not disabled.** The previous milestones deliberately kept out-of-scope controls visible and honestly labelled, which was right while the app was a prototype being reviewed by its author: a greyed New Deck said "this is known, it is not built yet". In front of a prospective user the same control says something else - that the product is unfinished - and eleven of them across four screens said it at once. Every one of them was therefore removed rather than disabled: New Deck, Import and the all-collections button on All Decks; the deck-row kebab; New Deck, Collection settings and the overflow on Collection; deck actions and Add card on Deck; and the 3M/1Y range on Progress. This is not a reversal of the honesty rule but its consequence. Nothing here was *pending work* whose absence needed explaining - each names a capability this platform has deliberately chosen not to have yet (D416) - and a control is only worth showing disabled when its absence would otherwise read as a defect. The rule the build now satisfies is that every visible affordance is functional, intentionally and clearly unavailable, or absent; there is no state where a control looks enabled and does nothing. The one control that was worse than unavailable is gone with the panel that held it: Import / Export's Merge / Replace radio group responded to every press and changed its selection while the two buttons above it could not run.

**D410. Demo mode does not advertise cloud settings infrastructure; cloud mode keeps it unchanged.** Profile's seven settings rows are a cloud-mode surface: six can only say "not available yet" and the seventh offers a backup this platform cannot perform. In demo mode the screen is now the header, the workspace identity card and the `__DEV__` rows, and nothing else. The alternative - keeping six honest placeholder panels - was rejected because a screen whose every row leads to "not built" is exactly the impression this milestone exists to remove, and because none of those six is work in progress. **The production architecture was not destroyed to achieve it:** the rows, the six notices, the backup panel, the real session email and the live Sign out all still render under `EXPO_PUBLIC_ITERA_MODE=cloud`, and a test asserts both directions so the gate cannot be dropped in either. The Notifications inbox's settings shortcut is hidden in demo mode for the same reason - it would open a Profile with nothing to show.

**D411. Progress reports one real window, and the range group was removed rather than wired.** `demoProgressViewModel` already takes a `DateRangePreset`, so wiring the KPIs to 3M and 1Y would have been trivial - and that is exactly why the decision needed making rather than assuming. The rest is not trivial: the heat map draws one 24px cell per day, so a year is roughly thirty-six rows; the retention series is asked for one bucket per day, so a year is 365 buckets; and the seeded history is 28 days long, so both would have rendered nearly empty. Implementing them honestly is a Progress layout change, which is a redesign this milestone is not allowed to make, and implementing them dishonestly is the defect it exists to remove. The buttons are gone and the date pill - which already names the window every figure is computed over - is the whole control area. The `preset` parameter stays as the seam, unused by product code, so a future range is a wiring change and not a rewrite.

**D412. A notification's destination is data on the notification, not an inference from its kind.** Three of the seven rows previously only marked themselves read, which is a supported shape but makes the first surface a prospective user taps a column of dead ends. Every row now carries an explicit `deck | collection | review | progress` destination, authored per notification: two rows can share a kind and belong in different places, so branching on `kind` would have been a second, weaker source of truth beside the one the fixture already has. Today's session opens the Review entry, the streak and retention rows open Progress, the new-cards row opens its collection, and the three deck rows open their decks; the hint names the surface each will open. The field stays **optional** and the mark-read-only copy is retained, because the case it covers - a notification the app cannot answer - is the one this rule exists to keep honest, not a case to delete.

**D413. Today's one action changes destination when nothing is due; it does not change composition.** The hero read *Start your next session* at zero due and opened the session route, which answered with a completion screen - not wrong, but the designed caught-up surface exists elsewhere and no session can create due work. The same control in the same slot now reads *Browse your library* and opens Library, which is the call web's `SuggestedSessionHero` already makes. The hero's metric grid, divider, stacked-card geometry, animation and spacing are untouched: the owner-approved composition is not something this milestone may change, and one control's label and destination is not that composition. The Deck screen's back control was corrected on the same principle: it read *Back to {collection}* while calling `router.back()`, which returns to whichever of five entry points pushed the deck, and four of them are not that collection. It says **Back**, and the collection name moved into the identity block as a caption - context rather than a promise about navigation, so the information survives the correction.

**D414. Fixture copy is derived from the entities, and dates are read on the learner's timeline.** The plausibility pass was read-only by intent and found one real contradiction: *"6 new cards were added to Interview Core"* counted every card in the collection and called all of them new, while those cards were authored between 8 and 88 days before the anchor and none was added on the day the row claimed. The count and the date now come from the cards actually added on the most recent addition day, so the row can only describe something that happened - it reads "1 new card was added". Correcting it exposed the second half: entity dates are authored against the fixed `DEMO_EPOCH` while everything the learner *did* is replayed relative to the current local day, so an absolute label would have shown a demo recorded three months from now an inbox dated last August. `fixtureDateLabel()` reads an authored instant as the same age measured back from the workspace's own anchor and formats it through core's one event formatter, and a regression asserts the import row still says "Yesterday" 400 days later. **No derived metric was touched.** The five empty demo decks were left empty (D406), and no cards were generated to make the Library look fuller.

**D415. The demo disclosure belongs on Profile, not on the end of every session.** The completion screen printed *"Demo mode. This session is held in memory and resets when the app restarts."* after every session - implementation vocabulary on the most-recorded surface in the app. It is removed, and the disclosure is not weakened: Profile's identity card states plainly that this is a **Demo workspace**, that the data is *deterministic demo data, not a synced account*, and that *nothing here is saved between app launches*. The same sweep removed "the demo workspace" from four not-found details and the Deck empty state, "the temporary Review destination" from the Today hint, and the false line "Product screens still use preview data" from cloud-mode Profile. Every `__DEV__` string keeps its explicit development language, and the dead `PlaceholderScreen.tsx` - "intentionally awaiting its own design review", with zero importers - was deleted. The distinction being held is between *disclosing a limit* and *narrating the implementation*: the first stays, prominently and in one place; the second does not belong on a product surface.

**D416. Mobile authoring and cloud are deferred product scope, and are not technical debt.** Itera's early product story may legitimately be **desktop authoring plus convenient mobile review**, so mobile card and deck authoring is not required for the current objective and was not started: no New Deck, Add Card, Edit, Delete, Duplicate, Move, Matching editor or Walkthrough editor. Cloud/Supabase remains deferred until after market validation, and demo persistence with it - M1A is untouched, no Supabase project, schema, OTP, RLS or `Repository` write exists, and demo state still resets on a full app restart (D369). None of the three was filed in `TODO.md`, and `TODO.md` was not edited: a decision with a recorded reason is not a chore, and turning it into one loses the reason. The corresponding `platform-parity.md` rows were re-stated from "deferred - master plan Phase N" to the product-scope decision they now actually are.

**D417. The `__DEV__` reset was verified rather than changed, including its rewind of `now`.** `resetDemoWorkspace()` rebuilds from `workspace.startedAt` and sets `now` back to that anchor - carried as a minor finding out of M-DEMO-3, and on inspection it is the property repeated recordings need: every reset within one app run reproduces byte-identical state rather than drifting with the wall clock. The regression drives a whole deck-scoped session to completion, marks the inbox read, asserts that scheduling, history, notifications and the derived screens all moved, then resets and asserts the workspace deep-equals a fresh `createDemoWorkspace(startedAt)` together with the Today, Progress, Library and Deck view models built from it. It stays `__DEV__`-only, refused again inside the helper, and is never offered as a learner control - on the caught-up Review screen it would read as one.

**D418. Third-party test noise was reported, not silenced.** Six `act(...)` warnings were Itera's own: test code calling provider setters imperatively outside `act`. Those are fixed. The remaining 108 have a single external cause - `@expo/vector-icons` loads its font asynchronously and calls `setState` after the synchronous render a test awaited - and production code was not reshaped to quiet them. Physical-device verification of this milestone is pending the owner and is not claimed.
